# Key Design Decisions — Archive

Moved wholesale from `memory.md`'s "Key Design Decisions" section on 2026-07-19. These entries are preserved exactly as originally written — none have been edited or corrected, even where later canon has since moved past them. They're a record of how the system's design evolved, not a current-state reference. For what's actually true now, check the canon file each entry names, or `memory.md`'s live "Active Reasoning" section for anything that's still active.

**The Unheld is a hard edge; sailing lives on rivers**
Nothing sails or fishes the Unheld Ocean — it is the true edge of reality, not a soft boundary. No islands exist in it. The only exceptions: Glasslight light-cartographers map its edge (never the water), and the People of Promise touch it deliberately (ritual bathing = transgression as worship). Ghost stories preserve the memory of ships that once went past the coast; Corvel is living proof. The continent is laced with interconnected rivers and lakes draining toward the Unheld — Vulture's Nest is the heart of the river web. Tides are an Unheld phenomenon that breathes up the rivers (when the grey water pulls back, rivers drop inland — this keeps the Tide Pull and the Glasslight tide framework working). **The coastline is a hard threshold: unheldness does not cross it, by flowing or by being carried — inland water is mundane, only the tidal motion travels upstream.** This is why Promise bathing must happen at the coast, and why Corvel's vials are ordinary water (what they carry is Pneum's diseases, not unheldness — see `experimental/pneum.md`). The Island in a Ship is unchanged: the Oracle's island is now the only location in the setting not on the continent. Former Unheld islands (Canille, Pneum) relocated to lakes. Eclipsera's Temple of the Sea renamed Temple of the Rivers.

**Stat loss reduces the stat's derived value** (`rules/card-glossary.md`, Stat Loss)
General rule, not a keyword: a card that reduces a stat for a combat (Sunder→Mind, Wither→Body, Erode→Soul) lowers whatever that stat governs. Body loss → −3 max HP per point (Body's derived value; clamps current HP, can Collapse). Mind loss → hand size. Soul loss → initiative. Only Body touches HP. Reverts at combat end. Every current/future stat-draining card inherits this. Sim: `combatsimulations/engine.py` Combatant.erode (Body-only HP).

**Soul = movement speed**
Soul governs initiative AND movement speed. High Soul characters move faster and act first. This is why flee/chase uses Soul, not Body. Body is impact, not velocity. Important for future card and mechanic design — don't use Body for movement checks. Flee is now formalized: 2d10 + Soul vs DC 10 + highest enemy Soul (ambush formula from the other side), GM-adjusted for terrain/obstacles/position/enemy intent — see `rules/combat.md`, Fleeing Combat. Enemies don't roll to flee; enemy disengagement is a GM behavior call.

**Abstract movement**
No distances in play. "In reach / close / far" is the language. Combat uses abstract positions (Frontline/Backline). When a character moves away from the combat area, that ends the combat — flee function. Enemies can re-engage. Non-combat chasing uses Soul checks. Avoid specific distances in any bestiary or quest content.

**Quick reference is `rules/core-rules.md`**
Canonical rules quick reference. Covers stats, DC table, combat actions, attack resolution, card anatomy, positioning, collapse, resting, cover, stealth/ambush, chase.

**Initiative is a continuous wheel**
Turn order is a closed loop, not a list that resets each cycle — there are no rounds (see `rules/combat.md`). Seat count equals combatant count; shifts wrap at the ends. Initiative Shift X (`rules/card-glossary.md`) is the only way card effects move someone in the order — it guarantees a positive shift never delays a target's next turn and a negative shift never accelerates it, and large shifts (|X| ≥ seat count) decompose into full revolutions plus a remainder. Slipstream and Synchrony (`cards/blue-mind.md`, `cards/green-soul.md`) are the first cards to treat wheel position itself as a build-around.

**Seats & Echoes** (`mythology/seats.md`, `mythology/echoes.md`)
Seats are fixed metaphysical positions — structural features of reality. Echoes are beings whose identity has aligned with a Seat's domain (Resonance → Alignment → Incarnation/Archon). Failed Seat-takers are the most powerful and least stable Echoes.

**The Pendragon Attempt** (`world/the-regency.md`)
The Regency council was originally an adventuring party. They attempted to claim the Seat of Love/Binding together. Failed. Aurora held. The five survivors (Oswald, Cedric, Elara, Archibald, Eveline) became the original council members — each losing internal contradiction, gaining an animal mask as scar tissue. Isabella and Percival were recruited after, don't have full knowledge of the foundation. Oswald's long-term plan: claim the Seat fully with Aurora suppressed. The letter at Table #9 of the Gilded Tusk is about this.

**Aurora** (`mythology/seats.md`, `places/eclipsera-city.md`)
Bearer of the Seat of Love/Binding. Bound beneath Eclipsera. The city's unity runs on her. The chains are not permanent containment — they are preparation for Oswald's claim.

**Animal titles = scar tissue**
The council's animal symbols (Lion, Panther, Bear, Fox, Owl, Pig, Elephant) are not heraldic. They're the shape the Seat left on each person when the attempt failed.

**Deck-building conventions** (canonical home: `rules/cards.md`, Deck Building)
Player decks: color counts match stats (Mind 4/Body 2/Soul 3 → 4B/2R/3G) — heuristic, not law. Enemy decks: 3 themed signature cards + 4–7 core cards (7–10 total), leaning toward the creature's stat spread. Enemies draw to hand size like players. Opening hands: everyone draws to hand size when initiative is rolled (added to `rules/combat.md`). Worked combat: `rules/combat-example.md` — includes rulings on 4 edge cases, all resolved by Drew. Tie-counts-as-successful-defense is ruled per-card, not universally: WITNESS says so on the card; new cards referencing "successful defense" must carry their own clarifying line until/unless the convention is promoted to a universal rule.

**Card system: signature sets**
Creature combat decks are filled from core cards. Signature cards are Oracle rewards. Drew determines how many land in a given set after reviewing the full 9. The standard loop: draft 9, red team all 9, fix/cut/replace until all pass, present all 9, Drew decides placement.

**HP formula: (2 × Body) + 9** (changed from 3×Body+6 — Drew, via sim experiment)
Flatter curve to decouple HP from Body and reduce Body's damage+HP double-dip. Crossover at Body 3 (both = 15); trims high Body (B4: 18→17, B5: 19, B8: 25) and lifts low Body (B1: 11, B2: 13). Bites harder at high stats, which is where powerful creatures live. Player impact: Frost stays 15, Steele 18→17. **Existing creature stat blocks keep their authored HP** (gm-guide allows GM-set HP); new content + players use the new formula. Sim uses it (`hp_per_body`). NOTE: the sim showed HP is NOT the main lever for Steele's dominance (even flat HP left Steele at 59% vs Frost) — the real driver is red damage + color spread.

**Damage-distribution rebalance (Drew, applied):** Pain is Fuel & Push d6→d4; Stillness & Excavate d2→d4; Attune d2→d4. Narrowed the color-EV gap (RED 2.62→2.53, BLUE 2.15→2.21, GREEN 2.44→2.47) — good for draft diversity / makes blue-green picks more competitive. BUT the sim showed single-card dice tweaks DON'T move deck-level balance: Steele barely budged (66% vs Frost). Reason: damage = stat + die, and a Body-4 stat (+4 flat on every red card) dominates a one-step die change (−1 avg). **The real Steele lever is the stat-to-damage ratio, not card dice** — e.g. capping how much stat adds, or a smaller stat mod + bigger dice. Card dice are the right tool for per-card attractiveness (Oracle draft balance), the wrong tool for reining in a high-stat deck.

**GAME LENGTH is the master balance variable (big finding).** Tested shifting ALL damage dice up/down one size (sim only, not applied): dice DOWN → Steele 66%→56% vs Frost (games longer, 8.6 turns); dice UP → Steele 66%→70% (games faster, 7.0 turns). Monotonic. Bigger dice = faster games = raw-stat power converts to wins more directly (fewer decisive hits, less room for reads/card-play). Smaller dice = longer games = flat stat advantage grinds through more RPS variance and dilutes. This unifies all balance findings: HP-flatten helped a little (slightly longer games), single-card dice nerfs did nothing (too small to change length), a blanket dice-DOWN would help a lot (shortens nothing, lengthens everything). The RPS mind-game, reads, and card advantage all NEED turns to exist; speed kills them and rewards whoever hits hardest. So to dilute stat-max: lengthen games (smaller dice / more HP / cheaper defense), never shorten them. Drew's "shift dice UP" suggestion does the opposite — DON'T. No canon change made yet; direction TBD.

**Full lever sweep (all sim-tested, only HP-flatten applied):** for reining in Steele's ~66% dominance —
- HP decouple 2×Body+9: mild help (~2pts). APPLIED/canon.
- Single-card dice nerfs (5 cards): ~0 effect (too small to change game length).
- Dice UP one size: WORSE (66→70%, faster games).
- Dice DOWN one size: HELPS most (66→56%, longer games) but blunt/drags/feel cost.
- Remove dice entirely (flat damage): slightly WORSE (66→69%) — removes the underdog's variance, and would gut the "d-scale damage dice" core identity. DON'T.
- Stat cap (die + min(stat,N)): ruled out by Drew.
CONCLUSION: the ONLY reliable dilution of stat-max is LENGTHENING games, and within that VARIANCE HELPS THE UNDERDOG (so flat damage / faster games hurt). Best remaining lever = DEFENSE ECONOMY (make blocking more available/effective → longer games, no single hit decisive), which lengthens without shrinking dice or gutting identity. BUT also worth questioning whether it's a problem at all: stat-maxing (Volk) is self-defeating (loses to everyone); Steele's ~66% is modest and comes from GOOD deckbuilding (high primary + viable secondary), which is arguably healthy.

**Minmax trifecta added to sim (Latin square, each stat takes 4/3/2 once):** Steele Body4/Mind3/Soul2 (4R/3B/2G, canonical), Sage Mind4/Soul3/Body2 (4B/3G/2R), Adept Soul4/Body3/Mind2 (4G/3R/2B). Round-robin under tactician OVERTURNED the "blue is weakest" premise. Color-archetype ranking: **Blue(Sage) 72% avg > Red(Steele) 62% > neutral(Frost) 41% > Green(Adept) 23%.** Blue is the STRONGEST 1v1 archetype (Axiom+Paradox control + Mind-4 fat hand; crushes Frost 90%). Green is WEAKEST — but structurally: its best effects are ally buffs (dead in a duel). CRITICAL: the 1v1 sim STRUCTURALLY overrates blue (selfish control) and underrates green (team support). Color identities revealed: Red=consistent bruiser, Blue=1v1 controller, Green=team anchor. So DON'T buff blue (already #1) and DON'T buff green's selfish power off 1v1 numbers (would break team play where its support already works). Green balance, like hand-size, is un-evaluable until the team sim. Blue/green EFFECT-buff pass is on hold pending this reframe — awaiting Drew's direction.

**TEAM SIM built (Stage 1)** — `team_engine.py` (Battle: N-v-N, interleaved initiative wheel, target selection, taunt-forced targeting, team win), `team_policies.py` (TeamTactician: focus-fire lowest HP, recency defense), `team_run.py` (runner). Shares the 1v1 card effects: ally effects route through `engine.allies(me)` — empty in 1v1 (behavior UNCHANGED, verified: Steele>Frost still 66.3, Sage>Frost 90.2), populated in teams. Wired a few green ally effects live (Renewal heal, Twin Strike buff, Mockery taunt, Blood Tithe ally-heal). Stage-1 3v3 findings: **hand-size/blocking CONFIRMED** — Sage (Mind4, hand5) went 56%→87% vs Red in teams, because a big hand blocks the extra between-turn attacks. Validates the block-capacity thesis AND the decision to keep the +1. Green did NOT come alive (Adept 21→16% vs Red) BUT that's not trustworthy: only a thin slice of green's support kit is implemented and the naive focus-fire policy can't PILOT support (no proactive heals, buff timing, protective taunts). STAGE 2 needed for a fair green test: implement the full green support kit (Resonate/Support/Conduct/Witness/Shared Burden/Intercept) + a support-aware policy. Team sim under-rates green for the same reason 1v1 did — the AI can't use the cards.

**TEAM SIM Stage 2 (green vindicated):** implemented green's support kit (Resonate all-ally +2/Resist, Support/Conduct buff+draw, Witness heal, Shared Burden damage-redirect tank) + Warden deck (Soul4 green support) + support-aware team policy. Green went from worst-by-far to TEAM ANCHOR: Warden vs Sage(blue) 56% (was Adept 20%!), vs Steele(red) 28% (was 16%). 1v1 unchanged (support cards inert, Sage>Frost 90.5). **BIG FINDING — emergent archetype RPS in team play: Blue(Sage)>Red(Steele) 87%, Red>Green(Warden) 72%, Green>Blue 56% = the exact card RPS wheel (Blue>Red>Green>Blue) reproduced at the deck-archetype level.** Every color has a home and a predator; no color is "best". CONCLUSION on the balance arc: the system is well-balanced ACROSS formats; red's 1v1 damage, blue's 1v1 control, green's team support are context-specialized, not broken. Don't buff/nerf colors off single-format data.

**GREEN SELF-TARGETING buff (Drew, CANONIZED):** Green (Soul) cards count the user among their allies — green's heals/buffs/Resist/draw can target the caster. Rule added to `rules/cards.md` (You Are Not Your Own Ally → "Green counts itself among its allies"). Redirect-to-someone-else effects (Shared Burden) still need a separate target. Effect: gives green-SUPPORT decks a 1v1 floor (Warden went to viable: beats Sage 59%, even w/ Frost 50%, loses only to aggro) while attacker-green (Adept) stays weak — rewards building green AS support. In teams it pushed green's counter to blue 56%→63.5%. Team RPS triangle now clean & decisive: Blue>Red 71%, Red>Green 68%, Green>Blue 63.5% (green>blue is the softest leg; Drew wanted more of a blowout — open whether to push green's anti-control support harder, risking the triangle). Sim implements via `_team(engine,me)=[me]+allies` in green support effects; 1v1 ripple on Steele/Frost negligible (RENEWAL/TWIN STRIKE self-buff minor).

**Why Blue(Sage)>Red(Steele) is 71% in teams (Drew wanted it ~65):** it's NOT a color imbalance and NOT Axiom (Axiom is a TEAM DUD — removing it from Sage made Sage STRONGER, 71→83% vs Steele; its single-target one-reveal ban barely matters in a 6-person fight; Axiom is a 1v1 star only). The real driver is blue's Mind-4 blocking wall + card advantage, which walls MIDRANGE decks. Steele is balanced midrange → feeds the wall. But aggressive red BEATS blue: Volk(Body5, 7-red) vs Sage = Volk 58%. And green sustain beats blue (Warden 63%). So the meta is the classic AGGRO > CONTROL(blue) > MIDRANGE, with green-sustain also beating control. Blue's blocking is coupled to both its legs (can't lower B>R without raising G>B past 65). RECOMMENDATION (given): don't nerf — 71% is control correctly eating midrange; the counter to blue is aggression or sustain, both available. The "clean color triangle" is really a richer archetype metagame. No change made.

**Composable brains + Scry (Drew Q: can you combine brains?):** YES — via a shared sub-brain mixin. Implemented `ScryMixin` in policies.py: every action-brain (tactician/greedy/reader/team) composes with ONE shared scry strategy; any brain can override it for card-specific cleverness. `engine.scry(actor, owner, x)` added to both Duel and Battle (reorder top X of own or enemy deck). Strategy (Drew's): own deck → surface value, bury Wounds; enemy deck → bury their threats + the color that beats your attacks, leave junk/their Wounds on top. Wired to ALIGN (own) + AXIOM defense (enemy). FINDING: scry has ~0 measurable impact (+0.0-0.1% A/B) — SAME structural reason deck-tracking failed: tiny reshuffling decks defeat deck manipulation. Scry's real value is the conditional riders (ALIGN draw/+2) and human information, not the reorder. Composability architecture is the real deliverable. Rulings-log: gap-retaliate & stalemate-cap accepted as final simplifications; scry resolved by implementation.

**Scry keyword extended (Drew): Scry now = Scry + Surveil.** Look at top X, place each on top, bottom, OR into the discard pile. Canonized in `rules/card-glossary.md` + `rules/cards.md`. Engine.scry (both engines) supports the discard bucket. FINDING: binning to discard is combat-NEUTRAL (~0, even a scry-centric 4x-ALIGN deck; slightly negative) — in a small RESHUFFLING deck, binning a card just recycles it faster on the next reshuffle (MTG surveil works b/c no mid-game reshuffle; ours reshuffles). BUT surveil has real value OUTSIDE combat: binning a Wound to discard moves it where a short rest can REMOVE it (rest clears hand/discard, never deck) and where Press the Wound counts it — a Wound-MANAGEMENT tool, not a tempo tool. Sim brain bottoms Wounds (combat-optimal); engine keeps bin capability for the keyword. Through-line of the whole balance arc: deck-STATE strategies (track/scry/bin) are ~neutral in this system's tiny reshuffling decks; tempo/read strategies (recency, blocking, aggro, sustain) move win rates.

**Expanded card set (Drew): +21 cards implemented (~even split, team focus).** Now 55 real cards (19B/18R/18G). New engine hooks: Armour (flat reduction, clears next turn), can't-defend (Interrupt), team Intercept (ally steps in to defend — Battle only), Fortress damage-shield, AoE splash (Chain/Trample), ongoing support ticks (Synchrony team heal, Rooted Oath anchored buff), Patience "did I wait" flag. Cards: R = Strike, Guard, Intercept, Fortress Stance, Rally, Trample, Charge. B = Interrupt, Chain, Calculate, Analyze, Study, Profile, Refract. G = Synchrony, Rooted Oath, Urgency, Delay, Communion, Mirror Step, Patience. All ally effects route through engine.allies/_team (inert in 1v1, live in Battle). Verified: Guard armour, Intercept redirect, Fortress shield all work via forced test; 1v1 unchanged. Two test archetypes added to roster: `vanguard` (Body4 tank/protection) and `tempo` (Mind4 control). FINDING: pure-protection Vanguard is weak (6% vs Steele) even piloted well — protective cards are ROLE-PLAYERS, not deck cores; a deck needs a damage win-condition. Team policy now values protection/tempo/AoE by team need.

**Initiative Shift fully implemented (Drew), with true repositioning:** turn order is now a rotating QUEUE (queue[0]=current actor, rotates to back each turn) in both engines. `_apply_shift(engine,queue,target,amount)` REPOSITIONS the target by `amount` slots — it SETTLES into that new slot (verified: a +3 on the 3rd-in-line moves them to the back after lapping), it is NOT locked into the slot after the shifter. A positive shift that crosses the current marker ALSO grants a bonus turn now (pending_turns, fired right after the current turn); the target then settles at its shifted slot. Negative shift moves the target that many slots LATER (a real per-slot delay, more faithful than a binary skip; also fixes teams where the old `abs//n` gave 0 for |amount|<team-size, so Mockery/Delay did nothing in a 6-wheel). Interrupt/Delay tempo denial now bites in teams. 1v1 unchanged (Sage 88.9, Steele 63.5). Battles terminate (~18-34 turns, no runaway). Minor fidelity gap: after a crossing bonus the target may also act at its resettled slot the same cycle rather than strictly next cycle — negligible (Drew: intended, no fix).

**Predictable implemented (Study def) — blind simultaneity now breakable for one reveal.** `_study_defense` marks the attacker `_predictable_to = caster`; the engines (both) expose that attacker's actual card to the caster on their very next reveal (`defender._known_attack = card`), then the mark expires. Shared policy helper `perfect_read_defense(me)` consumes the peek with certainty: play the exact counter if held → else tie the same color to negate the hit (a tie deals no damage) → else decline and keep cards. Wired into every choose_defense (random/greedy/reader/tactician + team tactician). Only the caster benefits (if an ally Intercepts, the caster isn't defending, so no read; the mark persists until the marked foe attacks the caster). FINDING: value is small and appropriate — ~0.74 dmg prevented per duel on the defender (color-varying attacker vs diverse-hand Seer, 2000-duel A/B). It's a single-reveal defensive rider on a mid card, not a swing; STUDY only marks when it wins a block, and reads only the next reveal. Note: no ROSTER deck currently runs STUDY, so it never surfaces in the standard tournament — the mechanic is proven via forced-STUDY test decks. Was the last documented sim no-op; the sim now models every implemented card's effect. Audited (Drew's request): the exposed card (`_known_attack`) is set immediately before the marked defense choice and cleared immediately after, so it is present on EXACTLY the marked reveals and nowhere else (500-duel audit: read-flag count == PREDICTABLE-fire count, zero leftover after any run) — the early-reveal never leaks into ordinary blind defenses.

**Self initiative-shift — the marker-crossing model (Drew, corrected across TWO passes).** This one crossed the threshold twice; the log keeps both moves because the middle state was itself wrong.
- STATE 0 (original): `_apply_shift`'s current-actor branch (`i == 0`) did `pending_turns.append(target)` on ANY positive amount — so a current actor shifting ITSELF +1 got a bonus turn. Latent, not live (no card self-shifts as the current actor: Interrupt/Urgency's `initiative_shift(me,+3)` run in DEFENSE hooks where `me` is the defender at index >=1). Wrong: a +1 self-shift shouldn't be a free action.
- STATE 1 (my first fix — ALSO wrong, over-corrected): made the `i==0` branch reposition and NEVER cross — clamped so a self-shift could not grant a bonus/skip at all. WHY wrong: Drew clarified the actual model.
- DREW'S MODEL: on your turn you are ON the turn marker. Your first unit of movement takes you OFF the marker but does NOT cross it — "you can't pass it if you're on it, you have to go all the way around first." So the current actor crosses (→ positive: bonus turn; negative: skip) ONLY on a FULL revolution, `|amount| >= total seats`. This differs from a NON-current combatant, who sits some distance `i` AHEAD of the marker and so crosses at `amount >= i`. Same underlying rule ("cross when forward movement reaches the marker"); the actor's forward-distance-to-marker just happens to be a whole lap because it is sitting on it.
- STATE 2 (current, correct): the `i==0` shift is DEFERRED — `_apply_shift` records `engine._pending_self_shift`; the new shared `_rotate_current(engine, queue, who)` applies it at rotation time (when the actor is a normal back-of-wheel combatant, which also dodges the index-0 / run-loop-rotation collision). For `amount>0`: `>= total` → bonus turn + settle a lap back; `< total` → come around `amount` sooner (may land at "acts next" for `amount == total-1`, still NO bonus). For `amount<0`: `>= total` backward → `skip_turns += 1`; smaller → already latest, stays at back, no skip. Verified across sizes: in a 5-wheel +4 = acts next / no bonus, +5 = bonus; -4 = hold / no skip, -5 = skip; monotonic (+ never slower, - never faster). Both run loops now call `_rotate_current` (identical to the old inline rotate when there is no shift; tournaments unchanged, Steele 63.2 / Team B 64.6). Side effect (intended): Delay's DEFENSE on the current attacker now routes through this path — a full-lap `-3` skips in a duel (total 2), merely goes-last in a big team (total 6). Non-current forward-cross bonus turn preserved (regression-checked).
- FRAMING (Drew, cleaner): the whole thing is a FENCE. The turn marker is a fixed fence in the wheel; "you can hop off a fence you're standing on without jumping it." The current actor is on the fence, so moving off is never a crossing — mechanically identical to "did they complete a full revolution," but a cleaner mental model than bookkeeping revolutions. CANONIZED (Drew asked for the canon edit — "it's important to define exactly how the current turn marker functions and what constitutes crossing it"): `rules/combat.md` Initiative now defines **The turn marker** (fixed fence; whoever it points at is acting; it is the seam between has-acted and still-to-act this lap), and `rules/card-glossary.md` Initiative Shift X now defines **Crossing the turn marker** (path passes over it, landing on it counts; forward-cross = extra turn, backward-cross = skip) and **On the fence** (a target on the marker doesn't cross by leaving it, only by looping fully back). Sim already matches; no code change from the canon edit. RED-TEAM follow-up (Drew asked): the first canon draft left the OLD pre-fence wrap sentence ("a positive shift past the top drops them at the bottom") sitting next to the new crossing rule — a real contradiction (that sentence reads as delaying a positive shift, the one thing the rule forbids), plus the crossing/fence logic was restated 4x (longest entry in the glossary). Reconciled: dropped the wrap sentence (a loop has no ends to fall off, only the marker to cross), folded the guarantee into one clause, cut the entry ~14→9 lines, and moved the vivid "hop off a fence you're standing on" line to its single home in `combat.md` (the marker definition), with the glossary referencing it.

**Red-team tool evolved (Drew, via a GPT review) — from "find mistakes" to "attack invariants."** WAS: `agent-tools/red-team.md` targeted content-layer flaws (ambiguity, redundancy, tone, mechanical weakness, keyword compliance, visible reasoning) with a "list at least 3 issues" floor — right for the early wording-and-redundancy phase. WHY changed: the simulator now exposes rule architecture, timing policies, and emergent interactions; the deepest recent bugs were invariant violations (Predictable broke blind simultaneity), which the old tool would have missed. NOW the pass attacks: (1) INVARIANT VIOLATIONS first — hidden rule change / new timing window / engine special-case / implies a new POLICY not just an effect → name the invariant and ask if it should be a temporary rule modifier (direct hook to the architecture north star above; this catches the next Predictable); (2) SIMULATION ABUSE — exploit it in `combatsimulations/` if testable, else reason degenerate lines by hand, and say which; (3) mechanical RELEVANCE (does it matter) vs (4) IDENTITY (a decision nothing else creates) — split from the old single "mechanical weakness"; (5) ambiguity split into RULES vs NATURAL-LANGUAGE; (6) EVOLUTION CHECK — the LINEAGE lens: what did it mutate from, mutation or duplicate, cleaner expression? (Identity = play-feel uniqueness; Evolution = design provenance — kept complementary); plus one required GENERATIVE finding (an unintended interaction, which may be the best idea). Discipline: RANK findings Critical/Moderate/Cosmetic (no more equal effort on commas vs timing bugs), "LEAVE IT ALONE" is a valid verdict (dropped the forced-3 floor so it stops inventing edits), and a closing philosophy line: assume content is intentionally minimal, add complexity only to solve a demonstrated problem. My two refinements to the raw review: scoped Simulation Abuse so it never implies a sim run that didn't happen, and differentiated Identity vs Evolution to avoid overlap. Quest/Encounter + Visible-Reasoning passes kept intact. First live shakedown: red-teamed GAMBLER'S RUIN — invariant pass CLEAN (custom damage hook, no new timing/policy), simulation pass ran (mean 8.70 / range 6-17 @ Body4, max-3 cap load-bearing, balance-neutral in Steele ±1% A/B), verdict LEAVE IT ALONE (tool correctly declined to invent edits; Drew confirmed the "ruin" is the variance downside of relying on it and rolling low, and the mitigation ordering is already correct). Max dmg confirmed 17 = 4 body + 3+3+3 (three odd rerolls) + 4 (final die free).

**Prompt-refinement pass widened (Drew, via a GPT review) — from "fix the prompt" to "find the bottleneck."** WAS: `agent-tools/prompt-refinement.md` asked "what was unclear, missing, or inefficient?" and "suggest one improvement to the prompt/workflow" — which implicitly assumes the PROMPT is the problem and biases toward prompt-engineering tweaks. WHY changed: sometimes the real fix is not a prompt edit but a missing artifact — a canonical timing doc, an engine invariant doc, a structural change. NOW it asks where the BOTTLENECK is (prompt / missing repo context / missing docs / workflow / other) and REQUIRES preferring repo-structure/documentation/workflow fixes over wording tweaks, with an explicit anti-inflation rule ("do not add an instruction if better repo structure or docs would solve it — a prompt that only ever grows is a prompt going wrong"). Kept the key property: "if no meaningful improvement exists, return nothing — silence means converged" (a real stopping criterion; N clean runs with no suggested change = the process has converged). Both tool upgrades are the same move: LINEAGE is now building processes that improve themselves, optimizing the next thousand runs rather than the current one.

**Combat invariants documented — `rules/invariants.md` created (Drew).** The refinement pass literally predicted this artifact ("sometimes the fix is a missing invariant doc, not a prompt tweak") and Drew called it on the next turn. WAS: the engine's core assumptions lived only in code + scattered across `combat.md`/`core-rules.md`/`card-glossary.md` + memory; the red-team's new Invariant Violations pass had no canonical target to check against, and rule-benders had no declared list of what they were allowed to bend. NOW there is one canonical list, canon-level (game resolution assumptions; the sim is their executable model). Structure: the EXCHANGE as an ordered resolution loop (declare → blind simultaneous selection → RPS → apply outcome → damage pipeline), each step an invariant with its known modifiers named beneath it; STANDING invariants (wheel+marker, blocking-costs-a-card, derived stats, deck cycles, reveal private-then-public); and a RULE-MODIFIER REGISTRY table (Predictable/Axiom/Paradox/Interrupt/Stagger/Initiative Shift/Armour/Resist/Fortress/Intercept/Shared Burden/Evade → invariant bent → lifetime) that is the literal seed of the flags→typed-modifiers→policy-stack architecture. Wired `agent-tools/red-team.md` "check against" to point at it, closing the loop (invariant-violation check now has a referent). Damage pipeline order verified against both engines' `deal()`: redirect → volunteer-shield → Armour → Resist → floor → apply; Armour before Resist; single-hit clamp to 0; unpreventable skips Armour+Resist.

**Translation Principle added to CLAUDE.md (Drew).** WAS: workflow step 3 said "Clarify before executing — mandatory," which, taken literally, pushes toward asking Drew to restate things formally. But this whole session showed the opposite working pattern: Drew communicates in metaphors / examples / partial ideas (the fence, Gambler's Ruin, "two designers"), and the value came from TRANSLATING those into formal invariants, not interrogating him. NOW CLAUDE.md carries a Translation Principle: formalize the underlying invariant when the evidence strongly supports it; mark derived ones as *inferred* vs stated so he can confirm/correct; reserve clarifying questions for genuine forks the evidence can't settle ("a metaphor you can translate is not ambiguity"). Tempers step 3 without removing it. Same self-improving-process thread as the red-team + prompt-refinement upgrades — this one tunes how the agent reads Drew, not how it reviews content.

**Unpreventable = truly unpreventable (Drew ruling, from a red-team of `rules/invariants.md`).** The red-team flagged that "unpreventable" (card-facing on Spark, `cards/red-body.md`, but undefined in the glossary) overpromised: WAS — the sim let it skip only Armour + Resist, while a damage floor (Equal Footing) still capped it and, in team play, Shared Burden/Fortress still redirected/tanked it (including the latent bug of an ally tanking your own "Pay 2 HP" self-cost). I offered three forks (A rename to Irreducible / B keep word + define / C make it truly unstoppable). DREW CHOSE C, with the clean principle: **Equal Footing, Shared Burden, and Fortress are ATTACK-damage defenses only** — they do not apply to thorns, bleed, status damage, or HP costs. So unpreventable damage bypasses the ENTIRE pipeline (Armour, Resist, floor, redirect, shield) and lands full on the original target. NOW: both engines gate the floor on `not unpreventable` (was ungated), and `team_engine.deal` gates redirect + Fortress on `not unpreventable` (were ungated) — verified: attack 10 vs armour3/resist1/floor18 → 2 dealt; unpreventable 10 → 10 dealt; team redirect tanks an attack but NOT an unpreventable hit. Also fixes the self-cost-redirect bug for free. Balance-neutral in standard decks (Steele 63.2 / Team B 64.6 unchanged) — Equal Footing/redirects rarely meet unpreventable damage there. Canon updated: `rules/invariants.md` step 5 now says the pipeline is ATTACK damage and unpreventable bypasses all of it; added an **Unpreventable** glossary entry (was missing despite Spark using the word). Supersedes the earlier "unpreventable skips Armour+Resist" note in the invariants-doc entry above.

**Wait / Pass mechanic added (Drew) — a deliberate pass buys tempo.** WAS: passing (no action) was a pure dead turn, a feel-bad. FIRST PASS (my instinct, wrong): a Wait granted Initiative Shift **+2** (come around SOONER), capped never-cross. Drew caught the flaw — you are ON the marker when you Wait, so you can't act sooner than now anyway, and +2/sooner points the reposition the WRONG way for the real use (team sequencing usually needs you LATER, to follow an ally's setup — the exact "hold" direction +2 couldn't serve). CORRECTED MODEL: **Wait = forgo your action; choose X; move −X (X seats LATER in the order), up to one short of a full lap; act normally when the marker reaches your new spot.** The trade is an ACTION for a POSITION — you take one fewer action this fight (that IS the whole cost; your turn count drops vs everyone else) in exchange for landing exactly where you want. Why −X beats +2: the +2 handed tempo back (skip your action yet return sooner — muddy, rarely worth it); −X pays tempo for positioning, a clean real decision. NO CAP on X (Drew): a first version capped X ≤ N−1 to stay within the lap, but Drew removed the cap — because Wait moves you BACKWARD, crossing the marker is a SKIP, not a bonus. So a large X simply laps the wheel and skips a turn per full lap (per the glossary crossing rule: X ≥ N backward = one skipped turn per revolution) — i.e. a player can Wait far enough to "opt out" of the tempo for a stretch. Safe to uncap precisely because a backward crossing is always a cost, never a reward (only a POSITIVE/forward cross grants a bonus turn, which Wait can never do). Drew's framing: consolation on the surface, but its MAIN use is team coordination — chaining turns into the correct sequence. A party spends a Wait to pull a turn forward and slot it next to an ally's, so a setup and its payoff resolve back-to-back instead of being split by an enemy's turn; the phase shift persists, so one Wait fixes a combo cadence for the rest of the fight. This also resolves the directionality gap I'd flagged: −X Wait IS the voluntary "act later" tool, and from the marker "later by X" is the ONLY shift Wait ever needs (no +/− knob). **Pass = wait = the same choice**, and it is what "if you waited / didn't attack" effects (Patience) key off — which the sim already handled (`_attacked_last` is false after any non-attack turn, so passing already fed Patience; no change needed there). SIM: a forced pass (the only kind the brains do — they never plan a combo) is just a lost turn, X=0, normal rotation; the tactical −X Wait is intentionally UNMODELED (choosing X is multi-agent planning the brains lack, and per Drew we are not building the ultimate brain before the game exists). The earlier +2 shift-on-pass was removed, so both tournaments are back to baseline. Drew owns the +2 first instinct; the −X is his correction.

**First UNHELD card — Loose Grip (Drew, from the origin chat).** Founds `cards/unheld.md` — nothing was tagged UNHELD before, so this is card zero of the Unheld Oracle pool. GREEN — SOUL: Attack Soul+d4; Effect Initiative Shift +2; Def "ignore the next Initiative Shift used against you." Closes a loop between the day's engine work (the initiative wheel) and the world's soul (the Unheld — "surrender the shore"): a full initiative-control card where you flow with the wheel (+2 self) and can't be turned against it (shift immunity). Mechanically clean under the marker rules built today — +2 as a self-shift on your own turn reseats you 2 sooner in team play (≥4 seats), no free turn; it's the mirror of Wait (Wait = −X later for positioning; Loose Grip = +2 sooner as a reward). Normalized Drew's "Range: Either position" → house "Both." Open nuance: the Effect's +2 target is unstated but reads as SELF per the flavor. Not yet implemented in the sim (the shift-immunity defense would be a new flag).

**Tag convention defined (Drew) — a tag is a card's ACQUISITION SOURCE / lineage.** HISTORY: tags weren't in the original design; they evolved in, then sprawled — every concept in the world (locations, factions, each Seat: Time/Memory/Change, Unheld, Echo) was becoming a tag, an unbounded set, so Drew cut them from the core. Location tags survived on creature cards (COIL/HOLLOW/ASHFALL/ENGINE/BRIARWOODS/WEALD/BASIN); the force-tags (Promise/Mason/Unheld/Echo, ~8) were the ones stripped. DIAGNOSIS: the proliferation was structural, not sloppiness — they were tagging by THEME, and themes are infinite (every card is *about* many things). RESOLUTION (Drew's call): **a tag marks where/from whom a card was obtained — its provenance.** Source can be a location, an archon, a faction, a creature, the Unheld — anything you can trace acquisition to. This dissolved my proposed source-vs-synergy fork: it's ALL source; synergy is just a possible downstream use of provenance, not its definition. Rules canonized in `CLAUDE.md` (card format): at most ONE tag per card (its source); core cards carry none; a tag is never a theme (theme lives in the flavor line — "a card about memory is not a MEMORY card; that's what the quote is for"); test = "does removing the tag change how the card is obtained?" Why it holds: sources are countable, themes are not — finite by construction. Nice resonance: a tag IS a card's *lineage*, and the repo is named LINEAGE. No migration needed — existing location tags already conform (they're sources); core stays untagged. Loose Grip was the proof case (UNHELD = source, "stop fighting the wheel" = theme-in-flavor). PHILOSOPHY (Drew, canonized in `world/lineage.md`): "Knowledge always has a lineage." The tag records the most specific source players can still meaningfully identify. Location tags = living traditions whose origin is still recognized. TAGLESS ≠ originless — a tagless card's lineage has DIFFUSED until no single place claims it: universality is not absence of a source but a source that became the world (so the core is the most deeply-but-diffusely lineaged, not the plainest). Card NAMES carry the individual (authorship, signature techniques) so individual genius never fragments the tag system — names keep the people, tags keep the traditions. The `CLAUDE.md` tag rule now points to `world/lineage.md` for this world-truth.

**New tool — Card Compression Pass (Drew), enabled by the tag convention.** `agent-tools/card-compression.md`. Drew's insight: cleanly separable strands (mechanic / name / flavor / source) are the precondition for recombination — the tag convention made cards DECOMPOSABLE, which opens gene-level analysis. The tool treats each card as 4 genes, scores COMPONENTS across the whole set (not cards holistically), finds the weakest genes, and proposes recombinations that cut card count while preserving/increasing expressive power — nothing deleted, only rehoused. My sharpenings on Drew's spec: (1) gene RESONANCE not just strength — the four strands must AGREE (Loose Grip exemplar), a strong-but-incoherent merge is worse than the sum; (2) DENSITY not minimalism — evict cards that don't pull weight, don't shrink the set; a merge that costs a meaningful choice is bad even if count drops; (3) propose-don't-apply + log each merge (ripples into decks/sim/print); (4) it's the constructive sibling of red-team (same Relevance/Identity/Evolution component judgments, opposite purpose — recombine strengths vs find flaws); (5) a MATURITY tool — run when a set is bloated, not on a young set. Indexed in `agent-tools/README.md` (not an auto-trigger; run deliberately).

**First Card Compression Pass run on the CORE set (Drew accepted 6 merges).** The core was ~98 cards (Red 34 / Blue 31 / Green 33) — mature enough to compress. Ran `agent-tools/card-compression.md`; Drew accepted all 6 proposed merges. Result: **98 → 92 core cards.** Each merge (survivor ← retired, which gene went where):
- **URGENCY ← Route-Song** (green): Route-Song's effect was identical (Init Shift +3 to ally); its −3-attacker defense became a *choice* on Urgency's defense (+3 self OR −3 attacker). Route-Song name/flavor banked.
- **SUPPORT ← Conduct** (green): identical empower+draw; Support unchanged, Conduct deleted. Conduct's d6/"finds its rhythm" banked.
- **ALIGN ← Insight** (blue): two scry-color-gambles → one card with a *choice* payoff (draw or +4 dmg). Drew bumped the scry from 2 → 3 ("if any two share a color"). Insight's distinct party-color-shield defense BANKED (real gene, no home in the scry identity). NOTE (Drew, for a future pass): Align's effect and defense are still too similar — deliberately left for a later compression layer.
- **INTERCEPT ← Fortress Stance** (red): kept Intercept's redirect-and-defend (richer than Fortress's plain damage-eat), took Fortress's "all allies +2 HP" as the defense. Fortress "walls in flesh" banked.
- **MIRROR STEP ← Spiral Current** (green): kept move-both, took Spiral's Quick-granting defense. Spiral "water teaches stone to dance" banked.
- **FOCUS ← Excavate** (blue): Excavate's recursion became Focus's defense; Excavate was the weakest card in the set.
BANKED (preserved, not deleted, for reuse): Route-Song / Conduct / Fortress / Spiral names+flavor, and Insight's color-shield mechanic. SIM reconciled to stay honest: swapped the 3 sim-implemented retired cards in ROSTER decks to their survivors (Spiral→Mirror Step, Conduct→Support, Fortress→Intercept), removed their `add()` defs, wired Intercept's new team-heal defense (`_intercept_defense`); both tournaments unchanged (Steele 63.2 / Team B 64.6 — the swaps are behavior-equivalent in those matchups). Print sheet regenerated (92 cards, retired ones gone). Systemic finding for NEXT passes: blue's scry/deck-manipulation cluster is still over-provisioned vs its (sim-proven-neutral) impact; green's initiative + ally-empower clusters next. First real outing of the compression tool — it worked.

**Second compression pass — the SCRY cluster (Drew).** Scry was early-development goto filler (a card needed a second effect, scry was safe) so it spread thin, and the sim already proved deck-reorder is ~combat-neutral. Direction: fewer scry cards, and make the survivors pull weight by pairing scry with GUARANTEED value (a draw / a real effect), since reorder-only is what underperforms. Applied (blue, 29→28):
- CUT **ANALYZE** (team-scry 2 + reveal-hand, both low-value). "Reveal opponent's hand" banked.
- BUFF **PROFILE**: Effect "Scry 2" → "Scry 2, then draw 1" (scry now carries card advantage); keeps Stagger def.
- BUFF **ANTICIPATE**: "Each time attacked, Scry 2" → "The FIRST time attacked, Scry 2 and draw 1" (bounded reactive dig+draw).
- ALIGN left (already the buffed gamble, scry 3 / choice).
- RIDER TRIM (de-scry background filler): PREDICT def scry-1 → "negate attacker's next item use" (mirrors its item-denial identity); SLIPSTREAM def scry-1 → "Gain Evade" (slip the hit); AXIOM def scry-2 → "name a color, attacker can't play it next reveal" (mirror-ban).
BALANCE NOTE: this pass MOVED the sim (duel Steele 63.2→56.8, team B 64.6→62.0). Main cause = AXIOM's rider swap: scry (neutral) → mirror-ban (a real, modeled tempo denial) made Axiom a genuine control card, +~6% to Frost (who runs it); Profile's draw contributes. DREW'S CALL: KEEP the mirror (try it out). He named a design preference in passing — **effect and defensive bonus should not be too similar** (a density rule: a card whose two sides do the same thing is one-dimensional). Axiom's now are (both mirror-bans), so it joins a **DIFFERENTIATION QUEUE** for a future pass, alongside ALIGN (whose effect/def are also too alike) and RALLY (frontline+2 / backline+2 — same effect, position-flipped). The instrument showed de-scrying filler into real effects is a buff, not a cosmetic trim. SIM reconciled: removed ANALYZE add() (wasn't in any deck), added Profile's draw to `_profile_effect`, changed `_axiom_defense` scry→mirror-ban. Print regenerated (91 cards). NEXT targets (Drew's queue): generic +damage buffs (easy redundancy slim); initiative-shift is a DENSITY audit not a cull (keep enough to combo in concert).

**COUNT-PRIMACY: Initiative Shift redefined around Drew's prime invariant (the initiative audit's big find).** The audit's Delay question ("reactive Wait?") led Drew to a real canon hole: a defensive negative shift on the ATTACKER (who is on the marker) resolved by naive seat-walking could land them in a coming seat — a "slow" that ACCELERATED. Three bookkeeping models failed in sequence: (1) rotating queue + fence (positive-only thinking; negative-on-marker unhandled → sim silently clamped Delay's def to a no-op in teams); (2) marker-on-position + acted-this-round flag (Drew's table scenario: A shifted −2 from seat 1 lands seat 3, passed over — worked for negatives, but Drew's +1-wrap counterexample killed it: A shifted +1 wrapping to the last seat must act at the marker's FIRST arrival — the flag would have delayed a POSITIVE shift a full lap); (3) the fix: **DREW'S PRIME INVARIANT — "The mechanics exist to enforce the fantasy, not to break or subvert it."** The fantasy IS the definition: ±X means your next turn moves by EXACTLY X turns. Track TWO things (Drew: "it takes both pieces"): seat (spatial intuition) and COUNT (turns until you act — the truth). A shift rewrites the count by ∓X and moves ±X seats; when they disagree, THE COUNT WINS: marker arrives early → PASS-OVER (costs no time); count ≤ 0 → bonus turn (the existing rule, revealed as the same principle); displacement (slide from a cut-in) NEVER changes anyone's count; Wait sets your count to your chosen seat's arrival (no pass-over — the forfeited action was the payment; the razor distinction: a shift WRITES your count, Waiting CHOOSES it). No rounds — not even light ones; the count subsumed the acted-flag entirely (Drew's 😭 fully resolved). CANON: prime invariant now opens `rules/invariants.md`; Initiative Shift X rewritten in the glossary around count-and-seat with the table check ("how many turns away were you / are you now — positive may only lower it, negative only raise it, by exactly X"); combat.md marker = position-not-person + turn-in-progress-completes; Wait count clause added. SIM: `defer_turns` (owed delay) + hover pass-overs (pop, reinsert behind next actor, no tick) replace the negative-clamp and full-lap-skip branches; `skip_turns` now means only genuinely LOST turns (Interrupt). VERIFIED: Drew's two scenarios as named acceptance tests (A −2 → acts tick 8 not 6, third-seat acts right after B; A +1 wrap → acts tick 5, first arrival) + 370-case exactness sweep (wheels 4–8, every index, ±1..6, incl. current-actor self-shifts): every shift moves the next turn by EXACTLY X. Tournaments: duel byte-identical (Frost/Steele run no negative shifts — honest null), team 38.1/61.8 (≈noise; Delay's def now real where tempo decks run it). Sim simplification noted: URGENCY's merged defense choice (+3 self OR −3 attacker) is modeled as +3-self only. Also applied from the audit: STARING CONTEST defense buffed from "attacker −1" (weakest shift in the set) to "move yourself to immediately follow the attacker" — reactive precision placement, on-identity (lose the contest by moving first), feeds Slipstream/Synchrony; Drew ruled DELAY's mirror stays (not all mirrors need fixing) and it does NOT join the differentiation queue. Audit verdicts: Urgency/Interrupt/Mockery/Slipstream/Synchrony dense, leave; suite kept whole for concert play (accelerators/decelerators/payoffs/immunity all present; Slipstream = the traffic payoff, only fires on shifts).

**THE CANONICAL COORDINATES: countdown vector under the wheel (Drew, closing the count-primacy arc).** Drew: "I see the math now — just count how many turns until next action, for all characters." The wheel is the human RENDERING; the countdown vector is the STATE. He asked for the one piece he hadn't internalized: how a shift changes the OTHER combatants' counts (he computes it visually via insert+slide). FORMALIZED AND ENGINE-VERIFIED (73-case schedule-diff sweep on top of the 370-case exactness sweep): a non-crossing shift is ONE relocation — (1) the target's next turn moves exactly X and the new seat PERSISTS (steady-state lap order = old order with the target alone relocated; cyclic order of everyone else identical); (2) during the transition, each of the X turn-slots the target passes nudges 1 turn the opposite way — and those slot owners can include someone's NEXT-LAP turn (a back-seat target shifted −2 passes the current and next actors' *second* turns); (3) nobody else moves at all. So "insert the target, slide everyone they passed" — Drew's visual procedure — IS the formal cascade; he had it internalized in seat-coordinates all along. Two instructive test failures en route (kept honest): measuring only first-actions missed next-lap slot owners (looked like non-conservation), and sequence-equality-after-removing-the-target failed because the reseat is PERMANENT (canon: the reposition persists) — the correct claim is cyclic-order equality sans target. CANON: added the two-coordinate-systems bullet to `rules/invariants.md` (the engine reasons in turns-until-next-action; players reason in seats; same state, and insert+slide computes the relocation exactly). Pattern note (GPT named it; it's real): the repo keeps finding a canonical representation beneath the intuitive one — tags→lineage, metaphors→invariants, wheel→countdown vector. Human-facing stays intuitive, engine-facing stays formal, and the rules document the seam.

**HIERARCHY FINALIZED + TRANSLATION GUARDRAIL + WORK MODES (Drew, closing the representation thread).** Three decisions in one pass:
1. **Linear order is canonical; countdown is DERIVED; wheel is UI.** Supersedes the previous "countdown vector is canonical" bullet (Drew's refinement): the canonical state is the LINEAR turn order; turns-until-action = position − 1 (current actor normalized to 1); the wheel exists only to visualize wraparound ("the rules are linear; the wheel is circular because turns repeat forever"). A shift = list operation (remove, reinsert ±X, slide intervening by one); the wheel updates because it pictures the list. Full design stack now in `rules/invariants.md` preamble: FANTASY (what should the player feel?) → INVARIANT (what must stay true?) → MECHANIC (rules enforcing it) → IMPLEMENTATION (lists/engines/visualizations), plus the subsystem questions (fantasy / canonical state / derived / visualization). Documentation rule that falls out: teach from the invariant, not the picture. Sim needs NO change — its queue already IS the linear order (index = derived count); the representations were aligned all along.
2. **Translation Principle hardened — "never silently redefine an established invariant" (`CLAUDE.md`).** Honest origin: during the initiative saga the agent twice reconciled a contradiction between Drew's words and the never-hasten invariant by INVENTING semantics (the passed-over token; the acted-this-round flag) — each mechanically coherent, each fantasy-breaking (the flag would have delayed a POSITIVE shift a full lap). The failure mode: user input contradicts an invariant → agent synthesizes an interpretation instead of surfacing the conflict. New rule: if it could be a wording slip, ask; otherwise name the invariant and the contradiction explicitly; the pre-interpretation test is "will this change the meaning of something already established?" Plus a three-level ambiguity taxonomy: (1) harmless → resolve silently; (2) design choice → choose and log; (3) protected invariant (turn order, RPS, core formulas, anything in invariants.md) → STOP and surface. An unnecessary clarification is cheaper than a silently mutated design.
3. **Work Modes adopted (`CLAUDE.md`) — compiler-style dirty tracking (Drew: token use getting heavy from rebuilding sim + print per change).** WORKING (default): edit targets only, record stale dependents under the new "Pending propagation" ledger at the top of memory.md. SYNC: propagate through dependents on request or at a natural pause, clear ledger. RELEASE: full verification (acceptance tests, tournaments, print, empty ledger). First use: this very batch is docs-only — no dependents dirtied, ledger stays empty.

**INITIATIVE: SOLVED AND CLOSED (Drew).** "As solved as it will need to be for the rest of the game's design." Final state: fantasy defined (±X means your next turn moves exactly X); prime invariant proven, not asserted (370-case exactness sweep + 73-case cascade sweep, all wheel sizes, all positions, both signs, self-shifts included); canon written at three altitudes (linear order = canonical state, count = derived, wheel = visualization — `rules/invariants.md`, `rules/card-glossary.md`, `rules/combat.md`); sim aligned (queue IS the linear order; defer/hover realizes pass-overs; Drew's two table scenarios are named acceptance tests); Wait, the fence, the marker, displacement, and crossing all derive from the one invariant with no special cases left. Initiative is now a **protected system** (Level-3 in the Translation Principle's taxonomy): reopening it requires playtest evidence, not impulse — the same fence the core HP formula got. Known, accepted margins (logged, deliberately not blocking closure): Urgency's sim defense models +3-self only; duels remain the degenerate corner (bespoke rules deferred until 1v1 is ever a real mode). The arc, for the record: rotating queue → fence → marker+flag → count-primacy → linear-order hierarchy — five models across three sessions, each killed by one of Drew's table-run counterexamples, ending at the discovery that his table intuition WAS the specification. The wheel turns for those who stop fighting it.

**Metric taxonomy named (GPT via Drew) — behavioral / mechanic-quality / implementation.** Three layers of measurement: BEHAVIORAL (did the game behave as intended — action economy, combat length, win rates: the simulator's jurisdiction); MECHANIC QUALITY (is the rule elegant — conditionals, cognitive load, resonance, differentiation: the design tools' jurisdiction — red-team finds flaws, compression recombines strengths); IMPLEMENTATION (how the engine executes: invisible unless optimizing code). Key division of labor, now codified in `card-compression.md`: **the sim can prove two designs equivalent; it can never pick between them** — when behavior ties, the simpler mechanic wins ("the ideal card reaches the desired behavior with the least unnecessary machinery" — Drew's "fewer cards, no ideas lost" was always this optimization). Claude's sharpening (logged as the stronger form): the layers LEAK at a human table — rules complexity IS behavior when the runtime is human (conditionals tax working memory → slower turns, misplays), costs the sim's code-reading players never pay. So "the prettier rule wins" is not taste above behavior; it is a prediction about behavior in the only runtime that matters — and pricing cognitive load is designer judgment, which is why compression proposes and Drew rules.

**Canon Gate reframed: tiers → AUTHORITY LEVELS (GPT via Drew; applied same-day).** Two refinements to the entry below: (1) RENAME — "tier" implied magnitude; the real axis is JURISDICTION ("who is allowed to commit this kind of change?"). A level-1 creature can be incredible; a level-3 wording tweak can be four words. Now: **Authority 1 — Established Language** (agent authority), **Authority 2 — Canonical Extension** (agent authority with audit), **Authority 3 — Constitutional** (Drew's authority — anything that changes how other content is interpreted). (2) ANTI-DRIFT RULE on Authority 2: "an extension may extend canon but may not redirect existing themes" — the guard against five hundred harmless extensions quietly reshaping the world; it reuses the Translation Principle's own test (*will this change the meaning of something already established?*), so one question now polices both interpretation and extension. Claude's addition: the post-review queue doubles as the drift instrument — cumulative reinterpretation shows up there as a PATTERN across many lines before it becomes a fact. Vocabulary swept through CLAUDE.md, experimental/README, both generators, tools README; "tier" now belongs exclusively to difficulty (Early/Mid/Late). GPT's meta-note, worth keeping: the gate wasn't an invented governance model — it named the one already being practiced, which is why it had evidence behind it.

**THE CANON GATE LOOSENED — tiered placement (Drew: "loosen the gate, on content only, not rules/invariants").** WAS: everything waited for Drew's formal pass before touching canon — and since most content verdicts were predictably "approved," finished work piled at the gate (the failure mode both this branch's audit and Codex's sweep independently flagged). NOW (`CLAUDE.md`, The Canon Gate — the Translation Principle's three levels applied to placement): **Tier 1** (uses established canon only: encounters/creatures/cards/items/NPCs from existing truth) ships DIRECT after mandatory red-team/alignment; **Tier 2** (clean extensions: new faction behavior, map seats, deepened NPCs) ships FLAGGED — Drew's review becomes veto-after instead of approve-first; **Tier 3** (rules, invariants, keywords, formulas, cosmology, contradictions, open world-level hooks) stays gated exactly as before, permanently. MECHANISM: new standing **Recently shipped (post-review queue)** section atop memory.md — one line per Tier 1/2 ship; Drew clears by blessing or objecting; every ship is one revert away. Why it's safe: the automated checks stayed mandatory and the history is reversible — the gate loosened because the checks got good, not because review stopped. The tier boundary was traced from Drew's own revealed rulings this week (Shrike shipped and kept = Tier 1; Borrowers-keep-flocks ratified in one line = Tier 2; Weck sent back = Tier 3). Propagated: experimental/README (folder purpose = genuinely-unresolved only; sign-off rule now Tier-3-scoped), both generators' routing, agent-tools README workflow step 6.

**External validation — the repo explains itself (Drew's report).** Three outside models have now navigated the repo cold: Grok (earlier review), GPT (ongoing via chat), and Codex (full sweep on its branch). Pattern across all three: no structural complaints — feedback is always CONTENT-focused. New visitors orient and jump straight into design work, which is the architecture succeeding invisibly. Codex's single biggest finding: good material stranded in experimental — the same conclusion this branch's audit reached independently days earlier (Canille/Pneum/Lightning Loop promotions), confirming both the finding and the known failure mode: Drew's canon gate works so well that finished work piles up AT the gate. Standing habit adopted: periodic experimental sweeps with promotion recommendations, whenever the folder feels heavy.

**Exemplars crib built (Drew: "exemplars would save us tokens in the long run").** The generators' onboarding demanded full canon reads every run (card-set-generator alone: glossary + all 3 core files + 2 signature sets + rules/cards.md ≈ 1,000+ lines per invocation). NEW: `agent-tools/exemplars.md` — a precompiled digest (engine facts, tier anchors with real statlines, card/statblock/encounter skeletons with calibrated exemplars, deck+tag conventions, Weck as the NPC register, tone in one breath). Explicitly marked a BUILD ARTIFACT, not a source of truth: every fact cites its canonical home, and when canon changes underneath it, the crib goes on the Pending-propagation ledger and refreshes at Sync — the Work Modes model applied to READING. The 3 heavy generators' onboarding collapsed to: crib + the task's own files + escalate-to-canon-only-when (bending a rule, novel keyword use, staleness suspected). Same pattern as everything this month: cache the derivation, keep the source authoritative, document the seam.

**Fleet reality (Drew's update).** Codex has had one full session on its branch (`claude/crystal-project-chat-76gzcn` — edge cases + definition refinements, deliberately kept unmerged; doesn't affect work here). Ongoing Codex help is limited by plan tier and Drew is NOT upgrading — the overseer function is instead covered ~80% by the existing Drew↔GPT copy-paste channel, which has already been the de facto pattern (the prime invariant, the metric taxonomy, the countdown coordinates, and the tag insight all arrived through it). Practical implications: the codex-starter note + branch map stay valid for occasional sessions; Codex's branch changes await Drew's merge decision; don't design workflows that DEPEND on Codex being responsive.

**Experimental audit executed (Drew ratified).** The folder had accumulated both finished work and fossils; the pass sorted it: PROMOTED — `canille.md` → `places/` (was already load-bearing: geography table listed it, tonal bible cites it by name), `pneum.md` → `places/` (survived the hard-edge Unheld rework cleanly; barge practice fits), `lightning-loop.md` → `items/` (Kaine + Storm Seat both canon; 3 refs in `quests/tide-pulls-back.md` updated; geography table now seats Pneum and drops Canille's "experimental" note). DELETED (stale, fully absorbed into canon) — `minotaur-coil.md` (cards promoted, passives in bestiary), `wall-reader-coil.md` (draft header only), `cards-flapjack-octopus.md` (canon strictly newer: Flatten interactions), `alignment-marshal-cards.md` (April draft; its 4 dropped cards — CORRECTIVE MEASURE, PLATFORM SHOVE, SEQUENCE LOCK, TRAJECTORY HOLD — salvaged to `archives/cut-cards.md` first per the cut-card rule). HELD IN EXPERIMENTAL (Drew's calls) — **black-maggie.md + lore-fragments** (content is promotion-quality but smuggles cosmology: the Thessians + a forgotten Archon — world-level ratification, a natural first review for Codex); **apnea.md** (dreaming island, near-ready, needs a map seat); **corrupted-bather.md + void-runner.md** (audit correction: `quests/tide-pulls-back.md` DOES exist, so these await a live arc, not a hypothetical one — but the Bathers still need an alignment re-check vs the hard-edge coast before promotion); **the-man-who-buys-wounds.md** (Weck's ledger unratified).

**FOUR-ITEM CLEANUP BATCH — one real bug found along the way.** (1) **`codex-starter.md` archived** — `git mv` into `archives/` (previously empty but for a `.gitkeep`); `CLAUDE.md`'s directory-table row broadened from "reserved for deprecated canon content" to include "retired process notes," since this isn't canon content but fits the archive's spirit given Codex's rare-contributor status (logged earlier this session). (2) **Card compression gained an "Unplaced Genes" section** — the tool's own preservation rule (every strong gene must resurface in a survivor) had no fallback for a gene that genuinely can't find a home; added a lightweight fragment-log distinct in shape from `cut-cards.md` (which holds whole rejected cards, confirmed by reading it — a structural mismatch for sub-card genes). Also fixed the tool's closing "what to check against" line, which pointed at `combatsimulations/` to judge "whether a mechanic actually matters" — a second instance of the sim-as-arbiter mistake already corrected elsewhere this session; repointed to red-team.md's existing Mechanical Relevance/Identity checks. (Left untouched: the file's OTHER sim reference — "the sim can prove two designs equivalent; it can never pick between them" — already correctly scoped, proving equivalence being a computational fact, not a design judgment; good instance of not over-correcting once a principle's been named.)

(3) **Discard-timing fix — turned out to be a live bug, not a documentation nicety.** Drew's observation (discarding should be written as happening after RPS resolves, not as part of the declare/select steps) led to investigation that found `rules/combat.md` and `rules/core-rules.md` both wrote step 1 as "attacker plays AND DISCARDS," before RPS in step 4 — and `combat.md` itself admitted the "blind" defense relied on the defender's discipline not to look, not on real concealment. Digging into the simulator's actual code (not just prose) found the real version of that gap: `engine.py` and `team_engine.py` both mutated `attacker.discard`/`last_color`/`attack_history` with the CURRENT card's color before calling `defender.policy.choose_defense(...)` — and four real policies (`GreedyPolicy`, `ReaderPolicy`, `TacticianPolicy`/`PunisherPolicy`, `TeamTactician`) read exactly those fields as their "blind" prediction. On a combatant's first attack of a duel, `ReaderPolicy`'s prediction was the current card's color with total certainty — reading the answer, not guessing from history. FIXED in both rules files (discard now explicitly tied to the simultaneous-reveal step) and in both engines (a Plan agent validated the exact minimal-diff fix: move exactly three mutating lines to fire immediately after `choose_defense` returns, confirmed via full-file read that no other field — `_attacked_this`, `_last_hit`, `_attacked_last` — leaks the same way, and that FORGET's defense, which reads `foe.discard` after `rps()` resolves, is unaffected since the reveal still lands before outcome application). Verified: both tournaments run clean post-fix; team win-rate nearly unchanged (58.1%→58.0%), duel numbers shifted more (44.9/53.8→47.5/50.0, avg turns 6.8→4.5) — consistent with genuinely-blind defenses mispredicting more often now that the peek is closed, not a new problem. This is the third time this session that "keep the sim's code honest to canon" (a maintenance concern, explicitly kept even after cutting "use the sim to evaluate design decisions") caught something real.

(4) **Sync vs. Release, answered directly, no file changes** — Sync propagates whatever's in the Pending-propagation ledger until it's empty, triggered by accumulated staleness; Release is a full verification pass regardless of the ledger, triggered by wanting a clean milestone state.

**TRANSLATION PRINCIPLE / CANON GATE — reordered and de-duplicated (Drew's catch, refined through two agents and a live pushback).** Drew: "the translation principle was conflated, as was the authority levels... don't overly trust what I have to say when it comes to CLAUDE.md, agent tools, etc." — an explicit instruction to apply independent scrutiny on agent-doctrine structure rather than defer, since (his own calibration) he's a novice on the agent side, decent on architecture, expert on the game. Investigated via Explore agent rather than assumed: the redefinition test ("will this change the meaning of something already established?") was stated three times across ~18 lines (Authority 2's body, Authority 2's parenthetical, Translation Principle's own bullet), and two independently-numbered 1/2/3 systems (Translation Principle's "ambiguity levels," Canon Gate's "Authority levels") shared the word "constitutional" for tier 3 with tiers 1/2 never mapped — an unlabeled parallel structure inviting exactly the conflation named. `memory.md`'s own history (this file, entries on Authority-2's anti-drift rule and the tier→Authority rename) shows the duplication was intentional reuse of one test for two purposes, not accidental drift — confirming this needed surgical un-conflating, not a rewrite.

A Plan agent validated the fix and caught an independent bug in the process: the existing text said "Authority 3 in the Canon Gate **below**" when Canon Gate sat *above* Translation Principle in the file — already wrong, unrelated to today's fix, caught only because the exact clause was being touched anyway.

**Drew's live pushback on the draft plan was the sharpest catch of the three:** "shouldn't the translation principle be applied before the canon gate? if something's not interpreted correctly then how can it be correctly gated?" Correct, and it exposed something the plan itself was quietly doing wrong — Authority 2 was written to forward-reference a not-yet-introduced term ("apply Translation Principle's redefinition test, *below*"), which is bad document structure independent of the conflation: you shouldn't ask a reader to use a concept before defining it. Reordered the sections (Translation Principle now precedes Canon Gate) so the reference direction matches both the logical dependency (interpret, then classify) and ordinary expository writing (define before use).

RESOLVED: redefinition test now stated exactly once, in Translation Principle, and named ("the redefinition test") so Authority 2 can point *backward* at it instead of restating it. The two 1/2/3 systems no longer share unlabeled numerals — Authority keeps its numerals (used pervasively downstream: encounter-generator.md, npc-encounter-generator.md, README.md, experimental/README.md — confirmed via grep, none needed changes), ambiguity-levels drops its numerals entirely and is explicitly framed as a different axis (confidence-in-reading vs. who-may-ship, kept genuinely distinct per Drew's own good example: a fully-confident interpretation of a clearly-stated new keyword is still Authority 3). Verified post-edit: the test phrase now appears exactly once repo-wide; section order is Work Modes → Translation Principle → Canon Gate → Do Not; both cross-references resolve correctly in the new direction.

**"DESIGN PHILOSOPHY" → "DESIGN PRINCIPLES" — Drew self-diagnosing a real pattern, checked against the dictionary rather than just accepted.** Drew: "I misuse words that have a clear definition... whatever the word exemplar is supposed to mean, I don't got it... design principles is actually more technically correct" than philosophy. Checked both claims rather than agreeing reflexively: **"exemplar"** — dictionary definition is "one that serves as a model or example... an ideal model," from Latin *exemplar*, "pattern, model." This matches Drew's own gloss ("the embodiment of the ideal") almost exactly — the word was right; the bug was mine, pouring two concepts (efficiency crib + curated best-content) into one filename until the word had to serve both. Told him this directly rather than agreeing with the self-criticism where the evidence didn't support it. **"Design principles" vs "philosophy"** — this one Drew called correctly: "philosophy" names an overarching worldview; "principles" names discrete, independently-checkable statements — and the file is a flat list of 8 independently-testable bullets, not a unifying worldview paragraph. Renamed `agent-tools/design-philosophy.md` → `agent-tools/design-principles.md`; swept all nine referencing files (CLAUDE.md ×4 instances, rules/invariants.md, exemplars.md, README.md, compiled-crib.md, red-team.md ×3, encounter-generator.md, card-set-generator.md) plus one stray "design-philosophy principle" phrase in Translation Principle's own prose. Confirmed clean via full-repo grep both before and after.

**BRIARWATCH JACKRABBIT → BRIARWATCH JACKALOPE (Drew, half-joking catch, then confirmed straight).** Drew: *"jackalopes Claude 😉"* — pointing out the exact thing Fenwool had just been built to avoid was already sitting in canon: "Jackrabbit" is a real animal, named plainly, in the middle of a creature roster that otherwise reskins everything. Asked whether it was a joke or a real rename request; Drew: *"jackalopes have always been the vision I've had of them, I just had to look up the name to remember it. so yeah let's rename it."* — a direct statement, not an inference, so applied without further hedging (Translation Principle). A jackalope is itself already-mythical (jackrabbit + horns), so this isn't "reskin a real animal into a fantasy one," it's "give the design its actual, already-intended name" — and it comes with one small addition, not just a find-replace: **a small rack of horns set close against the skull**, since Drew confirmed the horned image was the intent all along, not an afterthought. EXECUTED: renamed `bestiary/briarwatch-jackrabbit.md` → `bestiary/briarwatch-jackalope.md` and `cards/briarwatch-jackrabbit.md` → `cards/briarwatch-jackalope.md`; swept every cross-reference (`quests/hollow-below-briarwatch.md` ×2, `quests/shifting-burrow.md`, `agent-tools/compiled-crib.md`'s Creature Threat Rating anchor table, `printing/generate-cards.py`'s file list, `bestiary/fenwool.md` ×2, `places/briarwatch.md` ×2). LEFT AS-IS, deliberately: `memory.md`'s historical entries and `playtesting/session-1-combat-review.md` — a session log recording what the creature was actually called when that session was played; rewriting it would falsify the record, same reasoning as never editing memory.md's own past entries. Verified via whole-repo grep: zero remaining "jackrabbit" outside those two historical records.

**WHERE-THE-TRACKS-STOP ARCHIVED (Drew: "made for me as developer... meta").** Not table content — retired to `archives/where-the-tracks-stop.md` per the archives convention (`archives/` = deprecated canon + retired process notes). Removed its now-dangling Related Documents line from `experimental/the-man-who-buys-wounds.md`. Left otherwise unedited except one downstream fix — see the FENWOOL entry below.

**FENWOOL NAMED — Briarwatch's livestock, previously an unnamed placeholder word (Drew: "what's the livestock in briarwatch? I've avoided going into it because I don't want to just list real animals").** "Livestock" had been load-bearing across half a dozen files (the Warden Post's report, Bartho's ledger, Weck's culls, the Stonecoils' diet, the archived Tracks-Stop quest's "sheep prints") without ever being a real, named thing — an open slot Drew flagged rather than filled himself, which is exactly the "formalize what's strongly implied" case (Translation Principle). PROPOSED IN CHAT FIRST, not written straight to canon — offered one fully-realized concept rather than a menu, per Drew's own framing that he'd already been picturing something (sheep-shaped) and just needed the reskin. Drew confirmed: *"let's run with it."* **Fenwool** — broad, splay-toed grazers whose feet don't sink in Briarwatch's soft limestone ground; oiled, water-shedding coat that still shears (keeps the "wool" economic role without literally being a sheep); graze the hedgerow bramble at the farmland's edges — the same regrowing scrub the newly-displaced Jackrabbits and Scratchers use (ties directly into the BRIARWATCH REDEFINED entry above: forage competition, not coincidence, is why those two show up around the fields at all). Skittish, herd tight, sense wrongness first — reuses the world's existing "livestock knows first" motif (`places/weatherheart-vale.md`) rather than inventing a new one. EXECUTED: new `bestiary/fenwool.md` (no stat block — mundane/non-combat, same pattern as `bestiary/emerald-frog.md`); named Fenwool explicitly everywhere "livestock" meant Briarwatch's specifically — `places/briarwatch.md` (Wardens, Warden Post, Related Documents), `bestiary/stonecoil.md`, `quests/hollow-below-briarwatch.md` (×2), `quests/the-larder-fence.md`, `bestiary/fencerow-shrike.md`'s GM Secret, `world/tonal-bible.md`, `experimental/the-man-who-buys-wounds.md`, and the archived Tracks-Stop file's "sheep prints" line (fixed even though archived, since it cost nothing and Drew specifically flagged it: *"I'm pretty sure the word sheep is in there somewhere"*). LEFT GENERIC, deliberately: two "livestock" mentions in `bestiary/fencerow-shrike.md` and `quests/the-larder-fence.md` where Fenwool is already named earlier in the same file (redundant to repeat); `places/weatherheart-vale.md` (different location, own livestock, out of scope); `playtesting/briarwatch-playtest.md` and `memory.md`'s own historical entries (session logs and threshold entries stay as originally written). Verified via whole-repo grep: zero remaining "sheep"; "livestock" now only appears where it's either genuinely generic or already redundant with a named Fenwool nearby.

**BRIARWATCH REDEFINED — not a town, a farm-claim cut into Turnroot Weald; the Briarwoods dissolved into it (Drew, A3, ruled directly in chat).** WAS: Briarwatch a "farming settlement"/"town," with a separate travel-corridor woodland — the Briarwoods — running the full two days from Vulture's Nest through the Roadhouse to the Briarwatch fork, hosting its own creatures (Jackrabbit, Scratcher) under a `BRIARWOODS` card source tag. NOW, per Drew's ruling and the four follow-up calls he made resolving it: (1) **The road is just road** — no woods; Turnroot Weald only becomes relevant at Briarwatch's own fenced edge, nowhere earlier on the Vulture's Nest → Roadhouse → Briarwatch route. (2) **The `BRIARWOODS` tag → `BRIARWATCH`** — swapped on all four cards that carried it (Jackrabbit, Scratcher, Fencerow Shrike signature sets, plus the crib's example card), since a tag marks acquisition source and these creatures are now sourced from Briarwatch's farmland, not a separate wood. (3) **`quests/where-the-tracks-stop.md`** — wording scrubbed only (per Drew: don't relocate it yet); it stays loosely staged "on the road to Briarwatch," relocation to the farmland left for a later pass, flagged not forgotten. (4) **The Roadhouse stays exactly as it was** — Drew confirmed it explicitly ("still exists... still on the road to briarwatch") — Warden Post, notice board, and Session 1 routing through Briarwatch are untouched; only the *identity* of Briarwatch itself changed, not its functional stops. THE CORE MOVE: Briarwatch's farmland is now explicitly framed as land cut a little way into Turnroot Weald's own territory — this was already latent (the GM Secret already had the Masons "carving out the ground Briarwatch farms" from the Weald) but is now the location's front-facing identity, not a buried secret detail, and it's the stated reason the Masons' watchtower/larder fence exists at all. **Creatures displaced by that encroachment stayed close, per Drew's explicit line** ("creatures displaced live on the farmland"): Briarwatch Jackrabbits nest in the watchtower ruins, Briar Scratchers work the hedgerows and fencelines between fields — both now described this way in `places/briarwatch.md` and their own bestiary entries.
