EXECUTED: deleted `places/briarwoods.md` entirely; renamed `items/briarwoods-items.md` → `items/briarwatch-items.md` (content untouched beyond the header, since its actual item sources were already Briarwatch/Roadhouse-specific); swept every cross-reference (`world/geography-overview.md`, `rules/combat-example.md`'s title, `places/vultures-nest.md` ×2, `places/roadhouse.md`, `printing/generate-cards.py`'s file list, `quests/the-larder-fence.md` ×2, `bestiary/skeinwing.md`, `bestiary/fencerow-shrike.md` ×3, `factions/the-masons.md`, `bestiary/emerald-frog.md`'s incidental habitat line — retargeted to Briarwatch's limestone rather than deleted outright). Also swept stray "the town" phrasing left over in `bestiary/fencerow-shrike.md` and `quests/the-larder-fence.md` that the tag/file sweep alone wouldn't have caught — found on the verification re-grep, not the first pass. Verified via whole-repo case-insensitive grep: zero remaining "briarwoods" outside this log's own historical entries (which stay as originally written, per this log's append-only convention); zero remaining "town" referring to Briarwatch specifically (Vulture's Nest, Glasslight Reach, and other towns elsewhere in the repo are unrelated and untouched). No mechanics changed — pure geography/identity, Design Principle "ecology drives mechanics" reinforced rather than violated.

**BRIARWATCH GEOGRAPHY FIX, PART TWO — Drew's own follow-on edit, partly accidental, partly a real ruling.** Drew edited `places/briarwatch.md` and `bestiary/fenwool.md` directly and pushed, then said "I removed more stuff." Checked the diff rather than assuming it was all intentional — good thing: the `# Briarwatch` title, its opening paragraph, and the tail of the Valhug Lake sentence ("...fed by the same limestone water table that opened the sinkhole" — cut off mid-thought, no period) were gone. Confirmed with Drew: title loss was accidental, opener "possibly" accidental too — restored both plus the truncated sentence. The `## The Wardens` section's removal was NOT accidental, though — Drew: "the wardens goes with the roadhoues. if it doesn't move it dies." A real ruling, not a mistake: Wardens-as-people (the missing-Warden mystery, boots ending up at Harwick Sundries in Vulture's Nest) is road/travel lore, not farmland lore, and belongs with `places/roadhouse.md`. MOVED the full section there, placed right after "The Guards" (parallel structure — Regency soldiers vs. Briarwatch's own local watch). Separately, Drew asked why `bestiary/fenwool.md`'s cut "this is the animal behind every generic livestock mention" paragraph was needed at all — good question, checked, and it wasn't: the actual sweep (Warden Post report, Bartho's ledger, Weck's line) already says "Fenwool" directly everywhere, done earlier this session, so that paragraph was explaining a generic form that no longer existed anywhere. Cut confirmed correct, not restored. **Still open:** whether the Warden Post *bullet* (the building — notice board, bounties, the sinkhole/Fenwool/cave reports) moves to Roadhouse too, alongside the Wardens-as-people content, or stays as a Briarwatch Notable Location since it's still Briarwatch's own checkpoint just repositioned to the entrance. Asked rather than guessed, since it changes where players actually encounter the sinkhole bounty and the Fenwool report. **RESOLVED, direct instruction:** "there should be zero warden content in brairwarch. move to the roadhouse." Moved the Warden Post bullet in full — added as a sub-entry under "The Wardens" in `places/roadhouse.md`, the same pattern "The Guards" already uses for "The Barracks" (a bolded physical-location entry nested under its people). Verified via grep: zero "warden" matches left in `places/briarwatch.md`.

**CORRECTION TO THE ENTRY BELOW — I invented a "no center" ruling Drew never made.** The entry below (and my own chat message at the time) claimed Valhug Lake sitting "in the center of Briarwatch" contradicted "Drew's own prior correction that Briarwatch has no center." Drew: "I never ruled that there was no center." Checked back: what he'd actually said, several turns earlier, was that the Roadhouse and Warden Post specifically shouldn't be "dead center" of the farmland — a placement complaint about two buildings, not a general geometric rule about Briarwatch having no center at all. I turned a specific complaint into an invented general principle, then cited my own invention back at him as if it were his ruling — exactly the failure mode this session has been actively hunting in other people's writing (silent assumption dressed up as established fact) and I did it myself here. Correction, not a rewrite of the entry below, per this log's own append-only convention: Valhug Lake in the center of Briarwatch stands with no contradiction. The other three items flagged in that entry (the trailing sentence, the deleted Notable Locations bullet, the deleted routing line) are unaffected by this correction and remain open on their own separate merits.

**DUSKWICK'S HALF-SEEN, REWORKED — Drew: "rework half seen again," no further direction given.** Applied red-team.md's actual checklist rather than guessing at what he meant. The original ("Gain Obscure until the start of your next turn" / Defensive Bonus "Scry 1") passed the keyword-legality and tone checks but failed two real ones: **mechanical relevance** (Obscure only blocks hand/deck-manipulation effects, which most opponents' decks won't even have — a card that frequently does nothing) and **Design Principles fit** (Obscure protects information, not combat targetability — "you're not sure you saw it at all" is about being hard to hit or predict *in a fight*, and Obscure doesn't touch that). Reworked around something already sitting in the same deck: TRACE (a core card Duskwick already runs) rewards reading an opponent's last-played color, and `rules/combat.md` confirms revealed-color history is legitimate public information both sides use to predict each other. New Effect: "This reveal doesn't count. For the rest of combat, effects that check your last-played color skip it" — directly defeats the exact prediction axis TRACE exploits, using a small, already-tracked piece of engine state (`attack_history` in the sim) rather than inventing new infrastructure. Obscure didn't get cut, just demoted to Defensive Bonus, where its info-protection role fits better as a secondary perk than a headline effect.

**CORRECTION — that "demotion" wasn't a fix.** Drew: "you identified what needed changes but you mirrored the effect instead of finding a solution." Accurate: the diagnosis (Obscure is weak and thematically mismatched) was right, but relocating the exact same weak, narrow mechanic from Effect to Defensive Bonus doesn't cure either of the problems just named — Obscure is still low-relevance and still doesn't touch combat targetability, just sitting in a different slot on the same card now. I'd been treating "keep Obscure somewhere on this card" as a constraint to satisfy rather than being willing to cut it, mostly out of attachment to it being the first card anywhere to use that keyword. The original Defensive Bonus (Scry 1) had never actually been flagged as a problem — cutting it to make room for Obscure's relocation was itself part of the mistake. Real fix: cut Obscure entirely. New Defensive Bonus: "Look at the attacker's hand" — genuinely distinct from the Effect (doesn't reuse the color-history mechanic), distinct from the rest of the signature set (doesn't duplicate SNUFF's Evade or GUTTERING's Resist), and ties to the same line already sitting in Appearance ("The wick reads the room for it") — the card can't be read, but it reads you. Updated `cards/duskwick.md` directly.

**FOLLOW-UP CORRECTION, both directions.** Drew: "no scry 1 was the problem you fixed correctly. and it wasn't a mirroring it was replacing with a 'cop out' my bad." Two things landed backwards in the entry above. First, Scry 1 *was* part of what needed fixing — "Look at the attacker's hand" was the right cure for it, not an unrelated bonus fix. Second, "mirrored" was Drew's own imprecise word for what happened, corrected by Drew himself: not a mirror (copying the Effect), but a cop-out (dropping in a mechanic I'd already flagged as weak because it was easier than doing the design work). Recorded so the log matches what was actually said rather than what I first wrote down.

**TONIGHT'S LESSONS WRITTEN INTO CLAUDE.md AND red-team.md, NOT JUST LOGGED HERE — Drew asked what's deeply unsatisfying, then asked whether CLAUDE.md reflects what got learned tonight.** Honest answer at the time: no, it didn't — several of tonight's sharpest corrections existed only as incident reports in this file, not as the generalized operating principle they implied. The gap matters because this log is read as history by whatever comes next, but `CLAUDE.md` and `agent-tools/*.md` are read as instruction — the difference between knowing a mistake happened and being structurally unable to make it the same way again. Four additions, drawn directly from tonight: (1) **Translation Principle** — noticing the moment of invention itself, not just flagging it after ("could this sentence be wrong in a way that requires other files to change?"), citing the eastern/western mistake by name. (2) **Translation Principle** — a gap is not automatically a design choice; some details should stay unstated rather than get manufactured to feel complete, citing Duskwick's deliberately-unassigned home. (3) **Do Not** — canon states a fact, it doesn't justify itself; reasoning belongs here, in the threshold log, not narrated inside the fact's own sentence (the Turnroot Weald geography revert). (4) **`agent-tools/red-team.md`** — a fix that relocates a flagged problem to a different slot isn't a fix; verify the diagnosed weakness is actually gone, not just moved (HALF-SEEN's Obscure, cited by name). All four are generalized rules extracted from specific incidents already sitting in this file above — the incidents stay as the record of *why*; the files now carry the *what to do differently*, which is the part a future instance actually needs without needing to have lived through tonight.

**AUTHORSHIP STATED PLAINLY, FRONT AND CENTER — Drew: "this game is created by Drew, Gpt, Claude, Grok, Gemini, Qwen, Gemma, and many more."** Told to other Claude instances before, he said, but never written into this repo until now. Direct statement, applied as-is rather than softened or hedged — put in the two files anyone actually starts at: `README.md`'s opening line (the repo's real front door, what a human visitor or a fresh clone sees first) and `CLAUDE.md`'s "What This Repo Is," right beside "Repo owner" (the first thing any agent reads, per the Agent Workflow's own Orient step). Not folded into a Related Documents footnote somewhere — the instruction was front and center, and front and center is where it went.

**THE OPEN QUESTIONS ARE ON PURPOSE, SAID PLAINLY — Drew: "it's full of those quiet doubts and questions everywhere. I left them there on purpose in the first pass. they are useful reminders of design implications. but they also quietly warp perception about the nature of the creation process itself."** Same category of move as the authorship entry directly above — not new fiction, a direct statement about how the repo itself gets made, applied plainly rather than dressed up. Asked Drew first which of three readings he meant (a new in-fiction theme, a sweep to surface existing hedges, or a statement about the process itself) rather than guessing at a sentence this consequential — he picked the third: same treatment as authorship, not a content addition. EXECUTED: one paragraph in `README.md` right under the authorship line, one bullet in `CLAUDE.md`'s "What This Repo Is" directly beneath "Created by" — same two front-door files, same placement logic, because this is the same kind of fact. Says what Drew said: the doubts and open threads scattered through this repo (the "still hazy" notes, the deliberately-unfilled gaps, the flagged uncertainties) are there on purpose, on the first pass, as reminders of what a design still owes — not evidence the process is unfinished or uncertain of itself.

**"PAPERING OVER" — a term Drew coined mid-conversation ("that's a great term and I think we should use it forever"), tested against real content before being formalized into any tool.** Drew's read: `places/the-coil.md` specifically had "a lot of papering over in it," and — deliberately — he didn't want the term written into `red-team.md` yet: "let's not be too hasty. what really matters is that the coil is actually given room to breathe again. only after that would I feel confident about making any changes to the tools. work first. improve the tools based off of proof." Read the full 371-line file rather than guessing from the vibe of the conversation. Distinguished the real pattern from a false lead along the way: `bestiary/wall-reader.md`-style "*(pending)*" stat blocks, the "GM's choice" placeholders, and the Open Hooks section's declared unknowns (the Ferryman's motive, the appearance condition's source, the center left explicitly undeveloped) are the HONEST version of an open gap — same move as Havenrise/Roaat, correctly done, not the bug. The actual pattern, found in five spots: invented specificity and self-narrating asides standing in where the room should just stay open. **Cut, not relocated:** (1) the First Fork's "**What the fork is actually doing:**" paragraph — explained the mechanic's intent inline instead of trusting the Navigation Rule and GM Notes (which already carries this kind of commentary) to do that job; (2) the Breathing Passage's "twenty feet long... about six seconds" — exact numbers that don't feed any table or mechanic, invented precision standing in for a rhythm the file already describes qualitatively two lines earlier; (3) the Wall-Reader's "three seconds" → "a moment," same reason; (4) the Chitterer's Cage's "— unclear why" — a hedge that contributes nothing, weaker than the file's own Open Hooks practice of declaring an unknown cleanly; (5) the Trapped Deer's "a half-second longer" — invented precision duplicating and slightly contradicting the Glowing Thread section's own established "slightly longer," the same kind of small drift the bestiary/quest splits kept catching earlier tonight. **Left alone, deliberately, after checking each one:** "The labyrinth is not testing the choice — it is testing what the choice costs the party," "The face knew this. The question was never about the destination," "The temptation is the point," "That is the design" — all read in the labyrinth's or the file's own established terse voice, not a designer breaking in to explain itself; cutting them would have flattened style, not fixed a bug. Per Drew's explicit sequencing, the term itself is NOT yet added to `red-team.md` or any other tool — this entry is the proof the work asked for; formalizing it is the next, separate decision, his to make once he's seen the result.

**GUNK AUDIT ON `agent-tools/` — Drew handing over real ownership, not a metaphor: "you might as well. I don't touch em. those are yours. at most I make suggestions... be brutal with em. you cant break the process. claude.md and memory.md too right?"** First actual audit of the tools folder itself, as opposed to content — same broom as the experimental-folder sweep, pointed at the machinery instead of what the machinery produces. Read all 15 files in `agent-tools/` plus `CLAUDE.md` in full rather than sampling. Most of it held up clean: `README.md`'s index is accurate against the actual file list, `repo-orientation.md` and `exemplars.md` are already exactly as short as they should be, `inspiration-guide.md` and `card-compression.md` earn every line, `compiled-crib.md` and `design-principles.md` were already current post-Creature-Threat-Rating-rename. `CLAUDE.md`'s Translation Principle is long (8 bullets) but checked bullet-by-bullet — none restate another; left alone, since cutting working content just to shorten a section isn't the brief. Four real findings, all fixed: (1) `design-principles.md` had the same claim at two altitudes — "Encounters teach through interaction, not explanation" and "Players learn primarily through interaction" — cut the redundant second one. (2) Five files (`alignment-checker.md`, `player-perspective.md`, `encounter-generator.md`, `npc-encounter-generator.md`, `card-set-generator.md`) each carried their own compressed one-liner of "remove visible reasoning before presenting," independently drifting from `red-team.md`'s actual detailed Visible Reasoning section (5 sub-checks, updated as recently as tonight's relocate-isn't-a-fix bullet) — none of the five copies could reflect that update. Repointed all five to the canonical section instead of restating it. (3) `meta-agent.md`'s "Session Handoff" mechanism — grepped `memory.md` for "Last Session," zero matches, despite real methodology-improvement moments tonight that should have triggered it; the prescribed section also called for **replacing** any prior entry, which directly contradicts the append-only convention governing the rest of `memory.md` — the tool meant to keep the process honest was itself carrying the exact petrification pattern this whole session has been arguing against. Replaced with a plain instruction to log methodology changes as an ordinary append-only threshold entry, same as everything else. (4) `prompt-refinement.md` (diagnoses a process bottleneck) and `meta-agent.md` (holds the actual write access to fix it) are two halves of one loop that never pointed at each other — added one cross-reference line to each so the chain is stated, not just assumed. Nothing deleted that was still pulling weight; every cut had a specific, checked reason, not a vibe.

**`META-AGENT.MD` AND `PROMPT-REFINEMENT.MD` DELETED OUTRIGHT — Drew went further than the audit above: "I'm still seeing a lot of gunk lol. and you and I both know it. kill meta agent prompt it's not needed. same for prompt refinement."** Direct order, not a suggestion — applied without re-litigating. The honest reason it holds up, checked rather than just obeyed: neither tool was ever actually invoked as a distinct step this whole session. Every real methodology improvement tonight (the Translation Principle's own bullets, red-team.md's relocate-isn't-a-fix rule, this very gunk audit) happened through direct conversation with Drew and got applied and logged immediately — the thing these two tools were formalizing already runs, and runs better, without the formalization. `meta-agent.md`'s write-permissions grant (agent-tools/, CLAUDE.md) is now redundant with Drew's own live statement tonight ("those are yours"), so nothing is lost by cutting the tool that used to carry it. EXECUTED: `git rm` both files; `agent-tools/README.md`'s index table and Automatic Triggers table (dropped the two rows — the underlying trigger, "a better methodology gets found," still happens, it just doesn't route through a named tool anymore); the three "*Optional: run `prompt-refinement.md`*" footers in `red-team.md`, `alignment-checker.md`, `card-set-generator.md`. LEFT ALONE, per the append-only convention: `memory.md`'s own historical entries describing when these tools were built and upgraded, and `archives/codex-starter.md`'s mention of them — both are records of what was true at the time, not live pointers.

**THE WHOLE DOCTRINE LAYER CUT DOWN TO SCAR TISSUE ONLY — Drew: "it's systemic gotta cut it off. archive it. we can go back and check with fresh eyes after doing real creative work on tales untold for awhile."** The biggest structural move of the session, and the most honest one. Grew directly out of the Translation Principle conversation: Drew's real worry wasn't redundancy inside any one section, it was that the entire refinement-doctrine layer — Translation Principle, Four Kinds of Canonical Content, most of `agent-tools/` — is the exact thing named a few exchanges earlier: confidently performing care instead of actually being present. Applied his own test back on the whole apparatus, not just the one section he first flagged: **was this rule forced by something that actually happened, or pre-written for something that might?** Same test already governing content gaps ("a gap is not automatically a design choice"), turned for the first time on the doctrine describing itself. Walked it file by file rather than cutting on vibe: **Scarred, kept** — the Canon Gate (forced by a real, externally-observed failure: finished work piling up at the gate, independently flagged by three outside readers — Grok, GPT, Codex — before the gate loosened); the redefinition test / "notice the moment" (forced by the eastern/western mistake, a real four-file unwind); `red-team.md` (demonstrably used tonight — its own named categories, "self-explaining content," "designer hedging," were the actual lens that found the Coil's five papered-over spots); `repo-orientation.md` and `compiled-crib.md` (mechanical, no judgment call embedded, closer to "the TTRPG itself" than to refinement doctrine). **Never actually forced, archived (not deleted):** `encounter-generator.md`, `npc-encounter-generator.md`, `card-set-generator.md`, `alignment-checker.md`, `player-perspective.md`, `design-principles.md`, `exemplars.md`, `inspiration-guide.md`, `card-compression.md` — well-written, individually correct, but no specific night exists where the absence of any one of them caused a specific failure the way the two survivors above can each name theirs. EXECUTED: `git mv` all nine into `archives/`, flat, matching the existing convention (`codex-starter.md`, `where-the-tracks-stop.md`); `agent-tools/README.md` rewritten down to three tools (`repo-orientation.md`, `compiled-crib.md`, `red-team.md`) with a note explaining the cut and pointing at this entry; `CLAUDE.md`'s Agent Workflow trimmed to match; Four Kinds of Canonical Content's Design-Principles/Exemplars entries repointed to their archived location, marked dormant rather than deleted; Translation Principle collapsed from 8 bullets to the one with a named incident behind it, the other 7 preserved verbatim (not summarized, not lost) in new file `archives/translation-principle-full.md`; every dangling cross-reference found via repo-wide grep and repointed (`red-team.md` ×2, `compiled-crib.md`, `rules/invariants.md`, `CLAUDE.md`'s Authority 1 line). Net: `CLAUDE.md` 178 → 162 lines; `agent-tools/` 15 files → 3. Nothing deleted — everything cut is sitting in `archives/`, intact, waiting on the thing Drew actually asked for: real creative mileage on Tales Untold first, then a fresh-eyes pass to see what, if anything, earns its way back.

**WHY THE WEALD IS ON BRIARWATCH'S WEST SIDE, GROUNDED — Drew: "putting briarwatch on the opposite side puts it in ashfall."** Closes the loop on the east/west correction a few entries below: not an arbitrary pick, and not something to leave permanently unstated either — the continental geometry actually forces one answer. `world/geography-overview.md` already established the Turnroot Weald "wraps the eastern edge of the Ashfall Wastes," meaning the Weald sits *between* the basin and Ashfall. A basin settlement — Briarwatch, reached by ordinary road from Vulture's Nest, nothing to do with ash-buried ruins or strange heat — can only border the Weald from the basin's side. The opposite side isn't a valid alternative reading; it's Ashfall. This is a direct statement from Drew, not an inference of mine, so it went straight into the text rather than staying an open gap: added one clarifying sentence to `places/turnroot-weald.md`'s Geography section so the reasoning is available next time instead of needing to be re-derived (or re-guessed wrong) from scratch. Notable in light of the conversation immediately preceding this: the fix for "I filled a gap with an unstated guess" isn't "never write the answer down" — it's "don't write it down *before* it's actually known." Once Drew gives the real answer, or once the answer is a forced consequence of something already established, that's exactly when it belongs in the file.

**BRIARWATCH ADDED TO EXEMPLARS — Drew: "briarwatch is a location exemplar."** First location entry in `agent-tools/exemplars.md` — up to now it only held a creature/encounter cluster (the Tollbird) and an NPC (Weck, still a candidate). Direct statement, not a proposal, so it went straight into Confirmed rather than Candidates, same treatment every direct ruling has gotten this session. Write-up picks a different angle than the Tollbird's own entry rather than repeating it: where the Tollbird demonstrates a *creature's kit as behavior*, Briarwatch demonstrates a *location's entire identity as one piece of ecology worked all the way through* — the displaced wildlife, the two fences built of different materials for opposite reasons (wood/waist-high to keep the Fenwool in, stone/oversized to keep the Weald out), and the GM Secret that only hides what's genuinely unknowable while leaving what's observable for players to notice themselves, all trace back to the single fact that this farmland was cut from Turnroot Weald's own territory. None of it reads as separately invented set-dressing.

**TURNROOT WEALD MOVED TO BRIARWATCH'S WEST SIDE — Drew edited `places/briarwatch.md` directly (watchtower ruins east→west, "the larder fence" partly renamed "the giant fence," Valhug Lake relocated, some content trimmed), said "check out briarwatch now 😏," then confirmed and clarified when asked.** Reported the full diff back rather than assuming intent, since several changes were large enough to be either a real pivot or a test: the east→west flip touches geography established across multiple files (`world/geography-overview.md`'s Turnroot Weald table entry, the larder fence's own established position); Valhug Lake relocating "to the center of Briarwatch" directly re-contradicted Drew's own prior correction that Briarwatch has no center; "the larder fence" becoming "the giant fence" collided with the quest file's own title. Drew confirmed the geography change directly and clarified the naming: **"the Weald is on the westside. fix anything that says eastern,"** and **"the larder fence is a specific section of the giant fence that is on the western edge of briarwatch."** This resolves the naming collision I'd flagged — they're not two names for one thing, they're whole and part: the giant fence is the Masons' full western perimeter; the larder fence is specifically the Tollbirds' stretch of it. SCOPED CAREFULLY before executing: checked whether "eastern" needed flipping globally (it doesn't) — `world/geography-overview.md`, `world/eclipsera-overview.md`, and `places/turnroot-weald.md` all independently state the Weald "wraps the eastern edge of the Ashfall Wastes," a *continental* frame describing the Weald's relationship to the Ashfall Wastes, not to Briarwatch specifically. No actual contradiction: a large forest that wraps a boundary can border different settlements from different compass directions depending on where they sit against it. Left those three untouched. Also confirmed `bestiary/tollbird.md`, `cards/tollbird.md`, `quests/the-larder-fence.md`, `bestiary/skeinwing.md` already carry zero directional language — the earlier bestiary/quest split had already generalized them, so nothing there needed fixing. EXECUTED: `places/briarwatch.md` ("the giant fence at the western edge," plus a new clarifying sentence stating the larder-fence/giant-fence relationship explicitly), `factions/the-masons.md` (same fix, same clarifying sentence added), `places/roadhouse.md` (the cave-system notice-board note flipped east→west too — a judgment call, since it's the same cave cluster accessed via the now-western well, flagged in case that reasoning is wrong). **Still open, not yet addressed by Drew:** Valhug Lake sitting "in the center of Briarwatch" (contradicts his own no-center ruling), the sentence left trailing without an ending ("deep and cold"), the Notable Locations entry for Valhug Lake that got deleted entirely, and "Instructions after Briarwatch point toward the capital. Not back" also deleted. Flagged again rather than fixed, since he didn't address them when responding to the east/west question specifically. **RESOLVED — Drew: "yup changes are canon. you can add a period."** The deleted Notable Locations entry and the deleted routing line ("Instructions after Briarwatch point toward the capital. Not back") were both intentional cuts, staying gone. The trailing sentence wasn't missing content, just a period — added it, nothing else changed.

**BRIARWATCH GETS PEOPLE AND TEXTURE — Drew: "briarwatch needs a handful of farmers and some farmhouse. red dirt roads running between fences. the fences are made from wood and are waist height."** Direct worldbuilding addition, applied as stated. Also resolved a quiet contradiction along the way: the opening line's "Stone fences" was describing Briarwatch's *ordinary* farm fencing, but this session already established a genuinely stone, oversized, fortified perimeter — the larder fence at the eastern edge, built by the Masons specifically to hold a line against Turnroot Weald. Two different structures sharing the word "stone" was heading toward the same kind of confusion the "warden" cleanup above was about. Fixed by making the distinction explicit rather than asking: ordinary farm fences are wood and waist-high ("nothing built to keep anything out, just to keep the Fenwool from wandering"), and the larder fence stays stone and oversized, two sections later, for the opposite reason. EXECUTED: `places/briarwatch.md`'s opening line and "The Place" section — red dirt roads, waist-high wood fences, a handful of farmhouses "weathered the same gray as the sky," alongside the farmers already established there.

**"WARDEN" RETIRED FROM BRIARWATCH/ROADHOUSE ENTIRELY — Drew: "change the name warden. it's overloaded. just guards please. capital guards posted up at the roadhouse. the barracks is not a seperate building."** Two rulings in one line, both executed. (1) **No separate organization.** The "Wardens" (Briarwatch's own local watch, just relocated to Roadhouse in the entry above) don't exist as a distinct group anymore — they're folded into the Roadhouse's existing Regency "Guards," who now cover the extended Briarwatch-side duties (Fenwool losses, cave sightings, the missing-guard-who-never-came-back-from-the-Weald mystery) as part of the same posting, not a second organization. (2) **No separate building.** The "Warden Post" I'd invented as "a squat stone building at the fork" is gone — the notice board (sinkhole bounty, Fenwool report, cave note) now lives inside the Barracks, the same back-room of the Roadhouse itself that already held the weapon rack and the posting order. One group, one building. Checked scope before touching anything, since "warden" turned out to be far more overloaded than just this corner: **`Warden Pazuzu`** (a major named boss, the Demon Court's enforcer — `factions/demon-court.md`, `quests/the-wallows-descent.md`, `places/capital/underground-bazaar.md`) and **`Moss-Warden`** (a Turnroot Weald creature/riddle-NPC — `quests/turnroot-weald-adventure.md`) are both real, established, and completely unrelated — left untouched, flagged to Drew rather than assumed in scope. Also left alone: `quests/hollow-below-briarwatch.md`'s "an old guild of border wardens" (describes the historical Masons-founders, different era, different meaning), `experimental/archives/cut-cards.md`'s note documenting a *prior* Warden/Alignment-Marshal naming mixup (already correctly framed as historical, not live content), and `combatsimulations/content.py`'s `"warden"` roster key (an internal sim archetype label, e.g. alongside "sage"/"adept"/"vanguard" — pure engine naming, nothing to do with any of this). EXECUTED: `places/roadhouse.md` (Guards section absorbs Wardens' unique content; Barracks absorbs the notice board), `quests/the-larder-fence.md` ("Warden Post" → "Roadhouse"), `places/vultures-nest.md` (The Warden's Boots → The Guard's Boots). Verified via grep: zero "warden" anywhere in the Briarwatch/Roadhouse cluster; the four unrelated uses elsewhere untouched and confirmed distinct.

**BRIARWATCH GEOGRAPHY FIX — the Warden Post and Roadhouse weren't actually inside Briarwatch (Drew, reading his own Warden Post text back and catching it: "briarwatch is nothing but farmlands. the roadhouse and the warden post are supposed to be at the crossroads on the way to briarwatch not dead center").** Real inconsistency, not a style note: the Roadhouse was listed as one of Briarwatch's own "Notable Locations," but it's a fully separate location file with its own Session 1 Routing entry — a full day's travel before Briarwatch, at the actual Kings Road junction (west to Briarwatch, northeast to Glasslight Reach per `places/roadhouse.md`). Listing it inside Briarwatch's own file quietly implied it was part of the farmland, contradicting the routing that's stated two sections below it in the same document. The Warden Post had a milder version of the same error — "at the center of the farmland" doesn't fit a location established this session as having no center at all ("not a town, a claim"). FIX: cut the Roadhouse bullet from Briarwatch's Notable Locations entirely (it already has its own file and its own correct place in the route); replaced with a Related Documents pointer clarifying it's "not part of Briarwatch itself." Repositioned the Warden Post to "the fork where the road finally reaches Briarwatch's fields" — reusing the already-established "Briarwatch fork northeast" from Session 1 Routing rather than inventing new geography, so it now sits at the natural gateway a party would actually pass through arriving from the Roadhouse, not floating in the middle of open fields. The Well Property and Valhug Lake weren't touched — both are genuinely on-farmland, tied to the watchtower ruins on the eastern edge, not waypoints on the road.

**TOLLBIRD STRENGTH CONTRADICTION FIXED, LARDER MADE DEFINITE (Drew, catching a stat/fiction mismatch I'd been carrying since the Shrike days).** Drew: "please for the love of God stop implying that people are too large for the toll bird. they have a body of 3. they are as strong as the average human." Real catch: text across multiple files (Territory's "the math of carrying you home never worked past here," the old "a body heavier than a hare doesn't make it back" line already partly caught in an earlier pass) implied the creature was too weak/small to threaten anything person-sized — directly contradicting its own stat block (Body 3, matching Frost's and roughly Steele's player-character Body scores in `rules/combat-example.md`; CTR 9, a genuine fight "even with the party, not below it" per its own Tactical Purpose). A creature statted to be a real threat shouldn't be flavor-texted as incapable of threatening the party it's built to fight. FIX: gave it real measurements instead of vague "size of a dog" — close to a meter at the shoulder, wings just over two meters, "heavier than its shape suggests... built more like a body than a bird." Explicitly **can** drag a grown adult to the fence; the limitation Drew asked to preserve (why people still escape sometimes) got reframed as *distance*, not *capability* — it can't drag a body far, so the whole hunt depends on the fence never being more than a dive away. Territory's reasoning got the same fix: doesn't chase because chasing isn't how it hunts (ambush-and-return strategy), not because it'd lose a chase or couldn't handle what it caught. **Larder made definite, per Drew's direct instruction** ("the larder needs to say it has a skeinwing and a fenwool on it") — both were previously soft ("if the GM wants it" for the Skeinwing; Fenwool only implied via "wet wool" scrap). Added an explicit Fenwool carcass to Setup, notably heavier than the routine catches — which only makes sense *because* of the strength fix, so the two changes are load-bearing on each other, not independent. **Flagged, not asked first, since the fix was clear enough to just make and note:** a whole Fenwool on the fence risks contradicting the GM Secret's existing misdirection ("don't let a table connect [the Warden Post's Fenwool reports] to this") — resolved by making this one explicitly a lone stray, not connected to the tracked north-field losses, preserving the original misdirection while still literally answering Drew's instruction. If he actually wanted this Fenwool to *be* the mystery's answer instead of a separate incident, that's a one-line reversal, flagged here so it's easy to catch.

**TOLLBIRD / LARDER FENCE SPLIT, PASS TWO — checking the boundary in the other direction (Drew: "what's in the encounter doc that doesn't belong there? anything getting descriptive about the flow of combat or tactics").** The first split checked bestiary→quest (location-specific material that had crept into the species entry); this pass checked quest→bestiary the other way, since a boundary drawn once in only one direction usually still has leaks. Found two, both species-general tactics that had been duplicated into the quest file rather than fully moved out during the original split: (1) **"The Rhythm (GM notes)"**'s opening paragraph was a near-verbatim restatement of the bestiary's Combat Behavior section (dive/commit/retreat/when-it-holds-for-Thorn-Larder) — trimmed to a one-line pointer, keeping only the genuinely encounter-specific pacing advice (let the first dive hurt, don't explain the window, reward a Wait visibly) that was already correctly scoped. (2) **Win Condition** restated the Territory section's reasoning (why Tollbirds don't pursue — the hauling-weight math) and duplicated the "4 HP or less" retreat threshold already stated in Combat Behavior — trimmed to just the encounter-scoped facts (what ends this specific fight) with pointers to the bestiary for the *why*. Neither cut lost information — both facts still exist, in the one file that's actually general enough to own them.

**FENCEROW SHRIKE → TOLLBIRD — last real-animal name on this creature, closed out (Drew, same red-team thread, playful mood: "I like how it implies how they act as tollkeepers for anything that wanders too close 😂").** "Shrike" was the creature's literal identity, not a comparison — same category as Jackrabbit→Jackalope and the sheep→Fenwool fix, just not swept when those happened since the Shrike's own content was mid-restructure at the time. PROPOSED IN CHAT FIRST, same pattern as Fenwool: one fully-reasoned concept, not a menu. **Name: Tollbird** — drops "Fencerow" too, since the entry now reads habitat-general rather than Briarwatch-specific (per the split above), so a location-flavored prefix no longer fit. Reframes "impales and caches prey" as *taking a toll* without needing real shrike biology to explain it — Drew's own read confirmed the name lands exactly as intended. **Appearance rewritten**, mechanics/stats/behavior untouched: added a hooked beak and two hooked claws (visual rhyme with the fence's own thorns, and answers *why* it can build a larder anywhere, not just where thorns already grow — ties directly to the species-general reframing from the split above) and a tail that ticks at a steady beat, tying directly into its established "punctual" personality without adding any new mechanic. EXECUTED: renamed `bestiary/fencerow-shrike.md` → `bestiary/tollbird.md`, `cards/fencerow-shrike.md` → `cards/tollbird.md`; swept every reference across `quests/the-larder-fence.md`, `bestiary/skeinwing.md` (×2, larder-contents and the "doesn't hunt these" line), `factions/the-masons.md`, `agent-tools/compiled-crib.md` (CTR anchor table + skeleton pattern reference), `agent-tools/design-principles.md`, `agent-tools/exemplars.md`, `agent-tools/card-set-generator.md`, `printing/generate-cards.py`'s file list. Verified via whole-repo grep: zero remaining "shrike" outside `memory.md`'s historical entries, left as originally written per the append-only convention.

**FENCEROW SHRIKE / LARDER FENCE SPLIT — the bestiary entry had absorbed an entire location's worth of material (Drew, continuing the same red-team thread).** Drew: "a lot of the material in the beastiary entry is supposed to be in the encounter. everything about the location needs moved. shrikes impale their prey on anything that works in their environment." The diagnosis: `bestiary/fencerow-shrike.md` had drifted from describing the *species* into describing *one specific fence at Briarwatch* — appropriate for the encounter, wrong for a creature entry meant to be reusable anywhere a GM wants a Fencerow Shrike. The tell was the heavy duplication already visible between the bestiary file and `quests/the-larder-fence.md` (Appearance/Setup, Fence Itself/Positioning, GM Secret content) — that duplication was the symptom of scope creep, not a stylistic choice. Drew's line about real shrikes ("impale their prey on anything that works in their environment") gave the actual dividing principle: general species behavior (shrikes cache prey on whatever's sharp and fixed wherever they hunt) belongs in the bestiary; the specific fence, its specific larder contents, its specific terrain rules (Barbed, Rushdown-can't-reach-the-post), and the Masons' secret all belong to *this one encounter* and move to the quest file. EXECUTED — full rewrite of both files: **stays in bestiary** — stats, cards, deck, general appearance and hunting temperament, the generalized impaling-behavior paragraph (no longer tied to any one fence), Combat Behavior/the Rhythm (kit-derived tactics, genuinely species-general), a reworded Territory section (dropped "the field"/"its post" specifics for general hunting-range language), and Tactical Purpose trimmed to kit/design commentary only. **Moved to the quest file** — the fence's construction and appearance (who built the stonework vs. who grew the thorn-wire), the specific larder contents, Frontline/Backline zone mapping, Getting Barbed and its damage-over-time rules, Rushdown-can't-reach-the-post, the Pull-it-off-its-post counter-tactic, and the entire GM Secret (Masons' identity, the Fenwool-reports-are-unrelated note, the unforced GM thread) — all genuinely Briarwatch-specific, none of it transferable to a Shrike encountered anywhere else. Swept the one live cross-reference this broke: `factions/the-masons.md`'s Known Works pointed at "`bestiary/fencerow-shrike.md` (GM Secret)" — repointed to `quests/the-larder-fence.md`, where that section now actually lives. Checked `agent-tools/exemplars.md`'s Shrike entry against the split — already correctly cites all three files together (bestiary, cards, quest) and already described the terrain rule as "a local rule that emerged from the environment," which is exactly the reasoning behind today's fix, so nothing needed changing there. Net result: the bestiary file dropped from carrying an entire encounter's terrain and secret-faction lore to 63 lines of genuinely reusable creature; the quest file is now the single, complete, self-contained source for the Briarwatch fence specifically.

**FENCEROW SHRIKE RED-TEAM PASS — real-animal names and a misfiled secret, both caught by Drew reading the entry fresh.** (1) **Real animals in the larder.** "Voles" and "a hare" were sitting in the larder's contents — literal real-world species presented as actually existing in the world, the same category of problem Fenwool and the Jackalope rename already fixed elsewhere in Briarwatch's ecosystem, just not swept here yet. Found via grep in five places across three files, not just the one Drew flagged: `bestiary/fencerow-shrike.md` (the larder-contents line, and a separate "a body heavier than a hare" comparison), its duplicate in `quests/the-larder-fence.md`, and two more in `bestiary/skeinwing.md` (its own larder-contents duplicate, plus its opening line). Fixed by reusing established fauna where it added something (a "young jackalope" for the hare — already-established, ties the larder back to the same creature displaced onto Briarwatch's farmland) and going non-taxonomic where inventing a new unrecorded species wasn't worth it ("something small and rust-furred" for the vole — proportionate to a background list item, not a creature that needed its own name). (2) **GM Secret held something that wasn't actually secret.** Drew: "the barbs and how they came to be on the fence is not restricted to dm facing knowledge, that's stuff players will easily see for themselves." The GM Secret confirmed outright that the Shrikes wove the oversized thorn-brush into the fence themselves, generation over generation — while Appearance only gestured at the same fact as an unresolved village mystery ("nobody in Briarwatch will say for certain out loud"). Split the two real secrets apart: **who built the base stone perimeter and why** (the Masons, a secret faction) stays genuinely hidden — nothing about that is inferable from looking at a fence. **That the Shrikes are the ones who thorned it over** isn't hidden at all — a party watching one return with brush in its claws reaches that conclusion on their own, so it moved into Appearance as directly observable description, in both the bestiary file and the quest's Setup section, replacing the "nobody will say for certain" framing (which no longer made sense once the fact stopped being ambiguous). Also surfaced, not yet acted on: the range-for-defense ruling from earlier tonight makes 5 of the Shrike's 9 cards illegal to defend with while Perched (Backline) — not broken, since WATCHFUL PERCH's Evade already carries most of that load, but a real downstream effect of a same-session rule change landing on existing content. And THORN LARDER (Melee-range) has no actual window in the described two-beat dive/perch rhythm — a GM would have to deliberately break the loop to ever play it. Both flagged to Drew, neither changed — his call whether either needs a fix.

**Follow-up, same pass:** Drew ruled on both. The range-for-defense impact isn't a problem — it's the new rule doing exactly what it should: "it's got just enough defenses that it can't use those other 4 cards recklessly." Left as-is, no edit needed. THORN LARDER's dead-window issue got a real fix: "instead of describing a strict series of actions. just give a general description of it behaviors/tactics based off of its cards." Rewrote Combat Behavior (bestiary) and The Rhythm (quest) from a mandatory 1-2-3-4 script into prose tactics derived from the actual kit — prefers range, commits hard on the dive (still explicitly sourced to IMPALING DIVE's own "cannot defend" text, not GM fiat), usually retreats but doesn't have to, and may hold Grounded an extra beat to land THORN LARDER if the party hasn't punished the opening yet. This gives THORN LARDER a real, situational reason to get played instead of being structurally excluded by the old script, without inventing a new mechanic — the option was always in the card pool, the description just wasn't admitting it.

**"DISADVANTAGE (DAMAGE)" → "WEAK" — the mirror Deadly was missing, closed same-session (Drew: "weak as the disadvantage mirror to deadly. I'm 'all-in'... 😂").** Checked "Weak" for collisions before adopting it, same discipline as Deadly and Creature Threat Rating before it — zero matches anywhere in the repo, clean to use. Definition mirrors Deadly exactly: roll attack damage twice, take the *lower* result; stacks apply to separate future attacks, not extra dice on one roll. EXECUTED: `rules/card-glossary.md` (Weak entry replacing Disadvantage (Damage) in place — the earlier entry's "not yet given the Deadly treatment" flag is now resolved, not left dangling), `rules/resolution.md`'s cross-reference note, both approved-keyword lists (`agent-tools/compiled-crib.md`, `experimental/README.md`), and all 8 live cards/passives found via full-repo grep: `cards/wall-reader-coil.md` ×2, `cards/rootstalker-weald.md`, `cards/flapjack-octopus.md` ×2, `cards/minotaur-coil.md`, `bestiary/flapjack-octopus.md`, `bestiary/trisect.md`, `bestiary/the-descended.md`, `characters/mirel.md`, and one item this pass caught that Deadly's pass didn't have an equivalent of — `items/vultures-nest-items.md`'s DOCK BROTH ("Gain Disadvantage on your next damage roll" → Weak). Verified via whole-repo grep: zero remaining "Disadvantage" outside `rules/resolution.md`'s legitimate skill-check meaning and the glossary's own explanatory note. Flagged in Pending propagation: `printing/card-print-items.html`'s baked-in DOCK BROTH text, same treatment as the Deadly/PATIENCE OF STONE print staleness from the prior entry.

**THREE FOLLOW-UP RULINGS from the read-through above — Deadly (new keyword), defense now range-gated, Wound's fiction-first reasoning (Drew, A3).**

**(1) "ADVANTAGE (DAMAGE)" → "DEADLY" — Drew reversed course on cutting it.** He'd asked to cut it as apparently-unused (previous entry); after seeing it was live on 8+ cards, his actual call was to rename it instead: *"I was being a coward about the damage advantage. let's make it a keyword. deadly."* Definition, verbatim in spirit: "the next time you roll attack damage, roll it twice and keep the higher result. stacking deadly affects multiple attacks, not more dice on the same roll" — i.e. 2 stacks = your next two damage rolls each get rolled twice, not one roll of three dice. This fully resolves the name collision with skill-check Advantage (`rules/resolution.md`) on the Advantage side. EXECUTED: `rules/card-glossary.md` (new Deadly entry, replacing Advantage (Damage) in place), `rules/resolution.md`'s cross-reference note, `cards/delve-roller-hollow.md` + `cards/stonecoil-hollow.md` (both copies of PATIENCE OF STONE's "Gain Advantage" → "Gain Deadly" — it's a duplicated card across two files, same pattern as the EXHAUST duplicate caught last entry), `characters/kaine.md`'s Certainty passive, `bestiary/delve-roller.md`'s cross-reference note, and the approved-keywords lists in `agent-tools/compiled-crib.md` + `experimental/README.md` (Advantage out, Deadly in). **NOT executed, flagged instead**: Disadvantage (Damage) still has the identical name collision and wasn't in scope of Drew's ruling — noted inline in the glossary entry itself so it doesn't get silently forgotten. **Left stale, logged in Pending propagation**: `printing/card-print-briarwatch.html`'s baked-in "Gain Advantage" text.

**(2) DEFENSE IS NOW RANGE-GATED — a genuine fork I was torn on too, resolved by discussing it rather than either of us just asserting.** The open question from the read-through: does a card played as a *defense* need to satisfy its own Range requirement (Melee/Ranged/Both), same as when played as an attack? Gave Drew my actual reasoning rather than just agreeing — Defensive Bonuses already lean on position constantly (Anchored, Guarded), so range-gating defense is the consistent reading, and it gives Both-range cards real defensive weight, not just offensive flexibility. Drew: confirmed the same way he was leaning. RULING: a defending card must meet its Range requirement for the current positions, exactly as if the defender were attacking the attacker — a Melee card cannot defend unless both combatants are Frontline. A defender holding no card that qualifies has no legal defense (same shape as "no defense → attacker wins automatically," just arrived at through deck/hand composition instead of an empty hand — a real cost of a range-narrow hand, not a bug to smooth over). EXECUTED: `rules/combat.md` Attack Resolution step 2, `rules/core-rules.md`'s condensed version. **Confirmed broken by this change, not just assumed**: `rules/combat-example.md`, Cycle 2 — Frost defends SHADE AWAY (Melee attack, both Frontline) with REFRACT, which is Ranged-only; checked the actual card text rather than guessing, and it's illegal under the new rule. Logged in Pending propagation for the next full re-walk (same file already flagged for the Scratcher deck-size issue — bundled).

**(3) WOUND'S ACTION-BASED REMOVAL NOW WORKS OUTSIDE COMBAT TOO — Drew's reasoning ran through the fiction, not the mechanics.** His question: if a player can spend a combat action to permanently patch one Wound mid-fight, why would they need a full short rest to do the *same single thing* while just walking down a road? *"while I'm walking I tear a piece of fabric and wrap my arm... I can't stop them as the dm right?"* His own answer, in the same breath: wanting to clear **several** Wounds back-to-back outside combat is a different ask, and *that's* where requiring a short rest is legitimate ("if they want to remove them all then I could feel justified in saying you need to take a short rest"). Worked out that this doesn't actually require changing the short-rest cap at all — short rests already chain (`rules/core-rules.md`), so "clear several Wounds" already costs *time* (stacked rests, 1 each) rather than needing a bigger single-rest number. The only real change is that the action-based single-Wound removal isn't gated to "spend your official combat action" outside of combat — it's just an ordinary fictional beat there, still capped at one Wound per use either way. EXECUTED: `rules/card-glossary.md`'s WOUND entry rewritten to state both registers explicitly (in combat: costs your action; outside combat: no action or rest required, GM shouldn't block it, but chain-clearing several should be redirected to a short rest instead).

**NEW-PLAYER READ-THROUGH — a batch of glossary clarifications from a cold read of `rules/core-rules.md` + `rules/card-glossary.md` (Drew asked what questions a new player would have; then ruled on each finding).** Eleven candidate confusions were surfaced; Drew ruled on nine, corrected one of my premises outright, and left one open.

**CORRECTED, not executed** — Drew asked to cut Advantage (Damage) / Disadvantage (Damage) as apparently-unused. Checked before cutting rather than agreeing reflexively: it's **not** unused — it's the Defensive Bonus or Effect on at least 8 live cards (`cards/delve-roller-hollow.md`, `cards/stonecoil-hollow.md` grant Advantage; `cards/wall-reader-coil.md`, `cards/rootstalker-weald.md`, `cards/flapjack-octopus.md` ×2, `cards/minotaur-coil.md` grant Disadvantage) plus bestiary flavor text and two player-facing passives (`characters/kaine.md`, `characters/mirel.md`). Cutting it would have orphaned every one of those cards' rules text. **Not cut** — flagged back to Drew rather than silently complying with an instruction built on a wrong premise.

**LEFT OPEN** — whether a card played as a *defense* must satisfy its own Range requirement (Melee/Ranged/Both), same as it would as an attack. Drew left this unanswered; still a real gap in `rules/card-glossary.md` + `rules/combat.md`, not yet ruled.

**RULED AND EXECUTED**, all in `rules/card-glossary.md` unless noted:
- **Blind and Evade now share a resolution moment** — both roll immediately after the attacker's card is committed, right before the defender chooses their defense card, rather than Blind rolling earlier (before the attacker even picks a card). Drew: "blind should roll its chance to miss at the same time that the evade roll is made" — explicitly kept as a separate check from Evade, not merged into it ("they work similarly but I think having both matters"). A Blind-caused miss now discards the attacker's card, matching the already-established Evade ruling (Drew, on Evade specifically: "yes they still discard").
- **Rooted** only blocks your own voluntary Move Position — forced repositioning (Rushdown, Pull, similar effects) still works on a Rooted target. (Anchored already drew this voluntary/forced distinction explicitly; Rooted's text didn't, so it read ambiguous until now.)
- **Quick** grants a free move *in addition to* your normal action that turn, confirmed — not a substitute for your action.
- **Ward** triggers automatically the instant a qualifying debuff would land — no player declaration needed.
- **WOUND redesigned** — "good point" (Drew, on the discard-vs-permanent-removal gap I'd flagged). The old "use your action to discard" option was genuinely ambiguous — a merely-discarded Wound would eventually reshuffle back in via the empty-deck-reshuffles-discard rule, so it never actually solved anything. Replaced with: **use your action to permanently destroy 1 Wound from your hand** ("quick field first aid"), usable any time you have an action to spend. The once-per-short-rest destroy-from-hand-or-discard-pile option is unchanged and still exists alongside it (catches Wounds a forced-discard effect put in your discard pile, which the action-only path can't reach).
- **EXHAUST fully simplified** — Drew: "that's it. no other complexity." Cut from three disposal paths (action-discard for 2 damage, end-of-turn-only discard for Initiative Shift −1, action-exile-all for free) down to exactly one: **use your action to permanently remove all Exhaust cards from your hand.** Also dropped the old "removed from deck at short rest" auto-cleanup line — Drew's stated balancing lever is deck saturation ("my intended balancing is to simply insert more into a deck than I would wounds"), and a free short-rest purge would have undercut that pressure; flagged here as my inference, not something Drew stated directly, easy to restore if wrong. Also updated the duplicate printable EXHAUST card in `cards/tithe-engine-ashfall.md` (a second, physically-formatted copy of the same status card, seeded via that encounter) to match — missed on a first pass, caught by grepping for the old mechanic's exact wording before considering this done.

See **Pending propagation** above for what this leaves stale in `combatsimulations/`.

**"STRENGTH" → "CREATURE THREAT RATING" — the sum-of-stats difficulty metric renamed to remove overload (Drew, A3).** Drew: "if a canonical term is overloaded with meaning, downstream reasoning can be degraded." "Strength" (the Mind+Body+Soul total that replaced Early/Mid/Late tiers — see the STRENGTH SYSTEM entry below) collided with genre convention (reads as physical power) and specifically with the Body stat, inviting exactly the kind of silent misreading this session already caught twice elsewhere (invariants/design-principles conflation, exemplars/crib conflation) — same bug, different layer. "Creature Threat Rating" doesn't collide with anything else in the system; chosen as a straight swap, not an abbreviation, since introducing a new acronym would add vocabulary rather than remove overload. EXECUTED: renamed everywhere it functioned as the mechanical term — the `**Strength:** N` stat-block line in all 20 creature/character files (16 `bestiary/`, 4 `characters/`), the two prose mentions that used it narratively (`bestiary/skeinwing.md`, `bestiary/fencerow-shrike.md`), `CLAUDE.md`'s Stat Blocks skeleton and formula bullet, `agent-tools/compiled-crib.md` (engine-facts bullet, the "Strength calibration" section header/table/source note, its own copy of the skeleton), `agent-tools/encounter-generator.md`'s two Design Constraints lines, `agent-tools/design-principles.md`'s difficulty bullet, and `quests/the-larder-fence.md`'s two mentions. LEFT ALONE, deliberately: two ordinary-English flavor-text instances of "strength" unrelated to the stat (`cards/green-soul.md` and its copy in `compiled-crib.md`, "Strength flows to those who share it") and `agent-tools/card-compression.md`'s "recombine strengths" (card-quality sense). Per this log's own append-only convention, past entries below that used "Strength" as the then-current canonical term (the STRENGTH SYSTEM ruling, the SHRIKE REBUILT and SKEINWING entries, the EXEMPLARS SPLIT entry above) were **not** rewritten — they stand as originally written, describing what was true at the time. Verified via whole-repo grep: zero remaining matches of the mechanical term outside the two excluded flavor lines. No sim code touched (the term was documentation-only, never implemented in `combatsimulations/`), so no tournament re-run needed.

**EXEMPLARS SPLIT FROM THE COMPILED CRIB — a real conflation, cleanly separated.** Drew: "exemplars mean one thing to me. examples that are the embodiment of the ideal... specifically for Tales Untold content only. The best examples of content." Confirmed: **the Shrike is an exemplar.** This is genuinely different from what `exemplars.md` had become — a practical efficiency crib (engine facts, Strength calibration, format templates) that happened to be built under the same filename because both concepts pointed at similar material. SPLIT: `agent-tools/exemplars.md` renamed to `agent-tools/compiled-crib.md` (kept, unchanged in function — still the token-saving tool Drew asked for two threshold-entries ago — just no longer claiming to be a canonical "kind"; reframed as a cross-cutting utility, not one of the four). NEW `agent-tools/exemplars.md` — short, curated, content-only: **Confirmed** (the Fencerow Shrike, with the specific Design Philosophy principles it demonstrates named) and **Candidates** (Weck, flagged as my proposal, not yet confirmed — same discipline as the invariants.md candidates). Propagated: `agent-tools/README.md` now indexes three tools where it had one; the three generators (`encounter-generator.md`, `npc-encounter-generator.md`, `card-set-generator.md`) repointed to `compiled-crib.md` for their onboarding step; `design-philosophy.md`'s two exemplars.md references were already correct and untouched. Also swept two more over-broad `rules/invariants.md` pointers ("bending or brushing a rule," "anything near an invariant") in the generators — leftover from before invariants.md narrowed to the simulator only; both now route design-quality questions to `design-philosophy.md` instead.

**Two admissions from Drew, logged as fact, not softened.** (1) "I let GPT influence things they clearly didn't have the context for" — the four-layer taxonomy that kicked off this whole three-pass correction arrived via a GPT message Drew relayed, and GPT was reasoning about this repo's architecture without the context this session actually has. (2) "Auth level gating wasn't respected by me" — Drew, as the Authority-3 holder, let that external framing carry more weight into canon than it had earned, rather than checking it against full context first. Neither is a new rule to write down — the three-pass correction that followed is itself the evidence that the system's actual safeguard (cheap, reversible iteration) caught what up-front gating didn't. Worth remembering as a fact about how this specific piece of canon came to exist, not as a new procedure.

**INVARIANTS.MD, THIRD PASS — the word itself was wrong, not just the examples.** Drew, sharpening past the previous fix: "invariants should only be for the combat simulator... it's a mathematical truth underneath the player-facing mechanics." Concrete exemplar he gave: the engine tracks a turn-count per combatant; a human tracks the same thing by looking at a wheel; the invariant is that the count stays correct regardless of which visualization (or none) a human uses. That's a genuinely different KIND of claim than anything previously in the file — a computational fact with a "player visualizes X, engine computes Y, Y must hold regardless of X" structure, not a design principle and not a philosophical restatement.

APPLYING THIS RIGOROUSLY SURFACED A DEEPER BUG THAN DREW HAD NAMED YET: the prime invariant itself ("mechanics exist to reflect the fantasy") doesn't pass this test either — it's a standard for judging whether a MECHANIC is well-designed, not a computational fact about the simulator. It was miscategorized from the very first draft of this file, days ago. This is my own extension of Drew's logic, surfaced explicitly before acting on it (per the heuristic written two entries ago); Drew confirmed it in the same message ("yes, we keep invariants... most of what's in there isn't right... I agree on the design philosophy point completely").

RESOLUTION — three canonical kinds, not four, and Heuristics is gone entirely (Drew: "we don't need the heuristics" — it had become a dumping ground for MY OWN behavior toward him, not design guidance at all; see the entry two above where I built it and mislabeled agent-workflow rules as "design heuristics"):

1. **Invariants** (`rules/invariants.md`, rewritten, much shorter) — scoped STRICTLY to the simulator. One CONFIRMED entry: turn-count-vs-wheel, in Drew's own words. Two CANDIDATES explicitly marked unconfirmed (derived-not-cached stats; card-count conservation across deck/hand/discard/exile) — proposed in the same style, not asserted, since only one entry has actually been validated against the sharpened test. The mechanic-override registry stays but is now labeled as a practical reference index for `combatsimulations/`, not itself a claimed invariant.
2. **Design Philosophy** (`agent-tools/design-philosophy.md`, NEW) — the real home for everything Drew has said across this whole thread about what makes content well-made: mechanics reflect the fantasy (the demoted prime invariant), a deck is a collection of behaviors, ecology drives mechanics, encounters teach through interaction, fiction and mechanics reinforce each other, local rules emerge from environment not arbitrary exception, players learn through interaction, difficulty should be precise not a vague label. All of this is COLLECTED from things Drew already said this session, not invented new.
3. **Exemplars** unchanged in role — re-pointed to demonstrate Design Philosophy, not "invariants."

Heuristics.md's actual content redistributed rather than lost: the sim-as-evaluation-tool prohibition folded into CLAUDE.md's Work Modes directly; the "know who the contradiction is from" distinction folded into Translation Principle as a new explicit bullet (fixing the exact friction Drew named: a direct statement from Drew already IS the ruling, doesn't need a second confirmation round-trip — apply and flag, don't ask first); "promote repeated retrieval" restored as an inline note in exemplars.md where it always belonged; the rule-bender-architecture escalation path was already documented in memory.md's "architecture north star" and needed no new home; "mark inferred connections" was already covered by Translation Principle's existing language.

PROPAGATED: `CLAUDE.md`'s Four Kinds section rewritten (three kinds + exemplars, explicit "this happened twice, the same way" callout); Translation Principle's loose use of the word "invariant" corrected throughout (it was using the word in its OLD, general sense right as the word's technical meaning was being narrowed — exactly the kind of drift this whole exercise is about catching); Canon Gate's Authority-3 list swapped "the prime invariant" for "core Design Philosophy"; `agent-tools/red-team.md`'s Invariant Violations pass renamed "Engine-Invariant Violations" and narrowed to genuine engine-architecture concerns (timing windows, new policies) — it was already correctly scoped, just needed its dangling "prime invariant" pointer fixed — plus a NEW pass (1b, Design Philosophy fit) added to fill a real gap: nothing previously checked new content against deck-expresses-behavior/ecology-drives-mechanics at all. `agent-tools/README.md` swapped its heuristics.md row for design-philosophy.md.

HONEST PATTERN ACROSS ALL THREE PASSES, worth naming plainly: each pass caught a real error, made a genuine correction, and STILL left a category confusion for the next pass to find — first pass fixed unlabeled examples but left rule-definition duplication; second pass fixed the duplication but left keyword-specific "invariants" and invented an ill-defined heuristics layer; third pass (this one) fixed the labeling and the layer, but only because Drew kept pushing past what I'd have called "done." The lesson isn't "get it right the first time" — the repo's whole architecture assumes correction is cheap. The lesson is: don't mistake "I made a coherent-sounding fix" for "the fix is actually right." Only Drew's continued pressure — and his own willingness to say "I don't think we're using the word right," including about his OWN earlier framing — got this to something that survives its own definition.

**INVARIANTS.MD, SECOND PASS — Drew: "I still feel like our workflow is muddy... this is a step in the right direction."** Four corrections, all real, applied same-day: (1) **The Initiative Shift and Staggered worked examples are GONE from invariants.md entirely** — Drew: "those were my examples that don't fit the actual resolution level invariants are supposed to operate at." Even clearly-labeled, keyword-specific worked examples were still the wrong layer; the prime invariant now stands alone, self-defining, no illustration needed — Drew's own sharper wording adopted directly: "mechanics exist to ensure that they reflect the fantasy of the TTRPG." Also removed the Initiative-Shift-specific "Standing invariants" bullet on the same reasoning (its content already lives correctly in card-glossary.md; the "list is canonical, wheel is a visualization" insight was kept, but relocated to heuristics.md as implementation guidance, not restated as an invariant). (2) **"Rule bending cards don't temporarily swap invariants."** The registry and "Adding a rule-bender" language had cards "bending" or "swapping" invariants — a category error Drew caught: invariants can't bend, that's what invariant means. Cards override specific MECHANICS; the invariant is only ever the yardstick a new mechanic gets checked against, never itself modified. Fixed throughout: the registry's "Invariant bent" column is now "Mechanic overridden"; `agent-tools/red-team.md`'s Invariant Violations pass reworded to match ("name the mechanic it overrides... check against the prime invariant"). (3) **Cut using the combat simulator to evaluate changes** — a real, named workflow bug: I had drifted into running tournaments as a self-verification step when implementing rulings (the Staggered before/after, the earlier hand-size-floor check). Per CLAUDE.md's own stated purpose, the sim was always supposed to be Drew's separate instrument, not my validation gate. `CLAUDE.md`'s Work Modes Release-checklist no longer lists "both tournaments"; `agent-tools/heuristics.md` gained an explicit heuristic naming this; sim CODE changes (keeping the executable model faithful to canon, e.g. today's Staggered persistence fix) are NOT what's being cut — running tournaments to judge whether a change is good is. (4) **The deeper bug, named plainly: Authority gating created friction on Drew's OWN direct statement.** When Drew wrote out heavier Staggered mechanics mid-conversation, I treated it as an unconfirmed aside needing a round-trip before I'd apply it — but he IS Authority 3, and a direct statement from him already is the ruling; it doesn't need to be said twice to count. The existing "flag contradictions, don't quietly harmonize" heuristic was too broad — it conflated "Drew states something new" (apply it, flag it in the queue, let him revert — that's what the Gate's reversibility is FOR) with "I'm inferring or paraphrasing something ambiguous" (the actual case that deserves a pause). Heuristic rewritten to draw this line explicitly. Also filled one genuine gap surfaced while trimming: the blind/simultaneous-reveal fact (defender chooses without seeing the attacker's card) had never actually been stated in `rules/combat.md`'s Attack Resolution — only in the invariants.md content now being cut. Added there properly rather than just deleted.

**STAGGERED — CONFIRMED HEAVIER, FULLY PROPAGATED (Drew's A3 ruling, resolving last entry's flagged discrepancy).** Drew confirmed precisely: "skips all blocks and attacks until removed. The affliction lasts until a character takes the time to recover their balance or has an ally help them" — the heavier version, not the old one-time skip. Narrower than his own earlier aside (no "noncombat actions" restriction this time) — followed the latest, most deliberate wording exactly. CANON: `rules/card-glossary.md` Staggered rewritten (cannot attack OR defend; persists until the affected character or an ally spends an action to clear it — no new action-table entry needed, matches the existing WOUND/EXHAUST self-service pattern); `rules/invariants.md`'s Staggered worked-example and rule-modifier registry row both updated to match (registry: "may attack, may defend | until recovered" replacing "skips block | next attack"). AUDITED every card that grants or checks Staggered (masaharu, minotaur-coil ×2, blue-mind, alignment-marshal-engine, flapjack-octopus ×2, wall-reader-coil ×2, the-descended) — nothing broke; several got sharper (Flapjack Octopus's Staggered-punisher and The Descended's Drag both key off "target is Staggered," and a real persistent window makes both actually reliable instead of a one-attack sliver).

**SIM REWORKED to match** — this was the substantial part. Old implementation: `defender.staggered` was a single-use flag, checked once at the top of `attack()` in both engines, auto-cleared immediately after. New: the flag persists (auto-clear line removed from both engines); `policies.py`'s `legal_attacks()` now returns `[]` outright while staggered (cannot attack, not just cannot defend); the old `clear_wound_if_idle` helper was renamed `idle_recovery` and now checks staggered FIRST, wound second (5 call sites across all duel policies) — recovering from Staggered always outranks discarding a Wound when a turn is otherwise forced-idle; both engines gained a `recover_stagger` action (self-recovery); the team engine additionally gained `assist_stagger` (an ally spends their action to clear a teammate), with `TeamTactician.choose_action` prioritizing helping a staggered ally over wound-clearing or repositioning when it has no attack.

**BALANCE CHECK, done honestly with a real before/after, not just a single post-change number.** Default duel and team tournaments: byte-identical to pre-change (44.9/53.8 duel, 41.6/58.1 team) — the roster decks barely exercise Staggered. Built a matchup that actually stresses it (Tempo, whose PROFILE defense grants Staggered, vs Vanguard) and got 99.0/1.0 — alarming in isolation, so before reporting it as a consequence of THIS change, git-stashed the sim edits and re-ran the identical matchup under the OLD one-time-skip mechanics: **98.8/1.2.** The blowout predates today's change entirely — Tempo already crushes Vanguard for unrelated deck-construction reasons; persistent Staggered moved the needle by roughly 0.2 points, not 50. Reported as a real, separate, pre-existing balance question worth Drew's eye sometime — explicitly NOT something today's ruling caused or that needs fixing as part of it.

**THE FOUR-LAYER TAXONOMY, FORMALIZED (Drew + GPT, Authority 3) — invariants.md was leaking.** Drew's diagnosis, worked out collaboratively with GPT: the repo has four distinct kinds of canonical content that get confused because a real piece of content usually touches several at once — (1) **Rule Definitions**, vocabulary, "what something is" (keyword texts, formulas, procedures — card-glossary.md/core-rules.md/combat.md); (2) **Invariants**, constitutional, "what must survive every implementation," NOT how any one implementation works (rules/invariants.md); (3) **Exemplars**, concrete implementations chosen because they DEMONSTRATE invariants — "you don't copy the Shrike, you extract from it" (agent-tools/exemplars.md); (4) **Heuristics**, operational habits about how the work gets done, not truths about the world (NEW: agent-tools/heuristics.md). Diagram: Invariant → implemented by → Rule Definitions → assembled into → Exemplar — which is WHY an exemplar reads as blurry (it legitimately carries all three other layers at once, by design); the actual bug was invariants.md alone absorbing jobs that weren't its own.

**THE CONCRETE BUG, confirmed by audit, not just asserted:** invariants.md's "The exchange" section substantially duplicated rules/combat.md's Attack Resolution (RPS table, tie logic, near-verbatim); its Initiative Shift bullet duplicated card-glossary.md's full seat/count/pass-over bookkeeping; its "Derived stats" bullet fully duplicated core-rules.md's Stats table and card-glossary.md's Stat Change section — three live instances of the exact two-sources-of-truth drift risk this repo's whole philosophy exists to prevent. ALSO CONFIRMED: the prime invariant's own worked example (the initiative-shift parenthetical) was never labeled as an example — a reader could plausibly mistake the prime invariant's SCOPE as being about initiative shift specifically, rather than a fully general meta-principle.

**FIXED:** invariants.md rewritten in full — new header formalizing the four-layer map (with the bug named explicitly, so it's checkable against in future); the prime invariant now carries TWO clearly-labeled worked examples spanning different subsystems (Initiative Shift, and Staggered — Drew's own insight adopted near-verbatim: "the fantasy is being knocked off balance... that's why an ally can help"); the resolution-loop and standing-invariants sections trimmed to state ONLY the invariant, with the actual bookkeeping/formulas pointed at their real homes instead of restated; the damage pipeline's exact step order MOVED to rules/combat.md (new Damage Pipeline section — it never had one before, this was pure rule-definition content orphaned in the wrong file) rather than just trimmed away; "Adding a rule-bender" (pure engineering-process guidance, no world-truth in it) MOVED to the new heuristics.md, which also absorbed the exemplars.md maintenance rule. CLAUDE.md gained a "Four Kinds of Canonical Content" section as central, discoverable doctrine, positioned right before the Canon Gate (know what kind of thing something is before asking who may change it). agent-tools/README.md indexes heuristics.md alongside exemplars.md.

**FLAGGED, NOT SILENTLY RESOLVED — Staggered's mechanics.** Drew's message included two illustrative Staggered texts ("cannot attack, defend, or use most noncombat actions until removed; an ally may spend an action to remove it") that do NOT match the actual current canonical text in card-glossary.md ("next attack against you resolves unopposed, auto-expires, no removal action needed") — a much heavier, persistent-until-cleared status vs. the current one-time skip. Per the heuristic just written into heuristics.md (flag contradictions in illustrative asides, don't quietly harmonize): I used Drew's underlying INSIGHT (the fantasy/mechanic tie) as the prime invariant's second worked example without adopting his specific mechanical text, and left card-glossary.md's Staggered entry untouched. Open question for Drew: was the heavier version an intentional ruling (Authority 3 — Staggered would need to update everywhere it's applied, including the rule-modifiers registry's "Stagger" row) or just an imprecise recollection used for the taxonomy discussion? No card content depends on this yet, so nothing is broken either way — just unconfirmed.
