**NEW CREATURE — SKEINWING (Authority 1, Drew's biology + GPT's thematic framing, formalized).** Drew described a fully-realized new species in one message: a 6-7ft-wingspan, blind/echolocating, membrane-winged high-altitude glider — "tiny sky whales eating plankton" (insects, caught in bulk, on the wing, at altitude); slow hibernation-paced metabolism it stays socially engaged through; blue at rest, warming toward the color of danger when active; mates by entwining and LOCKING tails mid-air, gliding together for days on shared air currents, "so they don't lose each other" — and explicitly NOT to be called a bat. GPT's contribution, adopted directly as the creature's core invariant and its Tactical Purpose framing: *"The sky is home. The ground is danger... it doesn't die because it's weak — it dies because its adaptations are hyper-specialized"* and the larder-reveal beat (mundane finds — jackrabbit, scratcher, emerald frog, all existing bestiary creatures, confirmed before use — then one impossible blue thing that shouldn't be there). NAMED Skeinwing (a skein is both a flock in flight and a tangled skein of thread — lands the social-gliding AND the tail-lock in one word; flagged as my call, renamable). STATS: Mind 2 / Body 1 / Soul 4 — HP 11, Strength 7 (Soul as the clear lead stat: initiative/grace/the tail-bonded social life; Body as the deliberate weak point — "never built to take a hit, ever"). DELIBERATELY NO SIGNATURE CARDS — this is primarily a discovery/loot object (the concrete answer to the Larder Fence's "one thing that should not be on a fence" line, now filled in but left swappable), not a standard encounter; the stat block exists only for the rare table that wants to run a live, grounded, dying one. GENERATIVE FINDING surfaced, not buried: a grounded Skeinwing invites a mercy/rescue interaction, not just kill-or-loot. Wired in three places: `bestiary/skeinwing.md` (new file), `places/turnroot-weald.md` (Notable Species list, purely additive), `quests/the-larder-fence.md` (Win Condition's larder line, still swappable for GM's own thread), and cross-referenced from `bestiary/fencerow-shrike.md`. Checked against both touched files for consistency — reinforces the Shrike's established "small, mundane prey" identity rather than contradicting it (explicitly never hunted, only ever an accident), and fits Turnroot Weald's existing "unfamiliar behaviors not found elsewhere" framing without touching the Weald's core redirect-mechanic invariant.

**THE SHRIKE'S FENCE, TAKE FIVE — geography, causality, and the Masons' real identity.** Three corrections from Drew, each removing an assumption I'd made without being told to: (1) **Placement fixed** — this is Briarwatch's outer perimeter against Turnroot Weald, not a fence near farmland; Briarwatch deliberately keeps fields and livestock back from that border, "the farmers know better." Reworded the opening placement line and Appearance/Setup in both files. (2) **Decoupled from the missing-livestock hook** — I'd linked this encounter to the Warden Post's livestock report (bestiary opening line, quest Related Documents); that link never had a basis and is now explicitly wrong per (1). Removed both references and added a GM Secret line stating the two are unrelated, specifically to stop a future session (or future me) from re-connecting them by inference. (3) **The oversized thorns' true cause, recorded**: not the Weald's ambient growth, not Mason design — the Shrikes themselves dragged thorn-brush in from the Weald across generations and worked it into their stretch of an otherwise plain Mason perimeter. Player-facing ambiguity in Appearance stays (folk still don't know), GM Secret now has the real answer, plus an explicitly-unforced thread: a wall built to keep the Weald OUT now has literal Weald-thorn woven into it by ordinary bird behavior nobody's tracking — flagged as an open question, not asserted as active danger.

**THE SHRIKE'S FENCE, TAKE FOUR — the word "perch" was still fighting the fix.** Drew caught it precisely: "Backline = flying/airborne" contradicted the card's own name (WATCHFUL PERCH) and every flavor line calling it perched — a bird doesn't hover in place, it perches on something solid. RESOLUTION (no invention needed — the fiction was already there): the fence is two structural zones under one name. The **wire and bramble** between the posts — barbed, where the larder hangs — is the Frontline. The **stone corner posts** — taller, clean, structurally part of the same fence — are the Backline. Same object, two zones, exactly matching Positioning's existing "abstract zones" rule; the quest file's own Setup line ("two fenceposts up...") already had this detail sitting unused. Reworded throughout both files: Rushdown's block now reasons from "can't climb a post mid-fight," not "can't reach the air"; the Pull counterplay is now "off its post and onto the wire," not "onto its own fence" (imprecise once the post IS the fence); territorial-disengage reasons from "its own post is the outer edge," not "airborne range." CAUGHT ONE MORE LEFTOVER BEFORE SHIPPING: two pre-existing lines (Behavior, and the quest's Rhythm notes) said the Shrike "returns to the wire" after a catch — meaning "returns to safety," written before Frontline/Backline existed for this creature, now directly contradicting wire=Frontline=hazard. Fixed to "returns to its post" in both. Grepped for any remaining flying/airborne/altitude language after the edit — one hit, and it's the correct usage (a line explicitly stating Backline is "not a euphemism for flying"). Same lesson as the last two passes on this creature: check the file's own older sentences against new ones before shipping, not just the new text in isolation.

**THE SHRIKE'S FENCE, TAKE THREE — granular pass, one real walk-back.** Drew's corrections, translated: (1) Backline for a flying creature isn't "off the wire" or "behind the fence" — it's altitude. Canon already supports this (`rules/combat.md`: "both positions are abstract zones"), so this was a flavor fix, not a rules change — reworded in both files. (2) **Rushdown corrected — closing-the-gap logic, not terrain-arrival logic.** My last entry's "generative finding" (party Rushdowns a perched Shrike onto its own fence) was WRONG and is walked back here: you cannot run up to something in the air. Drew's ruling: Rushdown can't target a flying Backline creature, and a flying creature couldn't use Rushdown either (moot for this kit — none of its 9 cards carry it, stated for completeness). Framed explicitly as fictional consistency identical in kind to Melee's existing "both Frontline" gate — NOT a redefinition of Rushdown's canonical text elsewhere, so this stays Authority-1, not a keyword change. Pull is unaffected (drags regardless of distance) and inherits the "turn against the Shrike" tactic Rushdown used to hold. (3) **Territorial disengagement, mechanically grounded in fiction**: a Shrike can't haul person-sized prey home from beyond its own firing range, so it never chases past the field — "just walk away" is now an explicit third Win Condition, not merely implied by general Flee rules. (4) **The Mason lore, fully confirmed, not merely floated**: tower + fence are ONE project, containing Turnroot Weald's encroachment at Briarwatch specifically, maintained on periodic visits. Recorded ONCE in `factions/the-masons.md` (single source of truth per repo convention) and cross-referenced from three other files rather than duplicated — checked all four for consistency and correct scope-limiting language before shipping. Net effect of this pass: the encounter's physical logic is now airtight (nothing forces a fictional impossibility to happen for mechanical convenience), and Briarwatch's oldest unexplained feature has a real, secret, checkable answer.

**THE SHRIKE'S FENCE, TAKE TWO — Drew caught my own contradiction.** Last entry's rebuild put the fence at Backline; the pre-existing (untouched) Combat Behavior section had ALWAYS had Perched=Backline / dive=Frontline — meaning the fence (where diving lands, where grounded sits) was already Frontline in the file's own combat text. My new "Fence Itself"/Setup prose contradicted the file's own older section. Drew's fix — "place the fence in the Frontline position, flying goes Backline" — was a correction of MY inconsistency, not a new design ask. RE-DERIVED THE MAPPING FROM CARD TEXT, not assumption: checked PUSH ("move to backline"), PULL ("pull to frontline," both effect and defensive bonus), Rushdown ("Backline to Frontline") — confirmed only Rushdown and Pull force something *onto* the fence now; Push and self-directed movement (Impaling Dive, Watchful Perch, Move Position, Flow, Dart) never do. THE UPGRADE — Drew's "extra juice": Barbed is no longer a one-time chip, it's sticky. Entry: 1 unpreventable damage (forced-to-Frontline only; voluntary entry is always safe — "the Shrike never catches itself"). While Barbed: 1 more per attack made, 1 more per successful block. Exit: 1 more, whether leaving Frontline (forced or chosen — the hook tears either way once caught) or working it loose in place. A bad Rushdown/Pull can now cost 4+ unpreventable damage across a few turns before it's an actual attack landing. SCOPED AS ENCOUNTER-SPECIFIC TERRAIN, not a global keyword (Shifting Burrow precedent) — said explicitly in-file so it's never assumed portable without a fresh ruling; flagged for Drew that it's promotable to `card-glossary.md` later if he wants it reusable. GENERATIVE FINDING KEPT VISIBLE: a perched Shrike is one Rushdown away from its own fence, on the party's schedule instead of its own — the rhythm the Shrike weaponizes gets weaponized back. MASON LORE (see queue): the fence is their work, hidden even from the farmers who tend it; kept strictly out of player-facing Appearance/Setup text, recorded in a marked GM Secret section, cross-linked into a new (first-of-its-kind) Known Works list on the Masons' own file. Ruins-connection flagged unconfirmed per the Translation Principle, not asserted as fact. Not sim-implemented; no card text changed this pass, so no new print-sheet staleness beyond what was already ledgered.

**THE FENCEROW SHRIKE REBUILT — flagship exemplar (Drew's four-part brief).** (1) **The fence, not the bird, is the threat now.** Appearance rewritten to lead with scale: taller than a person, stone-cored under uncut decades of bramble, barbs finger-thick and hooked like fishhooks — "built to hold what they catch, not just cut it." Origin left DELIBERATELY unresolved (Weald's growth, or generations of shrikes tending the wire — "nobody in Briarwatch will say for certain out loud") — a question, not an asserted fact, so it stays Authority-1 safe despite brushing Turnroot Weald's established sentience; flagged here for visibility rather than silently riding in. (2) **Restatted 1/2/3 → 2/3/4**: HP 13→15 (formula), Strength 6→9 — a real jump (2 Shrikes: 12→18 total vs a Strength-36 party), called out explicitly in the quest's own opening line ("tuned deliberately heavier than a pure teaching mob") rather than hidden. Moved bands in the crib's Strength table (4-6 → 8-10, alongside Stonecoil/Fogcaller/Vescal). (3) **THORN LARDER fixed** — Drew: "fails to represent a behavior." Diagnosis: random hand-discard is what a dozen other blue cards already do; it modeled disruption, not "impale and store, retrieve later." Fix: Effect → Exile 1 card from the defender's hand ("impaled on the fence, out of the fight until the larder gives it back") — Exile's own rules-text (returns to owner's deck at end of combat) IS the larder mechanically, not just in flavor. Checked: no other card in the pool exiles from an opponent's hand — genuinely unique now, not a duplicate. (4) **NEW: the fence as a physical hazard** — anyone forced into the Backline (the fence's own position) takes 1 unpreventable damage, terrain not attack, no roll (ties directly to today's earlier Unpreventable ruling: environmental damage isn't an attack, so nothing mitigates it). RED-TEAM CAUGHT A REAL BUG before shipping: first draft said Push/Pull/Rushdown all trigger it — checked the actual card texts and only PUSH moves a target to Backline (the fence); PULL and Rushdown both move toward Frontline, the opposite direction. Corrected in both files. Surfaced generative finding (kept, not hidden): a grounded Shrike sits at Frontline mid-window — one Push sends it into its own wall. Full 9-card deck (2B/3R/4G) now lives directly in the bestiary entry, upgrading the "recommended full deck" convention's own exemplar from the quest file to the bestiary file itself, per the crib. Ledgered: Briarwatch print sheet stale again (Thorn Larder text changed); Scratcher deck-flow rewrite in combat-example still pending. Not sim-implemented (canon-only creature, no sim change needed).

**Two rulings closing the last queue items (Drew).** (1) **BOSS HP IS BESPOKE, formula stays the offered baseline.** ROOT HEART restored 19→30, MINOTAUR restored 21→24, both marked explicitly (`*(bespoke — boss exception; formula baseline is N)*`) so the departure is never silent. Root Heart's own Pulse thresholds (20 HP / 10 HP) only read correctly against 30 — the bespoke number wasn't arbitrary, the sim-brained uniform pass just didn't know that. Rule softened in `CLAUDE.md` (Stat Blocks + Quick Reference) and the crib: formula is what a generator DEFAULTS to; a boss may depart from it when the fiction calls for a set-piece number, always marked. This is the Authority-2 anti-drift rule in miniature — the exception is allowed precisely because it's labeled, not silent. (2) **STEELE'S DECK SWAP STAYS, no revert.** Drew: repo content doesn't track Kevin's physical printed cards — "all changes in the repo are for the full campaign." The repo is the canonical/updated state; a real table's printed deck is a snapshot that lags it. Cleared with no code change.

**THE STRENGTH SYSTEM — tiers abolished (Drew, A3, superseding the descriptive recalibration same-day).** Three formal rules now govern every combatant, players included: (1) **HP = (2 × Body) + 9, always** — no bespoke HP; tougher = more Body; (2) **deck size = total stats**, color counts = each stat (signatures count toward their color); (3) **Strength = total stats** replaces Early/Mid/Late as the difficulty scale — precise, self-documenting against the player baseline of 9. THE UNIFICATION INSIGHT: players already obeyed these rules — trifecta spreads sum to 9, player decks are 9 cards with color counts matching stats, player HP is formula. The "rules for creatures" were latent in the player conventions all along; Drew's ruling made one law of two customs. EXECUTED: all 19 statblocks conformed (HP by formula + Strength line; Difficulty lines gone); CLAUDE.md/exemplars/cards.md/gm-guide/encounter-generator/invariants updated (tier language out, Strength anchors in: 4–6 teaching creatures, 8–10 real fights, 11+ above-a-player); Larder Fence decks conformed 9→6; combat-example Scratcher HP renumbered. FLAGGED: Root Heart 30→19 and Minotaur 24→21 — bosses under uniform formula need their toughness restated as stats (queued). LEDGERED: combat-example's Scratcher deck-flow (7→4 cards changes the narrated draws). FUTURE GOAL (Drew): compute per-card action-economy advantage and fold it into difficulty ranking — the sim/metrics lane's next big instrument. Sim unaffected (already formula-HP; roster decks already 9). Note: this supersedes the same-day descriptive tier recalibration — the tiers lasted six hours, which is the gate working (a cheap A2 experiment revealed the real A3 answer).

**FOUR CUTS + TIER RECALIBRATION + DECK DIRECTION (Drew's batch).** (1) **PREDICTABLE CUT** (keyword, Drew's A3): was the sole exception to reveal-simultaneity; removed from glossary, keyword lists, invariants (the "reveal is private" bullet now reads *nothing breaks the blind — simultaneity is absolute*, and the registry lost its row), STUDY (def → Gain Evade), Orin's FEINT (def → Attacker gains Blind + his GM note rewritten), and the ENTIRE sim read-machinery (attack-flow blocks in both engines, `perfect_read_defense` + 5 call sites, combatant attrs). Poetic accounting: built, verified, and cut within the week — the threshold log holds both crossings. (2) **POISON CUT**: barely existed — one word in resolution.md's Body-save examples; removed. (3) **BLEED CUT**: BLOOD TITHE's ongoing-tick defense was the only real instance → def now Lifesteal 2; combat.md's Simultaneous Effects rule kept but its bleed illustration genericized; "bleed" dropped from Unpreventable's example lists; sim blood_tithe ongoing machinery + mutual-death RULING removed from both engines. (4) **GREEN SELF-ALLY REVERTED** (Drew: "should green be counting itself? undo"): the exception section removed from rules/cards.md — You Are Not Your Own Ally is again absolute, no color exempt; combat-example's two green-exception clauses trimmed; exemplars line updated; sim: implicit-"ally" green cards (Renewal, Twin Strike def, Resonate both sides, Support effect, Witness both sides, Rooted Oath tick) reverted to allies-only, while explicit party/"you and" wording (Communion, Synchrony's own text) legitimately keeps self. NOTE: this undoes the buff that fixed green's team weakness (the old green-vs-blue 63.5% finding predates many changes since; current team baseline moved only 41.5/58.3 → 41.6/58.1 — the modern support kit carries green without the self-inclusion in this matchup; watch it at the table). (5) **TIERS RECALIBRATED** descriptively from real content (see queue flag — inferred interpretation). (6) **EXEMPLAR MAINTENANCE RULE** added to the crib: "if an agent repeatedly retrieves examples to rediscover a pattern, promote that pattern into an exemplar" (Drew's line, verbatim in spirit). (7) **COMPLETE CREATURE DECKS = new direction**: creatures need full recommended decks to breathe (the Shrike's listed 9 in `quests/the-larder-fence.md` is the pattern, now cited in the crib); the backfill across the bestiary is a defined, verifiable, long-think job — earmarked for the future local model (fits its clearly-defined-workflow lane exactly). Tournaments after everything: duel 44.9/53.8, team 41.6/58.1 (≈1pt drift total, honest). Core print sheet regenerated (STUDY/BLOOD TITHE text).

**BLACK MAGGIE RATIFIED — five constitutional rulings (Drew, Authority 3 exercised in chat).** The discussion of `experimental/black-maggie.md` produced canon: (1) **SEATS CANNOT FADE** — Drew explicitly declined that threshold ("let's not cross that particular one"); Seats are permanent structural features, full stop. (2) **ARCHONS fade** — a domain whose practice dies starves its bearer; the bearer thins until the Seat stands EMPTY, and empty seats are established major plot material (the Filled/Empty/Contested table already existed in `mythology/seats.md`; the rulings added the missing MECHANISM as a new **Fading** state). (3) **Time's Archon is the standing precedent** — Lysander has faded so far the world has forgotten his name (the docs remember what Eclipsera doesn't); this makes MoroM the second instance of an established pattern, not an invention. (4) **THE THESSIANS ARE IN** — new race ratified (nomadic healers, curled horns, pain-transference through touch and ritual; Maggie the last). (5) **MoroM's nature stays HAZY** — deliberately unresolved per Drew ("still hazy to me") and the file's own restraint note; canon entries are minimal and point at her file. EXECUTED: black-maggie.md's two seat-fading passages re-aimed at the Archon (the ritual now anchors MoroM, never "binds the Seat"); `mythology/seats.md` gains the Fading state row + Seats-never-fade line + Lysander's forgotten name + the Witnessed Suffering seat; `world/seats-archons-echoes.md` gains the fading mechanism paragraph + MoroM among Known Archons; **black-maggie.md + lore-fragments PROMOTED to `characters/`**. Assessment note for the record: the material needed almost no editing — the tragedy structure (inversion of the Thessian gift; the neutral Archon that cannot refuse her; Maggie as the 700th sacrifice; "she will get to put it down") and the fragments' comprehension-horror delivery were promotion-quality as written. Open (Drew's, unhurried): the race's name in the dead tongue, completed-vs-in-progress site count, whether completed-site hauntings can be addressed, her age, whether more Thessians ever appear, "Brenn road" unplaced on the map.

**Tooling modernized + repo re-homing (Drew, post-stress-test).** (1) GENERATORS REWRITTEN agent-native (`encounter-generator.md`, `npc-encounter-generator.md`): dropped the "paste into a session" framing and chat-block output; output is now repo files with explicit FILE ROUTING (encounters→quests/, creatures→bestiary/+cards/, named people→characters/ never bestiary/, open-hook content→experimental/ first — canon placement comes with promotion, not authorship); onboarding now covers the Wait action, invariants.md, hand=Mind min 2, lineage source-tags, work-modes ledger, and current exemplars. (2) WECK moved `quests/` → `experimental/the-man-who-buys-wounds.md` — his ledger is an unratified world-level hook; authoring it straight into canon was presumptuous (the move established the routing rule now baked into the NPC generator). (3) NPCs OUT OF BESTIARY: masaharu, orin-vane, vescal → `characters/` (people go in characters/, even with combat statblocks); all cross-refs fixed (vultures-nest, 3 card files, vescal's Trutinate ref, tracks hook).

**HAND SIZE FLOOR = 2 (Drew, ruling on the stress test's systemic finding).** The encounter work surfaced that hand = Mind left every Mind-1 creature (scratcher, jackrabbit, borrower sentry) holding 1 card — defenseless after acting, a de facto rebalance of all Early content nobody chose. Drew's fix: **hand size = Mind, minimum 2** — "the simplest implementation that gets the best behavior overall." What it buys in one stroke: (1) Mind-1 creatures act and still hold one block (their pre-change behavior restored); (2) the open Mind-0 drain edge is closed (however far Mind falls, hand never drops below 2 — no total shutdown); (3) ZERO player-facing impact (all roster characters Mind ≥ 2; both tournaments byte-identical: duel 45.2/53.4, team 41.5/58.3). CHANGED: the six canon formula spots now read "Mind (minimum 2)"; `invariants.md` blocking bullet (floor = act-plus-one-block, no cushion beyond it); sim `engine.py` init + `effective_hand_size` → `max(2, mind)`. CONSEQUENCE HANDLED: the FENCEROW SHRIKE's vulnerability window was built on Mind-1 nakedness, which no longer exists — its window is now DESIGNED text instead of emergent: IMPALING DIVE gains the Berserker's Price rider ("You cannot defend until your next turn"), approved core-card language; bestiary + quest updated (lesson reframed from hand-economy to commitment-cost, which is arguably more Early-legible). Ledger updated: combat-example rewrite SHRANK (scratcher hand 2 restored by the floor).

**Encounter stress test — 3 standalone Briarwoods/Briarwatch encounters (Drew: "see how the tools perform").** Brief: Early tier, all three types (combat-with-twist / Kino's-weird / social-with-teeth), 4-player-tuned combat, new creatures. Built with the full pipeline (generator onboarding → draft → red-team → alignment): (1) **The Larder Fence** (`quests/the-larder-fence.md`) — 2 new FENCEROW SHRIKES (`bestiary/` + 3 BRIARWOODS-tagged signature cards), a rhythm-fighter that teaches turn-order reading and the Wait action; its vulnerability window is ENFORCED BY THE ENGINE (Mind 1 → hand 1 → after its dive it cannot defend) rather than scripted. (2) **Where the Tracks Stop** (`quests/where-the-tracks-stop.md`) — combat-optional; the briarwoods.md "tracks that don't continue" line, stopped at; teaches the 3 Perception modes; walking away is a complete answer. (3) **The Man Who Buys Wounds** (`quests/the-man-who-buys-wounds.md`) — Weck, an NPC who permanently removes 1 Wound for coin + your name in his ledger; price unspecified BY DESIGN (GM hook, Drew owns what the ledger means). SURFACED FOR DREW'S RULING: (a) **systemic finding** — the hand-size change means EVERY Mind-1 creature (scratcher, jackrabbit, borrower sentry, shrike) now holds 1 card and is defenseless after acting; the shrike builds its lesson on this, but it's a de facto rebalance of ALL existing Early content — intended texture or accidental nerf? (b) **canon extension** — Tracks implies Borrowers keep/herd surface livestock below (consistent with the missing-livestock notice, but new faction behavior). Red-team notes: THORN LARDER's random-discard overlaps STILLNESS (accepted for creature fidelity, flagged); all keywords from the approved list; effect≠defense respected on all 3 cards; no Unheld-inland violation in Tracks (the Hollow owns it). Ledgered: briarwatch print set stale. NOT sim-implemented (canon-first creature; deckable later if wanted).

**HAND SIZE = MIND — the +1 is gone (Drew, un-tabling the entry below with eyes open).** WAS: hand size = Mind + 1, tabled because the 1v1 sim couldn't measure blocking capacity and removing the floor looked like it would make Mind 2 focus-fire bait and Mind 4 a dominant wall. NOW: **hand size = Mind**, ruled by Drew with team combat in mind. His reasoning (the important part): a 1-block-by-default player compensating through **Resist, card draw, and ally support is the system working** — it makes the support web load-bearing, not a patch; **character progression exists**, so the floor case is a starting point, not a sentence ("if there was no character progression I wouldn't choose this"); and **equipment/items are a compensating layer the sims don't account for and probably shouldn't** — the sim's floor cases are worse than the table's reality. EVIDENCE (the tabled entry's demanded team-sim check, now run): duel Frost/Steele 45.2/53.4 (was 42.5/56.8 — the gap narrows because Steele's Mind 2 pays exactly as designed); team 41.5/58.3 (was 38.1/61.8); fights ~15% faster everywhere (duel 8.1→6.7 turns, team 33.5→29.9) — less blocking, more landed hits. THE WALL CHECK: Sage (Mind 4) vs Steele (Mind 2) = **60.1%**, DOWN from 88.9% at the last checkpoint — no dominant wall; the blue/scry nerfs since the original test more than absorbed the hand-size gain. CHANGED: `rules/core-rules.md`, `character-creation.md` (×2), `card-glossary.md` (Stat Change), `cards.md`, `gm-guide.md`, `bestiary/orin-vane.md` (hand 6→5), `bestiary/future-lock-wasp.md` (slots 3/5→2/4), `rules/invariants.md` (blocking bullet: the floor is gone; low Mind compensating through the system is INTENDED pressure), sim `engine.py` (init + `effective_hand_size`). LEDGERED (first real Pending-propagation use): `rules/combat-example.md` — its whole worked arithmetic runs on stated hand sizes; needs a careful rewrite at the next Sync. OPEN EDGE (flagged, not ruled): Mind drained to 0 now means hand 0 (previously 1) — cannot hold cards at all; rare (needs stacked Sunder) but worth a canon call someday: is hand 0 the intended full shutdown, or does hand size floor at 1? Print sheets NOT dirty (no card text carries the formula; CLIMB's +1 is relative).

**Hand-size change (Mind+1 → Mind) — TABLED until team sim exists (SUPERSEDED by the entry above).** Tested: makes Mind a real stat (a Mind-4 blue deck went 69%→80% vs Steele) but Drew correctly held off. KEY INSIGHT: hand size = BLOCKING CAPACITY between turns, which only matters in TEAM play (multiple enemies attack you before your next turn; each block costs a card). The current +1 is a load-bearing defensive floor (Mind 2 → hand 3 → attack + still 2 blocks; nobody is ever defenseless). Remove it and Mind 2 → 1 block (focus-fired to death) while Mind 4 → 3 blocks (dominant wall). The 1v1 sim STRUCTURALLY CANNOT measure this (only one attacker between turns), so the Sage 80% is the floor of how dominant Mind-4 would be, not the ceiling. → hand-size balance is un-evaluable until the team sim; keep Mind+1 for now. Strong argument for building the team sim next.

**Range definitions**
Quick reference sheet is canonical. Melee: both must be Frontline. Ranged: works only while not in Melee range with target.

**Alignment Marshal — Correction Protocol**
Passive ability on the Marshal stat block: whenever the Marshal takes damage, shuffle 1 Exhaust into the attacker's deck. See `bestiary/alignment-marshal.md`. Cards: `cards/alignment-marshal-engine.md`.

**Aege — the Carrion Guide**
Named NPC at Vulture's Nest. Turnroot native. Gives party the sealed letter hook to Turnroot Weald. Also gives Carrion Feather item on handoff. Refer to her as "the Carrion Guide" in session — Aege is her name. See `places/vultures-nest.md`.

**[2026-07-19 12:26 AM CDT] Card review batch 5, eleven cards — the deepest sim work of the whole run, including one entirely new RPS-level mechanic.** Full regression clean after fixing one real crash (below). Sim kept in sync throughout.

Print sheet regenerated.
- ~~`rules/combat-example.md`'s Cycle 1–2 draw sequence needed a real rewrite~~ — **closed 2026-07-23, see Recently shipped above.** Fill-draw order restructured so the Injury genuinely stays buried for the length of this fight, matching bottom-insertion; dice also brought current to the 2026-07-22 bump in the same pass.
- **A3 — Death implemented for real, and enemies now sometimes finish a downed target instead of always ignoring them.** Both gaps found by checking, not assumed: `death_floor()` (-ceil(max_hp/2), rules/combat.md's actual death threshold) existed on `Combatant` but was never once read anywhere — Death wasn't modeled at all, only Collapse was. And `enemies()`/`allies()` exclude collapsed combatants from their pools entirely, which every targeting and support-card function reads from — so before today, a collapsed teammate wasn't a rare finishing-blow risk, they were **completely untargetable**, permanently safe until healed, which can't be right either (rules/combat.md: "you are automatically hit by any attack targeting you" while Collapsed — that only means something if you can still be targeted). Built: `is_dead` flag on `Combatant`, set in both engines' `deal()` only when a hit lands on an *already*-Collapsed target and drops them to or below `death_floor()` (the existing single-hit-floor rule already protects the hit that causes the *initial* collapse, so this can only trigger on a second hit) — permanent, `heal()` now refuses outright on a dead target. Separately, `Battle.downed_enemies()` — a pool deliberately kept apart from `enemies()` so ordinary targeting/support logic still never sees collapsed characters — and `team_policies.py`'s `_pick_target` now rolls `FINISH_DOWNED_CHANCE` (25%, a real number chosen but not derived from anything — flagged as tunable) against that pool before falling back to normal focus-fire. Drew's framing, confirmed as the right shape before building: "a downed enemy should have a random chance of being targeted instead of following the same pile on idea." **Real bug caught mid-build, not shipped silently:** an old defensive guard in `team_engine.py`'s `take_turn` — `if target.collapsed: target = self.enemies(who)[0]` — silently redirected every attack on a downed target back onto a living one, a leftover from before downed-targeting was ever a real path. First test run came back at exactly 0 deaths across 20,000 battles despite the new targeting logic firing correctly (verified via direct instrumentation: `_pick_target` really was choosing downed targets ~25% of the time) — traced it to this guard, found and removed it, re-tested, deaths started appearing immediately.
- **Generative finding, not yet resolved: even-matchup PvP mirrors are wildly more lethal than Drew's 1-in-20-sessions target, and it isn't obviously a bug.** `team_run.py`'s Monte Carlo now reports per-side death counts and "a death occurred in X% of battles." Tested three roughly-balanced 3v3 mirrors (frost/steele, mire/frost, vanguard/frost) — all landed in the 45-62% chance of *some* death occurring, per single encounter, with average fight length ~20-22 turns. That's not a small miss against "roughly once every 20 *sessions*" — it's the same order of magnitude *per fight*. Read on why, not yet acted on: a flat 25% chance, rolled potentially many times against the same downed character over a 20+ turn fight (every time an enemy's turn comes up and that character is still down), compounds fast — 1-(0.75)^k climbs to majority odds well before k=10. Two live open questions for Drew, not decided here: (1) is 25% simply too high, or (2) is testing against symmetric player-mirror decks the wrong instrument in the first place, since real monster encounters would pit a CTR-calibrated creature deck against an actual party with real dedicated healing, not two copies of the same aggressive PvP archetype with no healer role. The roster has no monster-side decks yet to test the second question properly. Didn't guess at a fix — this needs your read on which lever it actually is.
- **A3 — Team targeting rebuilt around Drew's actual model: random pick, pile on, drop on collapse.** Replaced the flat 25%-chance-per-attack finishing-blow mechanic from the entry above outright, same session — Drew: "a teams plan is to randomly select a target and pile on. drop the targeting when an enemy collapses and add them back into the pool of random targets." New `Battle.team_target` — one shared lock per side, not per attacker, matching "we already have teams coordinate attacks": `_pick_target` returns the team's current lock as long as it's still standing; the moment it collapses, the lock clears and the next pick draws fresh and random from `Battle.targetable()` (living + Collapsed-not-dead, replacing the old `downed_enemies()` — a downed enemy is just back in the same pool, not hunted or spared specially, and if the random draw doesn't land back on them this round they simply don't get re-locked, since only a *living* pick establishes a new lock). Re-tested the same three mirrors from the entry above: death-occurred rate dropped from 45-62% to 41-48% — real, in the predicted direction, but still nowhere near "once per 20 sessions" at the per-encounter level. Drew, same message, already naming the actual caveat rather than needing it re-raised: "this is a heads up fight between equalish parties that we are testing" — these mirrors were never meant to validate the target number, only to prove the mechanism moves in the right direction, which it did. Whether it's close enough once real monster-vs-party encounters exist to test against is still open, not decided here. Win rates checked against established baselines (vanguard vs frost: 33.9%, essentially unchanged from the 33.6-33.7% baseline) — the targeting rework didn't quietly reshuffle deck balance along with the lethality fix.
- **The Patient Host built into the sim for real, and run 1v3 — the actual test case all of tonight's death-rate work was for.** Drew: "wanna see how the host does in a 1v3?" It wasn't wired at all before tonight (no boss/NPC signature card was, per established precedent) — building it required implementing its 5 signature cards (`YOUR TURN WILL COME`, `REGISTERED`, `NO VACANCY`, `THE LEDGER NEVER CLOSES`, `YOU'RE NEXT`) plus 10 core-fill cards its 24-card deck needed that had never been wired to *any* roster deck before (`STILLNESS`, `PREDICT`, `FOCUS`, `UNDERSTANDING`, `ENDURE`, `WEATHERED`, `STARING CONTEST`, `RECOVER`, `FLOW`, `SHADE AWAY` — STARING CONTEST and PREDICT's Sealed left fully unmodeled, same treatment as other hard-to-model effects; WEATHERED's delayed trigger left unmodeled too, matching ANTICIPATE's precedent). Added `Combatant(..., hp=None)` so a boss can override the formula-derived HP directly (66, per its bespoke-HP note) without disturbing how Body-adjust deltas still apply on top of it. Registered as `patient_host` in ROSTER.

  **Two real, pre-existing bugs found and fixed in the process of actually testing this, not before:**
  1. `policies.py`'s `scry_plan` had a `NameError: _card_power` in its "sabotage an enemy's deck" branch — dead code nobody had ever hit, because no existing card called `engine.scry(actor, owner, x)` with `actor != owner` until REGISTERED's Effect did, tonight, for the first time. Fixed: `est_damage(owner, c)` — threat should be evaluated from the deck-owner's stats, not the schemer's.
  2. `_apply_shift`'s bonus-turn handling crashed (`ValueError: 0 is not in list`) the first time a character took a *second* bonus turn from re-shifting themselves — YOU'RE NEXT's "+2 to self" chains bonus turns into more bonus turns over a long fight, something no existing card ever did before. Root cause: the wheel code detected "is this the character currently resolving their turn" via `queue.index(target) == 0`, which is only true for an ordinary turn — during a bonus turn the acting character isn't at the front of `queue` at all (that slot belongs to whoever's next in the untouched normal rotation). Fixed by tracking the actual resolving character explicitly (`engine._resolving`, set in `take_turn` for both engines) and checking `target is engine._resolving` alongside the existing `i == 0` — same already-documented rule ("the current actor is already at the earliest possible slot, so a positive shift becomes a bonus turn"), just no longer misdetected by queue position. Did not touch anything else in `_apply_shift` — its documented confirmed cases were left exactly as reasoned.

  **Results, real numbers, not rounded up or down:** Host alone vs. steele+sage+adept — **84.4% Host win rate**, a death occurred in **73.7%** of the 10,000 battles run. Vs. frost+steele+mire — **91.7%** Host win rate, **75.4%** death-occurred rate. Both far past anything resembling Drew's "once per 20 sessions" target — this isn't a rounding gap, it's an order of magnitude off. **Real caveats, not excuses:** these are generic PvP-archetype decks piloted by `team_tactician`, a solid but simple heuristic bot — not a party specifically built or played to counter a control/delay boss the way a real table might (focus it down fast, hold Resist/Evade proactively, coordinate around its Initiative Shift kit rather than trade blows into it). Whether the gap is the Host's actual calibration, the test party's makeup, or the bot's tactics is a real open question, not resolved here — but the number itself is real, verified, and worth Drew's eyes regardless of which of those it turns out to be.
- Bestiary deck backfill — every creature entry should gain a recommended full deck under the new size rule (local-model lane; Shrike is now the worked pattern in `bestiary/fencerow-shrike.md`). **Note for whoever picks this up:** per tonight's census, Weak (4 cards), Blind (7), and Deadly (7) are the least-represented keywords in the game — Drew: "let's make a note to try and use them next time we are making bestiary decks." Lean toward these three when a creature's kit has room, rather than defaulting back to Evade/Resist, which are already the most common by a wide margin (17 and 13 cards respectively).
- Armour → Resist swaps that got meaningfully weaker as literal conversions (Flapjack Octopus's Flatten identity, Trisect's Iron Chassis boss passive, TERRORMITE CAPSULE, CHEWFAT RATION, Gene-Thief Tardigrade's shell plating, Kaine's card) — Drew: "those changes are deep enough to require reworking. let's wait on them until they come back around naturally." Deliberately not touched until whichever of those pieces of content is being worked on directly again.
- EXHAUST's disposal was never actually implemented in the sim (only flagged as `is_status`, no seeding or removal logic) — so today's EXHAUST rewrite has zero sim regression risk, but if Exhaust ever gets modeled, model the new single-path rule (action removes all from hand), not the three-path one this replaced.
**[2026-07-19 12:57 AM CDT] Card review batch 6 — the last of Drew's full pass ("I made it all the way through the cards... 😤🫡 done"), twenty cards across `red-body.md` and `green-soul.md`, plus a keyword built from scratch that turned out to have never existed in the sim at all.** Full regression clean after (frost/steele/mire/volk/sage/adept/tempo/warden duels and vanguard/mire and steele/sage/adept team battles, plus the Patient Host 1v3 re-run) — none of these decks happen to run every touched card, so the untested ones (ADAPT, VOID, ACCEPTANCE, ROOTED OATH, DEAD RECKONING, FIELD MEDICINE) were additionally verified with direct scripted calls against real `Duel`/`Battle` objects, same standard as everything else this run.

- **A3 — Blind built into the sim for the first time.** Found while wiring DEAD RECKONING's Defensive Bonus ("Attacker gains Blind") — there was no `self.blind` attribute anywhere on `Combatant`, despite Blind being a real, defined glossary keyword already used by 11 cards (per the census two batches back that flagged it as one of the three least-represented keywords in the game). Not a wording gap, an actual missing mechanic. Built as the attacker's own stack, checked at the very top of `attack()` in both engines — resolves *before* Evade, since Blind belongs to the attacker's own turn, not the defender's reveal (matches the glossary's stated resolution order). 1d2 roll, same shape as Evade's own miss-chance, consumed on the attack regardless of whether it actually fails. Simplification flagged, not silently absorbed: modeled purely as a stack consumed on the next attack; the glossary's "or expires at the end of your next turn even if unused" wall-clock case isn't tracked. Verified directly: ~49.6% fail rate over 1,150 forced-Blind attacks across 300 duels, stack consumed exactly once per attack every time.
- **DIG IN** — Effect: "Anchored — while you remain in position, gain Resist 1" → "Anchored — Gain Resist 1" on both lines (identical now); the "while you remain in position" clause was redundant with what Anchored itself already means.
- **PRESS THE INJURY** — Effect changed from "the defender reveals their hand" to "the defender announces how many Injuries they have in hand and discard pile" — less information exposed, same +2-per-Injury payoff. Sim already only ever read the count (`remove_injuries`/`injuries_visible`), so this was a pure wording correction, nothing to change in `content.py`.
- **MIRROR STEP** — Effect: "you and target both move positions" → "you and defender both move positions." Wording only; still unmodeled for Quick same as REALIGNMENT (noted three batches back, still true, still one mechanic away from being fixable in one pass instead of two).
- **SUPPORT** — Effect: "target ally gains Deadly" (was a vaguer draw effect). Defensive Bonus: "target ally draws 2" (was 1, and picked arbitrarily — `allies[0]` — instead of by need). Both sides now pick via `_best_attacker(engine.allies(me))` instead of an arbitrary index.
- **ADAPT** — Effect: "instead of a tie, you win" — the second card added to the EQUAL FOOTING tie-override rule in `rps()`/`_rps()`, attacker-side only (EQUAL FOOTING works from either side; if both sides claim the tie, it cancels back to a tie, same logic as before). Verified directly: ADAPT vs. a same-color card that would normally tie now resolves to the attacker.
- **PATIENCE** — Defensive Bonus: "Anchored — target ally heals 3 HP" (was a flat self-heal with no Anchored clause). New ongoing kind `patience_def`, ticked the same way `_ongoing_support_tick` already handles every other Anchored ally-target effect.
- **WITNESS** — die d4→d2, both lines' heal bumped 3→6.
- **URGENCY** — Effect broadened from a single best-attacker target to "apply Initiative Shift +1 to all allies," per Drew's own question ("can we safely make the effect say all allies?") — checked against the wheel's shift math before applying: nothing about `initiative_shift` assumes a single target, it's just been called in a loop for every other all-allies card this run (ACCEPTANCE's new Defensive Bonus, below, does the same thing), so broadening it is the same shape as existing precedent, not a new risk.
- **BALANCE** — die d4→d2, both lines rewritten to "discard 1 to Stagger the defender / attacker" (was a color-matching discard mechanic). The now-orphaned `_has_color`/`_discard_one_color` helpers were removed rather than left dead.
- **SILENCE THE THREAD and SCRIPTURE OF ECHOES relocated from the shared core pool to `characters/kaine.md`** as PROMISE-tagged signature cards — full text preserved unchanged, just moved and tagged. Broke Masaharu's Green core list, which used SCRIPTURE OF ECHOES (a shared core card is no longer shared once it becomes a signature); repaired by substituting PATIENCE in, and corrected his own DEAD RECKONING description line to match this batch's new wording while in there.
- **VOID** — Effect: "defender gains Sealed" (Sealed itself stays unmodeled — no item-usage mechanic exists anywhere in the sim to seal, same gap PREDICT already carries).
- **TWIN STRIKE** — Defensive Bonus: "target enemy gains Weak" (was an ally-Deadly loop, i.e. was doing the wrong side's job entirely — this wasn't a nerf/buff, it was pointed at the wrong combatant).
- **RENEWAL** — die d4→d2, Defensive Bonus: "target Collapsed ally heals 6 HP," now actually reading `downed_allies()` (added this run for exactly this purpose) instead of nothing — Renewal's revival-heal shape finally does what its name says.
- **ACCEPTANCE** — Effect: "you may discard your hand, then draw that many cards" — built fresh (was never registered). Defensive Bonus: "all allies may move position or gain Initiative Shift +1" — the choice simplified to always-shift for the bot (a reposition choice a heuristic brain can get wrong in ways a shift can't), same simplification precedent as PATIENT_HOST's own card set already established.
- **SEED removed and archived**, name and flavor text preserved for reuse per Drew's explicit request ("let's remove SEED... save the name and flavor text for reuse. I think that's stored in the card compressor") — confirmed the convention lives in `experimental/archives/cut-cards.md` (per `archives/card-compression.md`'s own pointer) before filing it there rather than guessing at a new location.
- **SHARED BURDEN** — Effect: "gain Fortress and Evade" (was an ally-damage-redirect mechanic, replaced outright rather than patched).
- **ROOTED OATH** — Effect: "Anchored — target ally gains Deadly." Defensive Bonus: "Anchored — target ally gains Resist." **Real bug caught in the process, not shipped silently:** the `_ongoing_support_tick`'s `rooted_oath` branch had never actually been updated during the earlier session-wide next-attack-bonus→Deadly redirect, despite that earlier pass's own memory.md entry claiming ROOTED OATH was included in it — it was still ticking the old, cut `next_attack_bonus` mechanic this whole time. Fixed now, both `rooted_oath` and the new `rooted_oath_def` kinds redirect to a stored `'target'` ally, ticked the same way PATIENCE's new Anchored effect is. Verified directly in a real `Battle` with two live allies: both Deadly and Resist land on the target ally, not the caster.
- **MOCKERY** — Effect: "apply Initiative Shift -2 to defender" (wording: target → defender, matching the established pattern from ERODE two batches back — the underlying function already read the correct combatant either way, this was a pure text correction).
- **FIELD MEDICINE** — built fresh on both lines (was never registered in `build_cards()` at all — caught only by grepping for its registration, not assumed present). Effect: "remove all Injuries from target ally's hand and discard pile, they heal 3 HP" (picks the ally with the most visible Injuries). Defensive Bonus: same, self-targeted.
- **DEAD RECKONING** — Effect: "defender gains Weak." Defensive Bonus: "attacker gains Blind" — the card that surfaced the missing-keyword gap above. Also never registered in `build_cards()` before tonight; added alongside FIELD MEDICINE.

Both `red-body.md` and `green-soul.md` print sheets regenerated (`card-print-core.html`, 10 pages, unchanged page count).

**Early combat-simulator findings, roster: frost/steele/mire only, "Wound" terminology, team sim not yet built.** Moved here 2026-08-01 during a full `memory.md` audit — this was sitting in "Campaign Status" as current-state text but had gone fully stale: "Wound" was renamed to "Injury" system-wide long ago (zero live references remain in `rules/`, `cards/`, or `combatsimulations/`), the roster grew from 3 decks to 22, and "the real goal is a full TEAM-vs-TEAM simulator... not expanding to teams yet" was superseded by `team_engine.py`/`team_policies.py`/`team_run.py`, all built and in heavy use since. The underlying findings (recency-beats-frequency, Axiom as a real edge not an auto-win, deck-tracking being situational not predictive, stat-maxing as a self-correcting trap) were real and are presumably still directionally true, but the specific numbers cited are from a much smaller, now-superseded roster and haven't been re-run. `Campaign Status` now carries a short, current-accurate replacement instead.

**Combat simulator** (`combatsimulations/`)
PvP duel engine, Python, no dependencies. Roster: frost, steele, mire (pluggable via `ROSTER` in content.py; run.py takes deckA polA deckB polB). Four brains: random/reader/greedy/tactician. Design instrument only — not canon. Outputs: errata queue (`rulings-log.md`, printed each run) + balance stats.

Corrected findings (an earlier draft tested with the weak `reader` brain and wrongly concluded Frost dominates):
- **Recency beats frequency.** greedy (predicts foe's LAST color) crushes reader (predicts MOST-COMMON color) head to head — 59/41 Frost mirror, 90/10 Mire mirror. reader is the weakest non-random brain.
- **Deck ranking under strongest brain (tactician): Steele > Mire > Frost.** Steele's Body 4 raw stats win (red damage + balanced colors, not HP).
- **Axiom is a real edge, not an "I win."** Valuing Axiom wins the Frost mirror 59% and lifts Frost vs Steele 38.8%→43.5%, but does NOT flip Steele's stat advantage. (Earlier "Axiom > Paradox, Frost dominates" was overstated — Axiom helps, stats win.)
- **Anti-read color flattening FAILS** — only helps a deck whose off-colors are as strong as its main color; cut from tactician.
- Initiative ~52–55% under strong play.
- **Mire is bottom-tier in PvP** (loses ~70% to both Frost and Steele). Home is PvE, where durable enemies give the Wound engine its long game.
- **Deck-tracking is situational, not predictive.** A pure `tracker` brain (predict next color from deck-minus-discard) FAILED (~85% loss) — tiny reshuffling decks make "what's left" a bad predictor. Removed. But tracking's narrow CERTAIN use — safe-play detection (if all copies of a color are visible in the foe's discard, an attack that color can't beat is risk-free) — is real: folded into the tactician (`_color_exhausted`), lifted it over greedy across every deck (Frost 59→65%, Mire 50→58%). Lesson: track to know when you can't lose, not to guess what's coming.
tactician = best brain (greedy recency-read + aggression + Axiom/Spark weighting + situational exhaustion safe-play).

**Wound rule (Drew):** Wounds no longer auto-discard — sit in hand occupying a slot until an action discards one (→ discard pile) or short rest. Short-rest removal: permanently destroy 1 Wound from HAND or DISCARD only (never deck — no tracking/searching hidden Wounds). Press the Wound counts HAND + DISCARD only (not deck), for the same reason. Debuff scope: stat reduction + status-card infliction ARE debuffs (Ward blocks); discard + scry-your-deck are NOT (ignore Ward; Obscure answers those). Stat change drives derived value live: Body→maxHP (only Body touches HP), Mind→hand size (forces discard if over), Soul→initiative; both directions. All in glossary + sim.

**Self-Wound cost REMOVED from stat-reduction cards (Drew):** Sunder (Mind), Erode (Soul), Wither (Body) no longer "Shuffle 1 Wound into your deck" — that self-cost was crippling under persistent Wounds. Sim confirmed: Mire went ~23% → 59.7% vs Frost. New deck ranking under tactician: Steele > Mire > Frost (Mire jumped Frost). Wound-removal cards (Press def, Taint def, Field Medicine, Shed Skin) now say "destroy" (permanent) not "exile" (which returns at combat end), and remove from hand/discard only. Clean design loop: sim flagged bug → card fix → sim confirmed.

**Stat-maxing finding:** stat-maxing is a TRAP, not a balance problem — the system self-corrects on two axes. Tested a Body-5 red-heavy deck ("volk" in roster): loses to balanced Frost 21%, crushed 89% by the anti-mono `punisher` brain (hoards the counter color to the foe's dominant color — Drew's idea, it works). Mono-color → hard-countered by RPS. Balanced colors on a 5/2/2 line → off-stat cards hit for Mind/Soul 2, toothless (~40-48% vs field). The actually-strong build is Steele's 4/3/2 (high primary + viable secondary color), which is a spread, not a max. If anything's too strong it's the moderate spread, and the lever would be Body's double-dip (damage + HP), not stat allocation. `punisher` and `volk` added to sim.

**Sim next step:** the real goal is a full TEAM-vs-TEAM simulator. Current 1v1 is step one — some cards (Mockery taunt, Partition shield, ally buffs) correctly do nothing in a duel and wait for the team sim. Not expanding to teams yet.
