## Standing Reasoning, archived 2026-08-06 (first Sync-mode sweep)

Moved wholesale from `memory.md`'s "Standing Reasoning," per the CLAUDE.md Work Modes split adopted 2026-08-06: closed at Sync cadence (fully absorbed into the canon text each explains, no longer cited as precedent, no open follow-up thread). Preserved exactly as originally written, same as the rest of this file — a record of past reasoning, not a current-state reference. Checked against the live file first: none of these are still pointed to by name from `CLAUDE.md`, `unresolved-concerns.md`, or another `memory.md` entry.

**[2026-08-05] Anchored's fix, and why the bug wasn't where I'd have looked for it.** My own first read of GRAPPLE's "Anchored — Defender gains Rooted" flagged the card as misusing the keyword, because the glossary's own text ("You gain a specific benefit...") reads as self-only. Drew corrected the actual bug's location: the glossary was wrong, not the card. The fastest way to be sure before touching the file was checking whether the mechanic already worked this way anywhere in shipped canon — it did, twice (`ROOTED OATH` targets a named ally, `IRON GRIP` shows the plain-Rooted-vs-Anchored distinction directly, right on the same card). This is the same shape of catch this session already made once with the HP formula and the distance-exemption list: a rule that's *documented* narrower than what's actually *implemented* is itself a kind of drift, and the fix belongs in the documentation, not in bending the new content to match a description that was never fully accurate. I'd defaulted to assuming the newer, less-tested thing (a hand-pushed experimental card) was more likely wrong than the older, load-bearing thing (a keyword definition four other cards depend on) — usually the right prior, wrong here, and worth naming so it isn't the automatic assumption next time either.
  - **Held AID back rather than either shipping it or cutting it outright.** SUPPORT and AID aren't a clean duplicate (AID's numbers differ), which made it tempting to ship both as "close enough, more variety." The mechanical-identity check (red-team.md's own #4: does this create a decision no other card creates?) says variety in stat totals isn't the same as variety in decision space — AID doesn't ask a player to weigh anything SUPPORT doesn't already ask. Showing the side-by-side rather than rendering a verdict kept the call where it belonged; this wasn't ambiguous enough to need Drew's design judgment, but concrete-enough evidence that he could see the actual overlap instead of taking "these are similar" on faith. *(AID itself shipped 2026-08-05, reworked to clear this exact overlap — see `memory.md`, Recently Shipped. This entry's "held back" framing describes the state at the time it was written, not AID's current status.)*

**[2026-08-05] Root Heart Overgrowth — a deliberate closure done right, and a connection that was there to find, not invent.** `characters/aege.md`'s "not established" framing was itself flagged once already this session (2026-08-04, landing the Holdfast) as a deliberately-open thread — the redefinition test's whole point is catching a closure like this *before* it ships silently. This time it's not silent: Drew named the cause himself, directly, across two separate turns of conversation (first confirming the overgrowth cycle is the "why," then confirming the Masons' line is *why the cycle turned inward specifically*). My job was execution quality, not gatekeeping a call that was never mine to gate.
  - **The sinkhole connection wasn't assumed — it was checked before being asserted.** Before touching `factions/the-masons.md`, read `quests/shifting-burrow.md`'s GM Secret in full: the Briarwatch line already has one documented, unknown-to-the-Masons consequence (a failed underground pillar causing the sinkhole), stated in almost the exact register Drew's new cause needed (*"The Masons don't know yet. Nobody has told them..."*). Recognizing that Drew's new cause was a *second instance of the same pattern* — one containment line, blocked pressure, an unintended consequence nobody's traced — rather than a coincidence worth ignoring is what turned this from "a boss gets a new ability" into "two campaign threads that already existed turn out to share a root cause." That's a real find, not manufactured connective tissue — the sinkhole passage was sitting there, fully written, before this conversation started.
  - **Matched the existing "Masons don't know" register on purpose rather than inventing new phrasing for the second consequence.** Two unrelated-looking failures from the same cause is a stronger read if both are described the same way structurally (a containment line doing exactly its job, an unplanned-for cost nobody's connected) — echoing the wording risked feeling repetitive, but distinct phrasing would have undersold that they're the same *kind* of secret, not just two secrets that happen to share a source.
  - **Confirmed the mechanical design wasn't disproportionate before shipping it.** A boss's core aggression getting a real cause could easily have justified a bigger swing — new keyword, new card, a stat rework. Checked against `rules/equipment.md`'s own "boss may go bespoke, marked explicitly" precedent and `CLAUDE.md`'s Do Not list (don't invent mechanics in isolation): extending Retaliation Phase's existing threshold trigger with a flat, escalating Pulse bonus reuses a mechanism the file already had rather than adding a second system next to it. HP/CTR/deck untouched on purpose — the fiction changed; the fight's basic shape didn't need to.

**[2026-08-05] The "tricky language" addition and the coastline rule — a real redefinition-test check, not a rubber stamp.** Drew's framing (*"it's a wish, a manifestation"*) implies the working reaches the Unheld's influence into the ordinary world, and `world/the-unheld.md` has a hard, specific rule that cuts directly against that: the Unheld cannot cross the coastline by any means, cannot be carried, "does not travel upstream." Read carelessly, landing this as written canon fact would flatly contradict that rule on the same page it cites it. The resolution wasn't to soften Drew's idea or silently drop the tension — it was to notice that Wild Magic is already established as uncontrolled and unpredictable (the ritual bathing section: *"The experience is not uniform and cannot be predicted"*), so framing the working as an *attempted* route around a rule everyone else in the setting respects, with genuinely unconfirmed results, uses an axis the file already had rather than inventing an exception to the coastline rule. This is the shape of check the redefinition test (`CLAUDE.md`, Translation Principle) is for: catching a collision before it ships, not refusing the idea because a collision exists.

**[2026-08-05] The unplaced epigraph quote, placed — and the distinction that matters between this and a wrong guess.** Leaving the quote out of canon rather than inventing a source for it wasn't a hedge that turned out unnecessary — it was the correct call at the time, since nothing in the source file said who spoke it, and `world/the-unheld.md`'s own established rule (only two kinds of people interact with the Unheld at all: Glasslight cartographers and the People of Promise) meant a wrong guess had real odds of colliding with something already on the page. Drew supplying the missing fact directly is what unblocked it, not a change in method. The line placed clean on the first attempt precisely because it had been left for him to answer rather than resolved by a guess that would have needed unwinding if it landed on the wrong faction.

**[2026-08-04] Future-Lock — the second Canon Gate pass caught something the first one's method wouldn't have, because the mechanic itself, not just the prose, needed checking against what already exists.** Drew: *"do the future-lock wasp rewrite next."*
  - **Recognized a mechanical fork before touching a file, not after.** The draft describes Future-Lock as if the keyword already exists ("*In combat terms it is the keyword Future-Lock*"), when what it's actually proposing is a wholesale swap of the underlying mechanic — status-card deck contamination with real-time clearing and rescue-based paralysis, versus a direct hand-size debuff reusing CLIMB's own engine hook. `experimental/README.md`'s own rule — *"No new keywords introduced without discussion first"* — exists for exactly this shape of change. Asked before writing anything, rather than treating "do the rewrite" as license to pick a mechanic unilaterally just because a draft existed.
  - **The real find came from reading the glossary's existing Stat Change section, not from the draft at all.** That section already guarantees hand size never drops below 2 from Mind loss — *"hand size never drops below 2, however far Mind falls."* Future-Lock, as approved (hand-size reduction with a paralysis floor), needs to go to 0. Neither the draft nor Drew's approval of the design direction addressed this, because neither was checking Future-Lock against a rule elsewhere in the same glossary — they were designing the mechanic in isolation. This is the same shape of catch as Aege's open-question closure: something a same-file read cannot surface, only a read against the rest of canon can.
  - **Resolved as a stated exception, not a silent one, and the distinction is the point.** Future-Lock isn't a Mind stat loss — it's a direct hand-size modifier via the ongoing-effects system, the same category CLIMB's +1 already lives in, which never touches Mind at all. That's a real, principled reason the two don't have to share a floor. Written into the glossary entry itself (*"No floor at 2. This is not a Mind loss"*) so a future reader hits the explanation at the point of the exception, not a contradiction they have to go hunting for the reasoning behind.
  - **Confirmed no simulator work was needed rather than assuming either way.** The wasp is explicitly GM-table content (*"Not a combat encounter. An environmental hazard"*), and `combatsimulations/content.py` had zero Future-Lock references before this pass. Checked directly rather than inferring from the file's own framing — a hazard file *saying* it's non-combat doesn't guarantee nothing downstream expects it to be simulable.
  - **`experimental/weald` trimmed once both its patches shipped**, keeping only the two threads that didn't — the rejected diet/clothing extrapolation and the deferred Root Heart "overgrowth phase" claim — so a future read of that file doesn't re-present settled work as still open.

**[2026-08-04] The two audit suggestions, ratified rather than left as suggestions — and one of them needed proof, not just agreement.** Drew: *"rename CERTAIN CONTACT to CERTAIN STRIKE. remove the ward phrase."* Both were things I'd proposed rather than found broken, so the bar before executing was different from a bug fix: confirm the suggestion still holds up under a second look, not just that it was requested.
  - **UNMAKE's fix could have been wrong in a specific, checkable way, and I checked it before touching the card.** My original suggestion was "the qualifier is redundant unless it's meant to signal a real, currently-undocumented Ward interaction — in which case the fix belongs in the glossary, not on this one card." Removing the phrase without checking would have been gambling on my own hedge. `remove_positive_status()` has no Ward check anywhere in its body, and UNMAKE's `_unmake_effect`/`_unmake_defense` call it plain, with no bypass logic layered on top — so "ignoring Ward" was never live behavior for any card, not just this one. The phrase was safe to delete outright, and the code needed no change, which is itself the confirmation that the suggestion was right rather than an assumption that it was.
  - **The rename swept eight files, not one** — `cards/red-body.md`, four bestiary decks (`alignment-marshal`, `minotaur`, `trisect`, `gluttony-abomination`), `cards/wall-reader.md` and `bestiary/wall-reader.md`'s promotion history, and `combatsimulations/{content,engine}.py` (the `add()` call, the function name, two comments, one `keyword:resist:self` frozenset entry). Wall-Reader's own file already carried a layered rename history (*NEVER LIFTED* → *CERTAIN CONTACT* on promotion) — added to it rather than overwriting it, matching the SIDESTEP/SWAY/DUST precedent from earlier tonight: the old name stays visible in exactly the two spots making a historical claim (`content.py`'s promotion comment, `memory.md`'s range-pass entry), swapped everywhere else.
  - **Checked character files for restated text before calling either done** — the same habit that caught six stale FOCUS/PROFILE/FLOW restatements two passes ago. Clean this time; nothing restates either card's text outside `cards/` and `bestiary/`.

**[2026-08-04] CLIMB re-ranged to Melee against its own flavor text — executed as instructed, flagged rather than quietly absorbed or refused.** Drew: *"CLIMB range: Melee."* CLIMB's flavor line is *"The higher you rise, the farther you see"* — a vantage-point image, seeing into the distance, which is the Ranged fantasy almost word-for-word. Locking it to Melee reads as the same shape of mismatch as several findings the same-session core-set audit was built to catch (a card's own name/flavor pointing one way, its mechanics pointing another). The difference here is directionality: those were bugs found and reported *up*; this is an instruction handed *down*. Executed without alteration — a direct instruction on a card's own field isn't a bug for me to correct — but named plainly rather than treated as if the tension doesn't exist, since staying quiet about it would mean the audit's own standard doesn't apply when the mismatch is one I created rather than found.

**[2026-08-03] Three cards reworked to Initiative Shift, translated from Drew's shorthand into the corpus's own established phrasing rather than copied literally.** Drew gave three lines: *"INTERRUPT effect: Gain Initiative Shift +2 / DISTRACT effects: Target gains Initiative Shift -2 / FOCUS bonus: Gain Initiative Shift+2."*
  - **"Gain X" and "Target gains X" are conversational shorthand, not intended final card text — the corpus already settled this question for a different keyword and the same reasoning carries over.** Every Initiative Shift card already in the game — INTERRUPT's own untouched Defensive Bonus, DELAY, QUICKEN, REBUTTAL, DOUBLE DOWN, URGENCY — reads "Apply Initiative Shift ±N to `<target>`," never "gains." That verb is reserved for status-token grants (Vulnerable, Weak, Blind, Rooted) that route through `apply_*()` helpers; Initiative Shift moves a counter directly via `engine.initiative_shift()` and has never once been phrased as something a combatant "gains." Wrote all three cards in the established verb, not Drew's shorthand — the same move already made explicit in this file once before, when eight older cards' "Target gains X" wording was corrected to "Defender gains X"/"Attacker gains X" because "the code was always bound to the current RPS opponent... the wording was simply wrong."
  - **Field labels disambiguated which half of each card changes, and the grammar (singular vs. plural) did real work.** "INTERRUPT **effect**" (singular) → Effect only, Defensive Bonus untouched. "FOCUS **bonus**" (singular) → Defensive Bonus only, Effect untouched. "DISTRACT **effects**" (plural, the one word that differs from the other two) → both Effect and Defensive Bonus, read as the deliberate signal it appears to be rather than a typo.
  - **What each card gave up.** INTERRUPT's Effect was real turn-denial — `foe.skip_turns += 1` plus `me.cannot_defend = True`, forcing a lost turn and self-locking defense — replaced with a plain self +2. That is a genuine power reduction on the attacking half of the card, done on a direct instruction rather than measured or questioned; DISTRACT's Sealed-grant Effect was already unmodeled in the sim (no item-usage mechanic exists to gate), so its replacement is a straight upgrade from doing nothing to doing something. FOCUS's Defensive Bonus (discard-to-deck resequencing) becomes a clean tempo grant, in line with its Effect (Scry 2) already being about seeing ahead rather than hitting hard.
  - **`skip_turns` is now dead code in the engine, worth knowing rather than silently discovering later.** `engine.py` calls it *"lost turns (Interrupt)"* in its own comment — INTERRUPT was its only grantor. No card sets it anymore; the read side (`engine.py`/`team_engine.py`'s turn-skip check) still exists and still works, just never triggers. Left in place rather than removed, since ripping out a whole mechanism wasn't asked for and a future card may want it.
  - **Verified below the level `check_sim` reaches.** The canon-reconciliation check only compares color/stat/range/die — it has no idea what an Effect or Defensive Bonus *does*, so a card whose text changed to something the callback doesn't implement would pass clean. Called each new `_effect`/`_defense` function directly first (confirms no runtime error, no KeyError, no missing engine hook) and then ran 1000 full duels per card through the actual reader-policy AI, not just the isolated callback — a card can execute standalone and still crash once real policy decision-making and RPS resolution are in the loop.

**[2026-08-03] CENSER SWING fixed the same way as GORE, at a proportionally smaller die cut — a judgment call worth stating as one.** Drew: *"go ahead and fix CENSER SWING."* No numbers specified this time, unlike GORE where Drew gave the exact die himself.
  - **The range fix is identical and non-optional: Melee → Both.** Same diagnosis as GORE — *"if target is Frontline"* on a Melee-only card checks something Melee's own legality already guarantees. Moving to Both is the only change that makes the sentence true instead of decorative.
  - **The die cut is not identical, and copying GORE's number would have been the lazy answer, not the right one.** GORE's kicker is `+d6`, average 3.5 — losing it roughly half the time (once Backline becomes a legal target) costs about 1.75 average damage, and Drew's own d8→d4 cut removes 2.0 average from the base, a close match. CENSER SWING's kicker is a flat `+2` — the same reasoning caps the compensating cut at roughly 1.0 average, which is one die step (d8, avg 4.5 → d6, avg 3.5), not two (→ d4, avg 2.5). Cutting to d4 anyway, just because GORE did, would have imported GORE's specific balance answer onto a card with a different-sized problem.
  - **No simulator involvement, unlike GORE — confirmed before editing, not assumed.** `cards/vescal.md` and Vescal's whole signature deck have no `add()` calls, no damage functions, no `ROSTER` entry anywhere in `combatsimulations/content.py` — only DUST (Vescal's cut SMOKE SCREEN, promoted to core earlier this session) ever crossed into the sim. So this was a canon-text-only fix: no `_censer_swing_damage` to keep in sync with a declared die (the exact two-places-not-one trap GORE's fix had to route around), no Oracle collision (signature cards were never pool-eligible), nothing to regenerate beyond whatever print sheet might reference it (none do — Vescal has no dedicated print sheet in `generate-all.sh`'s `CARD_SETS`). The smallest version of this fix shape, and worth recording as the baseline against which GORE's was the complicated case.
  - **`unresolved-concerns.md`'s CENSER SWING line is deleted, not struck through** — the file's own convention: it shows the present, not history. The history lives here.

**[2026-08-03] Flavor-text font size increased, all print sheets, verified against the single most text-dense card in the set rather than trusted from the CSS numbers alone.** Drew: *"increase the font size for all cards flavor text."*
  - **One CSS edit, eight downstream sheets.** `printing/generate-cards.py` defines `.flavor`'s font size exactly three times — the base rule and the `.dense`/`.denser` auto-shrink tiers a card's own text length triggers (`weight = sum(len(field) for field in [...])`, `> 195` → dense, `> 285` → denser). All three sheets — core, briarwatch, mason, frost, steele, oracle, items, items-field — are generated from this one template, so one edit reached every printed card in the repo without touching a single `cards/*.md` file. Base 8pt → 9pt, dense 7pt → 8pt, denser 6.2pt → 7pt: clean 1pt (or near-1pt) steps that preserve the density hierarchy — a text-heavy card's flavor line still shrinks relative to a sparse card's, just less than before.
  - **Checked the actual risk, not just the number.** A fixed-size printed card (`60mm × 84mm`, `overflow: hidden`) with a larger font can silently clip text with no error anywhere in the pipeline — `check_print` only diffs whether the HTML changed, it has no idea whether what changed still fits inside its own box. Computed every core card's text weight to find the genuine worst case (`FOLLOW-UP`, colorless, 410 characters — well past the denser threshold and the single densest card in the entire core set), isolated its rendered page, and screenshotted it with the pre-installed headless Chromium rather than trusting the CSS arithmetic. Flavor line fully visible, inside the border, no clipping. A verification that reads "the numbers look reasonable" is not the same as a verification that looked at the rendered output.

**[2026-08-03] GORE's effect never had a real condition, because its own Range made the condition true by construction.** Drew asked how it worked; the honest answer was "it doesn't, quite." *"If target is Frontline, deal +d6 additional damage,"* printed on a card whose own Range is Melee — and Melee is legal only when both combatants are already Frontline (`rules/combat.md`, Range Matrix). GORE could never legally be played against a target that wasn't Frontline, so the "if" could never resolve false. The simulator (`_gore_damage`) implemented this literally: `if foe.position == 'frontline': base += roll(6, ...)`, a branch that always fires whenever the function runs at all.
  - **Not a one-off — found a twin while diagnosing it.** `cards/vescal.md`'s CENSER SWING has the identical shape: Range Melee, *"If target is Frontline, deal +2 damage."* Same vacuous check, same root cause. Reported alongside GORE's diagnosis; both are now fixed — see the CENSER SWING entry above.
  - **Drew's fix makes the sentence true rather than rewriting it.** Range → Both, base die d8 → d4. No wording change anywhere — moving to Both is exactly what turns *"if target is Frontline"* back into a real, sometimes-false condition (the target can now legitimately be Backline when GORE is played), and the die drop is what pays for that: a guaranteed d8 becomes a floor of d4 with a genuine — not guaranteed — d6 kicker on top. Verified at the object level, not just by reading the code: forced `foe.position` to `'backline'` and `'frontline'` across 2000 samples each and confirmed the observed damage ranges match exactly (4–7 Backline, 5–13 Frontline) — the branch really is conditional now, not just printed that way.
  - **The checker cannot see a mismatch inside a custom damage function, and that had to be handled by hand.** `check_sim`'s canon reconciliation only compares the *declared* base die (`add()`'s 5th positional argument) against the card's printed `Attack:` line. `_gore_damage` computed its own roll with a hardcoded `roll(8, engine.rng)` that never referenced that parameter — updating only the `add()` call would have left `check_sim` green while the simulator kept silently rolling d8 underneath. Both places changed together; verified by measurement (the runtime damage range), not by the check passing, since the check structurally can't catch this class of drift.
  - **The fix collided with the Oracle's own pinned composition, and the resolution mirrors last pass's STILLNESS/REBUTTAL swap exactly.** GORE was one of the Oracle's fixed Red Melee-12 (`Oracle/baseoracledeck.md`, set 2026-08-03 earlier the same day). Moving it to Both would have silently dropped Red to 11/7/3. Swapped in **INTERCEPT** — a plain *Gain Protect and Resist 2* grant on both sides, the same shape as GUARD and PAIN IS FUEL already in the pool, and notably a card that was *cut* from this exact pool on 2026-08-01 for redundancy at a 20-card size. GORE's departure reopened exactly the slot INTERCEPT was trimmed from — a closed loop, not a coincidence chased for its own sake.
  - **Measured the aggregate effect rather than assuming the die drop dominates.** A levelled duel series (GORE plus generic filler vs. a mirrored control, 20,000 paired seeds) showed the fixed version (d4/Both) at 34.2% against the as-printed version (d8/Melee) at 33.0% — a small **+1.2 point** net gain, meaning Both's added flexibility outweighs the smaller guaranteed floor at this stat level. Reported, not acted on; Drew set both numbers deliberately in the same instruction.

**[2026-07-23 6:36 AM CDT] A real methodological caveat on tonight's own testing, given directly, worth remembering every time a sim number gets cited going forward: "the coreset I keep a tight leash on... Frost and Steele were made by my daughter and Kevin... so they are excellent comparison points for you because the bestiary and NPC decks as well as any other decks in the sims are all built by you with varying degrees of input from me but none have been given a precision deck-building pass by me."** Checked against the real files, not just taken on faith: `testcampaigndecks/frost.md` (Player: Ollie) and `testcampaigndecks/steele.md` (Player: Kevin) confirm it — real people, playing real characters, building real decks, and `content.py`'s `FROST_DECK`/`STEELE_DECK` match those files' actual decklists exactly. Crimson and Sky (Drew's own, from `playtesting/live-test-2v2.md`) are the same category — real, human-built — though their sim-usable form is a flagged reconstruction (their real decks were deliberately hidden during that live test and never fully recorded; already noted honestly as such, above, and not in `content.py`'s ROSTER at all).
  - **The actual asymmetry, stated plainly**: Frost and Steele are the one real ground-truth pair in the whole simulator — decks an actual skilled player built for their actual character, no algorithmic shortcut anywhere in them. Every other deck the sim runs — mire, volk, sage, adept, warden, vanguard, tempo, garret/brasca/wyn/tallis, the Patient Host itself, every bestiary/NPC kit — is mine, built with Drew's input at varying depth, but never given the kind of deliberate optimization pass a real player gives their own sheet.
  - **What this means for tonight's own numbers, specifically**: the party-vs-Patient-Host test (garret/brasca/wyn/tallis, naively Oracle-drafted by my own heuristic, vs. a Patient Host deck I also built) pit two non-precision decks against each other. The 78% Host win rate is real data about what happened in that specific matchup — it is NOT clean evidence about the Host's balance in isolation, because neither side got a real deckbuilder's pass. A test that actually isolates the Host's own tuning would need either Frost/Steele themselves (or decks built with equivalent real care) on the other side of the table — not Claude-drafted stand-ins on both sides. Doesn't invalidate tonight's REGISTERED fix (that was about the card's own text matching its own stat, not about relative win rate), but it's a real caveat on how much weight the win-rate numbers themselves should carry, and it applies to every non-Frost/Steele matchup this session ran, not just the newest one.
  - **Practice worth adopting, Drew's own suggestion**: he cites his inspirations constantly and directly (Labyrinth, Made in Abyss, Delicious in Dungeon, `world/tonal-bible.md` and elsewhere) — the standing counter-example to tonight's "no telling" problem on the Patient Host's own name. When a naming or design choice has a real, nameable influence behind it, say so at the time, in the ship note — cheap insurance against exactly the unrecoverable-provenance problem from two entries up.

*(This entry's operative conclusion — Frost/Steele are the sim's one real ground-truth pair, read future numbers accordingly — stays live and citable at `memory.md`'s short standing-caveat version, "Frost/Steele are a comparison anchor that erodes with every card change." This longer entry is the provenance/verification trail behind that caveat, not a second copy of the rule itself.)*

---

## Standing Reasoning, archived 2026-08-06 (second Sync-mode sweep)

**[2026-08-03] HP back to (3 × Body) + 6 — a reversion, and I flagged it as one before building.** Drew: *"max HP= 3xBody + 6."* (A3.)
  - **This undoes a documented decision, which is why it got named rather than just executed.** `archives/key-design-decisions.md` records the opposite move: *"HP formula: (2 × Body) + 9 (changed from 3×Body+6 — Drew, via sim experiment). Flatter curve to decouple HP from Body and reduce Body's damage+HP double-dip."* Body drives red damage dice *and* HP, so a steeper HP curve makes one stat pay twice. The only live trace of that reasoning was a comment in `engine.py`; the rest was archived. Stated to Drew, then built — it is his formula and his jurisdiction, and it is one commit from undone.
  - **The measurement moved my own position, and that is the entry's point.** Frost (Body 3) vs Steele (Body 4), 20,000 paired seeds, both formulas: **old 62.2% / 35.8%, new 59.3% / 38.9%.** The steeper curve hands the higher-Body deck **+3.1 points** — the double-dip is real and measurable. But it moves the matchup **toward** even, not away, because the imbalance now runs the other direction. My concern was correct in mechanism and wrong in direction.
  - **The archived premise is stale and should not be trusted as-is.** That note was written when *"Steele's ~66% dominance"* was the problem being solved. Steele now loses at 35.8% under the very formula introduced to rein him in — later work (the damage-distribution rebalance, the global dice bump, the color-range differentiation) overshot past the target. An archived finding is a record of a decision, not a standing fact about today's numbers.
  - **The change lives entirely at the tails.** Body 3 gives 15 either way, so the player baseline never moved and 14 of 46 formula blocks are untouched. Everything else spreads: Body 1 drops 11→9, Body 8 rises 25→30. Frail things get frailer and heavy things get heavier — which is what "Body is doubly valuable" means at the table.
  - **Three consequences the sweep surfaced that a numeric find-and-replace would have missed.** (1) `bestiary/the-first-bundle.md` states its phase rule as *"each time its HP crosses a quarter of its maximum"* and then hard-codes the quarters; 21 → 24 means the thresholds move 16/11/6 → 18/12/6, and 24 divides evenly where 21 never did. (2) `bestiary/skeinwing.md` restated *"HP 11"* in prose a paragraph below its own block. (3) **The Minotaur stopped being bespoke** — HP 24 at Body 6 is now exactly the formula, so its *"(bespoke — boss exception; formula baseline is 24)"* marker was declaring a departure from a number it matched. Marker removed. The formula moved to meet the creature.
  - **`rules/combat-example.md` needed real arithmetic, not a substitution.** The worked fight carries an after-action ledger. Damage dealt is unchanged (stats and dice untouched), so only the maxima move and the remainders follow: Steele 17/17 → 18/18, Scratchers 4/11 and 3/11 → 2/9 and 1/9. Frost is Body 3 and does not move at all. Checked first that the Scratchers leave on a GM behaviour call rather than an HP threshold, so nothing in the narrative depended on the old numbers.
  - **Absolute low-HP thresholds were left alone deliberately.** Jackalopes flee at 1 HP, Borrowers burrow at 3, the Bone Collector retreats at 5. Those read as "nearly dead" markers rather than fractions, and the fractions barely moved (5/17 → 5/18). Only the First Bundle states a proportional rule outright, and only it was rescaled.

*(Doubly superseded now — this entry explains a reversion to (3×Body)+6, which the 2026-08-06 formula change replaced with (3×Body)+Soul+Mind. Kept as a record of the Body-double-dip reasoning and the sweep discipline it modeled, not as a description of any formula still in effect.)*

## Work Log — The Trail

Drew: *"let's restructure. put the memory.md work log into archives... the work logs are the trail, and key design decisions explains how we got our answers."* Moved wholesale on 2026-08-11, preserved exactly as originally written — this is the ship-by-ship chronological record, distinct from the distilled decisions above. **As of 2026-08-11, this is the live destination, not a one-time historical batch** — Drew: *"recently shipped should be an archive thing."* Every new Authority 1/2 ship gets logged here directly going forward, at the top, newest-first — no more staging in `memory.md` first and moving at Sync. Review still works the same way: Drew reads from the top whenever he catches up; objecting means reverting the commit, same as always.

**[2026-08-11] A2 — Stats → Aspects repo-wide rename scoped and queued for Sync mode, not executed now.** Drew: *"Rename stats to Aspects in sync mode."* Confirms the full rename flagged as deferred in the prior ship — but explicitly for Sync cadence, per `CLAUDE.md`'s Work Modes. Researched the real scope ahead of time so the eventual Sync pass doesn't start cold: **86 files, ~514 word-boundary `\bstats?\b` occurrences** (the raw substring count, 162 files/1,417 hits, is mostly unrelated `state`/`status`/`static`/`statue`/`estate` and isn't the real target). Real complications a blind find-replace would break: Python identifiers (`combatsimulations/`'s `COLOR_TO_STAT`, `Card.stat`, per-archetype `*_STATS` dicts; `agent-tools/verify.py`'s `check_stat_blocks()`, dict key `'stat'`) are explicitly out of scope — this pass is prose only, never code, since renaming a live variable/function/dict-key is a different, far riskier operation than renaming English text. "Stat Block" (76 occurrences/40 files) names the formatted display convention itself, not the 3-stats concept directly — whether it becomes "Aspect Block" or stays as-is is a real decision for Drew at Sync time, not an assumption. Three named headings/mechanics (`CLAUDE.md`'s Stat System Quick Reference, `rules/character-creation.md`'s Stat Increases, `rules/card-glossary.md`'s Stat Change — cross-referenced by name from the Future-Lock entry) need considered renames. `combatsimulations/README.md` mixes the stats-sense and the unrelated statistics-sense in one file, needing manual handling. Must not touch "Status" (Positive Status Effect, Status Cards — an already-established, unrelated keyword family sharing the substring). `rules/combat.md` itself already has both vocabularies coexisting (Core Combat Philosophy uses "Aspect," the rest of the file still says "stat") and needs internal reconciliation as part of the eventual pass. Logged under `memory.md`'s Pending propagation with this same detail, ready to execute without re-research whenever Sync is called. Not shipped this turn — this is scoping, not execution.

**[2026-08-11] A3 — Core Combat Philosophy given its true canonical home in `rules/combat.md`; "The 3 Aspects" established as the canonical name for Mind/Body/Soul.** Drew, one message after the pillar principle and interaction tiebreaker landed in `agent-tools/design-principles.md`: *"balance between 'The 3 Aspects' (canonical name for the 3 stats mind body and soul), control distance, act before your opponent does. that's the core combat philosophy. fold it in with the the 3 pillars section in design principles and give it a its true canonical home in the combat rules. the interaction tiebreaker and the shorthand both go to canon rules as well."* Correcting a mis-filing from the prior ship, not adding new content over it: `design-principles.md`'s own header says "not a rule" — the pillar-risk principle, the RPS/Initiative/Position shorthand, and the telegraphed-over-hidden rule all describe how the game itself works, not how to build well-made content for it, so that file was never their real home.

New `## Core Combat Philosophy` section in `rules/combat.md`, right after the opening line, before Stealth & Ambush — the natural top-of-file framing spot. Built around Drew's three-part statement directly: Balance between The 3 Aspects (Mind/Body/Soul, now named — maps to RPS, the color triangle), control distance (Position), act before your opponent does (Initiative). The pillar-risk principle and the telegraphed-over-hidden rule folded in immediately after, in the same section, matching how they were already paired in `design-principles.md`. Both bullets removed from `design-principles.md`, replaced with a one-line pointer explaining the move; two external cross-references (`playtesting/live-test-2v2-review.md`, `agent-tools/mechanics-perspective.md`) and one internal `memory.md` pointer, all still pointing at `design-principles.md` from the prior ship, repointed to `rules/combat.md`.

**"The 3 Aspects" scoped deliberately narrow — not a repo-wide rename.** Established as the canonical name and used in the new section, but "stat(s)" is used pervasively throughout the rest of the repo (`CLAUDE.md`, `rules/character-creation.md`, every card and stat block) and a full migration wasn't asked for or executed here — that would be a rename on the scale of tonight's Locations→Places move, not a natural side effect of relocating one section. Worth a direct question to Drew before attempting it, not an assumption.

**[2026-08-11] A2 — Six more entries drained from Active Pending Threads and Active Reasoning: two archived fully, two extracted as design principles, two moved to `unresolved-concerns.md` as debt.** Drew, working through both sections by name: *"Pendragon Arc can be archived safely. road encounters is debt. unresolved concerns. [balance findings on far-off content] this is debt. [Frost/Steele erosion] archive. cards changes can effect all simulator decks. ['the game's about interaction'] this is a design principle. the section containing this gets archived. this is a design principle: A team that ignores an entire pillar... additionally the pillars are - battlefield positioning, initiative positioning, and deck color balancing."*

**Archived fully, verbatim, immediately below:** Pendragon Arc (Active Pending Threads — its resolved facts now live independently in `world/the-scar.md` and `world/the-regency.md`; the deliberately-open GM-secret threads inside it stay open there, not lost); the full "Three core pillars" entry and the full interaction-tiebreaker entry (Active Reasoning), once their standing principles were pulled out.

**Extracted as design principles, `agent-tools/design-principles.md`:** the telegraphed-over-hidden rule (from the interaction-tiebreaker entry), and the pillar-risk principle (from the three-pillars entry) — the latter now carries Drew's fuller names for the three pillars (battlefield positioning, initiative positioning, deck color balancing) alongside the existing RPS/Initiative/Position shorthand used elsewhere in the repo. Two live external cross-references (`playtesting/live-test-2v2-review.md`, `agent-tools/mechanics-perspective.md`) and one internal `memory.md` pointer, all previously pointing at the Active-Reasoning entry, repointed to the new home.

**Moved to `unresolved-concerns.md` as debt, not archived as history:** Road encounters (the specific undecided question — fold Jackrabbits/Briar Scratchers into the Day 1 toolkit or leave them ambient — distilled there; full original context archived just below for the trail) and the "Balance findings on far-off content" scope rule (same treatment — Drew's "this is debt" read as: this names an ongoing, standing category of unaddressed balance concern, not a settled methodology that belongs in Active Reasoning).

**Frost/Steele erosion caveat, archived with a correction attached, not silently narrowed:** the original entry was Frost/Steele-specific; Drew's generalization ("card changes can affect all simulator decks") is folded into the archived entry itself rather than promoted to a new standing doctrine line — close enough to self-evident once stated that it doesn't need its own permanent home. 12/12 verify.

**[2026-08-11] A2 — Campaign Status's Clean Slate and Combat Simulator paragraphs archived; `combatsimulations/README.md` updated with what was actually unique in the latter.** Drew: *"under campaign status. the clean slate entry is for archives. the combat simulator entry might contain a unique explanation that belongs in the relevant agent tool. fully move key design decisions to archives."* Read as walking Campaign Status entry by entry: Clean Slate archives fully (its live facts — low-resource start, no gold/items, Session 0 framing — already established independently in `quests/washed-ashore.md`, spot-checked before moving, not assumed). Combat Simulator's genuinely unique content — `team_engine.py`'s existence, the 23-deck `ROSTER`, the death-rate tracking target — wasn't documented anywhere; `combatsimulations/README.md` still described a 3-deck, single-engine tool. Folded the missing pieces into the README (new "The roster" section, engine-count fix in the intro, a note distinguishing the Frost/Steele/Mire tournament's original 3-deck scope from the roster's current size) before archiving the full original paragraph. The session spine table stayed in `memory.md`, Campaign Status — a live pacing reference `rules/equipment.md` cites directly, not settled history; "fully move" applied to what actually qualified as decided history, not the whole section indiscriminately. 12/12 verify.

**[2026-08-11] A2 — Recently Shipped itself moved out of `memory.md`, into this section, permanently.** Drew: *"recently shipped should be an archive thing."* Correct on the same logic that's driven every restructure tonight: a ship entry is already historical record the moment it's written, whether or not Drew has read it yet — staging it in `memory.md` and moving it to this file at Sync was always two steps for one thing. **[2026-08-11] A1 — Active Pending Threads vs. `unresolved-concerns.md` distinction made explicit, one line in each.** Drew, confirming and sharpening a first cut I proposed: *"active pending threads is possible future connections noticed while doing work that doesn't require answering them. unresolved concerns is debt flat out. things that need answered for current work to fully tie into the repo correctly."* Neither file moved — both already lived in the right place, they just didn't name each other. **[2026-08-11] A2 — memory.md drained: ~40 closed Active Reasoning entries archived, Content Reference and World Context Notes removed as redundant with canon, genuine open threads relocated.** Drew, mid-restructure: *"wait stop. memory.md is a total mess. huge sections are all finished work."* He shared a second framework (relayed from GPT) that reframed the whole pass — classify by function (CANON MEMORY / TRANSITION / UNRESOLVED / HISTORY / WORKLOG / REDUNDANT), find each category's natural home, drain with one test (*would losing this make a future agent materially more likely to misunderstand or damage current design?*), then compress. Test: not "does this read finished," but "could I point to the specific fact that resolves this, or only to a sentence that implies one exists." 12/12 verify.

Drew: *"2."* **Resolution, to preserve as much already-shipped material as possible while honoring the retcon:** the Last Bath (dead, released, no return, literal completion) is true orthodox doctrine — new content in `factions/people-of-promise.md`. Living "ritual bathing" is *not* that doctrine; it's the Glasslight congregation's own long-normalized custom, drifted from it so gradually that most who practice it (Corren, Sael, Wren, Mirel) don't experience it as heretical at all — this required zero changes to any of their files, since none of them ever asserted the practice was literally the founding doctrine, only that it was normal *here*. Kaine's actual crime, sharpened rather than invented: not bathing the living (the congregation already did that), but the specific theological claim that surviving it proves the Last Bath's completion can be unlocked before death — and then pushing already-changed followers back in, repeatedly, chasing more of it. Added the visible marks his longest-devoted followers carry (animal eyes, stone-patched skin, moss, furred ears, a flattened dark nose, claws, tails) as what his theology reads as *unlocked* rather than damage, and "The Pitch" — the scene where he shows a new initiate a changed body instead of arguing doctrine, with Drew's exact quoted line and the grin too large for the sentence. Steve gets this pitch, is unsettled by it, proceeds anyway — matches his established characterization exactly, no changes needed there either. Fixed one real inconsistency in `quests/tide-pulls-back.md`: Mirel's assessment of Kaine's practice had assumed the old framing (ordinary return = doctrine, only repeated pushing = deviation); reworded to match the new one (the claim itself, not the act of returning, is what nobody else in the congregation asserts out loud). Added the new comparison table to `factions/people-of-promise.md` directly.

**[2026-08-09] A3 — Correction: bypassing Price is still impossible, but binding a domain perfectly isn't — those are different claims, and the entry above conflated them.** Drew: *"no it's not possible to bypass Price. but it is possible to remove an Archon and claims it's Seat. the Seat of love/binding simply doesn't have the kind of power it would take. Held-the-ordinary-way domains pay Price piecemeal and decay gracefully if payment lapses, same as any Haven. A domain bound instead of held doesn't pay as it goes; the debt still accrues, uncollected, until called in whole — and when it is, nothing fades. It's unmade entirely, in a single stroke, all at once. Reworked `world/the-regency.md`'s Pendragon Attempt section to match: six people reached for something that could genuinely have worked, in the narrow sense of binding, and would have ended in something far worse than an ordinary failure if it had — they just never got that far, because Aurora already held the Seat. They cannot — healing would close them"*). Named as the likely reason the backlash was severe enough to produce the Scar and the five non-aging masks — not incidental collateral of an ordinary failed claim, but the cost of six people trying to violate the most fundamental law this cosmology has. **(2) Oswald's real ambition, unknown to the rest of the council**, who read a century of consolidation as ordinary ambition: he doesn't believe his brother is dead, doesn't know he's the Scar, and believes killing or removing Aurora outright — not the current suppression, which he treats as only a stage toward that — would open the Seat for his brother to finally take. **This required correcting text I'd written earlier tonight, not just adding to it**: Oswald's Wound/Refusal had him trying to win the Seat for himself ("keeping the Seat out of his hands"); rewrote both to be about making Aurora removable instead, which is the actual, real stake — the psychological mechanism (substituting a controllable variable for an unbearable uncertainty) stayed exactly the same, just pointed at the true object. "The ongoing attempt" section rewritten the same way — Oswald isn't positioning himself to claim the Seat, he's positioning Aurora to be removable, and the Table #9 letter's "threat to the other council members" framing is now flagged as real but possibly incomplete, not wrong. **Left deliberately open, matching the Scar's own existing restraint**: whether Oswald is right that his brother survives in any sense, whether the Scar — "nothing inside it" — could even take a Seat if he is, and what finishing the extraction actually costs the other four original members. **Attribution:** inspired by Fullmetal Alchemist's equivalent-exchange cosmology and the Elric brothers' arc, per Drew directly — the tonal/structural shape (a forbidden bypass of the setting's own fundamental law, catastrophic backlash, a brother devoted to undoing it) is the borrow; nothing FMA-specific was copied into canon text.

**[2026-08-09] A3 — Foundational retcon: there is no Prince, hasn't been in a hundred years, and Oswald is the Regency's actual ruler.** Built across several turns of live design conversation, each one correcting the last: I first floated a cosmological mechanism (Oswald needs the Prince gone to make his Seat claim total rather than borrowed) — wrong, Drew redirected to a pure timeline problem. I then guessed a succession of disposable child-heirs across the century — wrong again, simpler and darker than that. The actual history, Drew's own words: *"there is no prince. he died. remove the connections to living prince please... Oswald is the ruler. he took power when the prince died. it was a hidden regency member who killed the prince. that's the story now."* **Reconstructed sequence, now in `world/the-regency.md`'s Pendragon Attempt section as "What happened to the throne":** Pendragon's presumed death (he's actually the Scar, unchanged) left a Prince — a separate person, next in line — being prepared for coronation while the five companions held the throne "in trust." He was murdered right before the ceremony, by a hidden Regency member never identified (deliberately left open — "it may not even be one of the five," matching this file's own restraint elsewhere). With no heir left, the temporary arrangement never ended. Oswald has been the Regency's actual sovereign for a century, "Regent" in title only. **Removed every live reference to a currently-existing Prince**, since the retcon makes them all false rather than just outdated: Oswald's Sphere, both Rumors-table lines about him, the "consolidating power around the Prince" Council Dynamics line, `places/capital/gilded-tusk.md`'s "Prince's guards" flavor line and its whole "Prince's Appetite" notice-board hook (retargeted to Oswald's own table as "The Regent's Appetite," same reward, same function). **Removed the Prince-specific warmth paragraph from Oswald's own section entirely** — per Drew: *"Oswald- no warmth towards any prince. there are none. that doesn't mean remove his warmth that he shows people."* His general pleasant/approving baseline personality (established two corrections ago) stays untouched; only the now-nonsensical Prince-specific ambiguity thread is gone. **Also built out, confirmed correct by Drew before writing ("the rest absolutely does")**: the animal titles are now explicitly literal masks, not just monikers, worn by the original five to cover that they don't age (tied explicitly to this session's own Price/Second-Cut work — their aging slowed because the Seat's touch loosened how thoroughly they're anchored as ordinary people, the same anchoring Havens already runs on) and worn by Isabella/Percival as empty ceremonial tokens of office. Added a new public-facing "What the city wonders" passage — rumors about their unnatural age, whether they're still human, whether the faces under the masks have been quietly swapped — collected as genuine uncertainty, nothing confirmed or denied, matching this repo's standard restraint on this kind of thread. **Flagged, not resolved:** what Oswald actually needs to finish the job is open again — my first guess (remove a rival claim) doesn't apply anymore now that there's no living Prince to remove. Logged in the same Active Pending Threads entry as the campaign-climax flag; don't guess a fourth time without Drew.

**[2026-08-09] A1 — Oswald's Wound corrected a third time: he wouldn't call it a failure.** Drew: *"Oswald wouldn't call the first attempt a failed one. he would say it's one ongoing attempt. they have Aurora bound beneath the city after all."* The prior fix (below) had already corrected the cosmology (a Contest is winnable, not impossible) but still framed the event in "first attempt failed, second attempt now underway" language — which is the narrator's neutral framing, accurate for the objective historical record, but not how Oswald would ever hold it in his own head, and this section is written from inside his head. Reworked: Aurora being bound beneath the city right now, extraction running, isn't aftermath following a loss to him — it's the same attempt, still executing, decades into itself, with the Pendragon Attempt as its opening move rather than its ending. This sharpens the belief/wound/refusal further: precision isn't something he needs for a hypothetical second try, it's what's keeping one continuous, still-running operation from coming apart at whatever point in the whole machine is weakest today — which is a tighter, more paranoid, more totalizing stake than "don't fail again" was. Didn't touch the neutral narrator language elsewhere (`world/the-regency.md`'s own Pendragon Attempt section still says "the first claim failed") since that's an objective description, not Oswald's voice, and nothing about it was wrong. **Also flagged, not built:** Drew named this thread — Oswald's Contest for the Seat — as pointing toward a real campaign climax, logged in Active Pending Threads' existing Pendragon Arc entry rather than developed further, per his own standing rule not to build that arc without him.

**[2026-08-09] A1 — Oswald's section corrected twice: a real cosmology error caught by Drew's own question, plus a tone correction.** Drew: *"it's impossible to take a Seat from an Archon?"* — asked, not told, and it caught a real mistake. The Wound I'd written asserted the first Seat-claim attempt failed because Aurora already held it, "full stop, no discipline in the world could have changed that," presented as the objective truth Oswald refuses to accept. That's not what `mythology/seats.md` actually says: the Seat States table has a **Contested** state ("two beings fighting for a Seat warp reality until one wins"), and the Love/Binding Seat's own entry is explicitly marked "Current state: Contested" — an active, resolvable fight, not a settled impossibility. I'd introduced an absolute that isn't canon and that actively undercuts the Oswald/Aurora plot thread's own stakes (if it's flatly impossible, his entire life's project is empty rather than dangerous). **Fixed:** Wound reworked so the real uncertainty is what a Contest actually turns on, not whether one is winnable — Oswald's refusal is narrower and worse than denying reality: he won't sit with not knowing the actual variable, so he substitutes one he can act on (precision). Second correction, same pass: *"Oswald isn't cold. formal, but people would call him 'nice' most of the time. He smiles when he approves, even when talking with subordinates behind closed doors."* The original draft had him "formal past the point of warmth" as a baseline — wrong register entirely. Reworked so pleasantness (measured, approval-based) is his actual default, and the violent flip is unsettling specifically *because* there's no visible transition from the smile, not because he was cold to begin with. The Prince paragraph adjusted to match — his usual warmth is now established as real but transactional (the reward half of the same system that produces the temper), so the ambiguity Drew wants kept open (sincere or tactical) has something to actually contrast against.

**[2026-08-09] A2 — Lord Oswald given real interiority: belief, wound, refusal, closing the biggest voice gap in the repo.** I'd flagged him unprompted as the most load-bearing character with zero interiority anywhere — pure title, sphere, and plot-mechanism, no `characters/*.md` file (matching precedent: none of the seven council members get one, they live in `world/the-regency.md`, with Elara and Eveline as the two precedent deep-dive sections) and nothing about how he actually reads in a room. Drew supplied the real material directly: *"he is tall and stiff. Extremely formal in public. Quick to anger behind closed doors. He loses his temper and becomes violent when his orders aren't executed perfectly. a dictator, authoritarian... same rare shows of warmth towards his family, not resolved if it's sincere or merely a part of his plans to control the prince according to rumors."* Built as a new "Lord Oswald — What the Title Cost Him" section, `agent-tools/finding-a-voice.md`'s belief→wound→refusal method, grounded in material already in canon rather than invented cold: **belief** — an order executed imperfectly is the same as no order, precision is the only thing between control and collapse; **wound** — the Pendragon Attempt's established fact is that the first Seat claim failed because Aurora already held it, full stop, no discipline could have changed that; Oswald's private account swaps the cause to a discipline failure instead; **refusal** — he won't hold the structural version long enough to test it, because it would mean the second attempt his whole remaining life is organized around might be unwinnable regardless of what he does. The warmth toward the Prince is written and left genuinely unresolved — sincere or purely tactical — same restraint this repo already gives Isabella's loyalties and Aege's Echo stage; explicitly marked table discovery, not something to resolve in the file. **Attribution:** the tonal archetype (formal-authoritarian public face, private violence at imperfect obedience, ambiguous-but-real-seeming family warmth) was named by Drew as modeled on King Bradley (*Fullmetal Alchemist*) — noted here as the actual creative provenance per this file's own attribution rule, not written into the canon text itself, since nothing FMA-specific was copied, only the tonal shape.

**Process note:** Drew, direct — pure prose/lore edits that don't touch cards, stat blocks, or rules text don't need a `verify.py` run. The checks are for mechanical drift (card format, deck math, HP formula, simulator parity); nothing in this batch or the one before it touched any of that, and every run was passing trivially. Still running it for anything that touches cards/items/rules going forward — just not reflexively for pure worldbuilding.

**[2026-08-09] A2 — Light maps and path maps split into two distinct kinds of "map," each with its own real duration.** Drew: the Glasslight chapter of the Cartographers Guild specializes in light cartography, the Gallery should be half light maps, light maps last about a week before fading, and path maps (the Route Walk survey documents from this session's earlier Havens work) go up from a week to a month. Fixed a real inconsistency the earlier Gallery text had left standing: it had described Soft Edge gallery work as fading on the "same" timeline as the town's plain silvered paper (a few hours, per Economy) — reworded so light maps share the same underlying light-craft limit but are worked harder and hold longer, about a week, resolving the mismatch rather than leaving two different numbers implying the same thing. Also caught and fixed a second inconsistency while in the file: the Kettlewright showpiece was written as a permanent fixture sitting against a fade rule that had just been given teeth — added one line (he retouches it himself, before it fades, the same discipline as a Mason recutting a post) rather than leaving it unexplained. `factions/the-cartographers-guild.md`'s Structure section now states the Glasslight chapter's light-cartography work explicitly — a regional specialty, not a redefinition of what the Guild is chartered to do everywhere else. `world/the-in-between.md`'s Havens paragraph and the Guild's Survey ledgers bullet both renamed "map" to "path map" (now that there are two kinds) and bumped the duration to a month; kept the two fade mechanisms structurally distinct — light maps fade because of the town's light-craft medium (Economy), path maps lose trust because they're a one-time payment record decaying in a Havens sense (Price/Second Cut) — different causes that happen to look similar on the surface.

**[2026-08-09] A3 — "The rifts" purged as a vague, undefined city-wide threat; the Abyssal Ruins' specific rift kept.** Drew: *"rifts have got to go."* I'd asked him to count the references for an unrelated reason and found the real number was much smaller than it first looked — 60 hits turned out to include "drift"/"driftwood" matches; the real word-boundary count was 13 mentions across 8 files. Reading those closely surfaced two unrelated things sharing one word: (1) a specific, located, quest-grounded phenomenon — the Abyssal Ruins' Central Gateway and the stabilizing crystal in `quests/abyssal-ruins-cathedral.md` — and (2) a vague, plural, never-mechanized city-wide background threat that Percival's entire council portfolio and part of the Regency's founding-legitimacy folklore had been resting on, with nothing underneath it the way the Unheld and the In-Between both have. Asked which sense was meant before touching anything, since removing sense 2 meant reworking Percival's portfolio (which I'd just tied the Cartographers Guild to, same session) and a live quest depended on sense 1 surviving. Drew: *"percivals scholarship is cartography head of the cartographers guild. keep the abyssal ruins rift, that's much more manageable."* **Fixed — sense 2 removed everywhere, sense 1 untouched:** `world/the-regency.md`'s Percival Sphere row rewritten from "studying the rifts" to cartography, head of the Guild outright (not an oversight assignment tacked onto scholarship elsewhere); Oswald's and Percival's own Devout-rumor lines reworded to reference the roads/Havens instead of an undefined threat (Oswald's now ties directly into this session's own payment-tiers work: "without the Regent, the roads would have gone back to blank years ago"); the Council Dynamics line reworded from "studying the rifts" to "runs an institution touching every mile of road outside the city," keeping the real point (no Seat-scar, no context for why certain doors don't open) intact. `factions/the-cartographers-guild.md`'s Percival paragraph rewritten from "answers to" (external oversight) to "headed by" (his actual council seat). `places/capital/gilded-tusk.md`'s Table #9 letter hook and `places/capital/capital.md`'s library line both reworded to drop the rifts framing without inventing new cosmology in its place. Left untouched: `places/abyssal-ruins.md`, `bestiary/phase-leach.md` ("Rift-Stalker," "rift-bleed"), `quests/abyssal-ruins-cathedral.md` — all sense 1, all kept exactly as Drew asked.

**[2026-08-09] A3 — The Canon Gate change reverted: Authority 3 waits for Drew's sign-off again.** Drew, direct: *"revert the A3 change."* Yesterday's change (see below, "The Canon Gate itself changed") had A3 shipping the same way A2 already does — noted, veto-after. That's undone: `CLAUDE.md`'s Authority 3 line is back to *"Drew's authority, permanently"* / "goes to `experimental/` or a chat proposal and waits for explicit sign-off," and the "every Authority 1/2/3 ship gets one line" sentence is back to 1/2 only. The `unresolved-concerns.md` entry flagging the sign-off removal as a live risk (Process & Harness section) is removed rather than left stale — the risk it named no longer exists once the change causing it is undone, and the section had no other entries. Both the original change and this reversal stay in `memory.md`'s history exactly as they happened; this is a threshold log, not a record that gets cleaned up after the fact.

**[2026-08-09] A2 — Payment tiers in the In-Between formalized: glyphs hold as long as they're carved, maps hold about a week.** Grew out of a live design conversation with Drew about the fiction underneath the In-Between's table function (fast travel, blind encounters, GM hinting) — he named the mechanism himself: "the in-between shifts because nobody pays the price there. glyph posts create stable paths for as long as the post glyphs last. maps are more temporary... let's say a week usually." Added to `world/the-in-between.md`'s Havens section as a new tier underneath the existing "payment has not stopped" rule: a glyph is a *standing* payment (renewed on a schedule, lasts as long as the carving), a map is a *record* of a payment already spent (a Route Walk pays for a stretch once, in the walking; what's left afterward is memory of that, not the thing itself). **Named why it works, at two altitudes on purpose.** Officially, in-world: this is just how the Guild and the Masons have always operated, no explanation needed — matches Drew's own framing ("the people of Eclipseria don't feel a need to explain it... same as not explaining that walking takes time"), so nothing here is written as something an NPC would say out loud. GM-facing, in an aside: Price is literally the Second Cut (`mythology/creation-myth-the-three-cuts.md`) running at ground level, and `rules/character-creation.md`'s Declaring a Price ("the adherence, not the statement, is what's actually paid") is the identical Cut at a person's scale — the same mechanism Drew and I had both independently landed on in conversation before either of us said it. Not a coincidental echo (Song and Silence's register) — a structurally exact identity, just never stated in one place before. Cross-referenced into `factions/the-cartographers-guild.md`'s Survey ledgers bullet (why the rotation exists instead of one definitive map) without re-explaining the mechanism there.

**[2026-08-09] A2 — The Cartographers Guild given a Regency owner: Lord Percival.** Drew: "Percival, lock it in." Chosen over the two other real candidates from the design conversation — Eveline (structurally the obvious "critical infrastructure" slot, but her two existing holdings both hide something exploitative underneath a mundane surface, which risked implying the same shape for an institution built specifically to be the Masons' bright, public opposite) and Oswald (fits the "roads are the Regency's most popular good" legitimacy angle, but makes the Guild about his image rather than what it actually produces). Percival's own sphere is already "the only one actively studying the rifts" (`world/the-regency.md`) — a Route Walk survey ledger reporting a stretch gone back to blank is rift data either way, so this connects an existing thin portfolio to a concrete apparatus instead of inventing a new one. `world/the-regency.md`'s Sphere table and `factions/the-cartographers-guild.md`'s Relationship to the Regency section both updated; the Guild's own day-to-day purpose is unaffected — a route walk gets logged the same regardless of who reads the ledger after.

**[2026-08-09] A1 — Real contradiction caught in the Glasslight batch just shipped: "aurora" wasn't a free word to use.** Drew asked for a contradiction check on the just-shipped Glasslight content, run against the whole repo rather than just the new text itself. Found one: the Soft Edge's new light-dance detail and the Kettlewright painting were both described as "aurora-like" — but Aurora is the named Archon of the Love/Binding Seat (`mythology/seats.md`), bound beneath Eclipseria, load-bearing across `world/the-regency.md`, `factions/the-weavers.md`, `quests/the-wallows-descent.md`. `factions/the-weavers.md` even opens with a line guarding against exactly this confusion ("this is not where Aurora is held"). Using the same word for an unrelated light phenomenon on the opposite side of the map risked implying a connection nothing in the brief intended — the same failure mode the coincidental-echo doctrine (Song and Silence, Fog Goggles) exists to prevent, just missed on the first pass instead of caught before shipping. **Fixed:** both mentions reworded to describe the same visual (banded, drifting light) without the word — "the light-dance" is now the term used in both the Soft Edge section and the Gallery's Kettlewright description. No other contradictions found in the batch; the climb, Tide Market, Gallery/Kettlewright-Kess tie, and Hana all checked clean against existing geography, the Cartographers Guild file, and the coastline-threshold rule.

This collapses that asymmetry — A3 now ships the same way A2 already did: noted prominently in the queue and in chat, Drew's review is veto-after rather than sign-off-before. `CLAUDE.md`'s Canon Gate section updated (the Authority 3 tag itself, from "Drew's authority, permanently" to "agent authority, always noted"; the "every Authority 1/2 ship gets one line" sentence widened to 1/2/3, since A3 now genuinely belongs in that pattern instead of being the exception to it). This is itself an Authority-3-scale change — the rule governing who may authorize Authority-3-scale changes — and is being noted per the very rule it establishes, immediately, in this same entry.

Kiwi Bird's actual FREEZE collision this session was a card-name collision caught by `verify.py`, not a behavior duplication — the two are different failure modes, and this step targets the one nothing currently checks for.

Resolved by narrowing the Guild's actual job to exactly what Drew said: not mapping the blank (impossible, and stated as such in-file), but continuously re-certifying the paid-for roads themselves — Route Walks, way-marking, survey ledgers — which is the real mechanism behind "the Regency maintains the roads" finally getting a face. Headquartered in Glasslight Reach, explicitly distinguished from two things it could get confused with: the light-cartographers (`places/glasslight-reach.md`, same city, same discipline, narrower mandate — the Soft Edge only) and "the Cartographer" (`places/capital/underground-bazaar.md`, Kess's grandmother, an individual whose personal magic maps are a completely different register). New file `rules/river-fishing.md`, cross-referenced from `world/geography-overview.md`'s Rivers and Lakes (available anywhere on the whole river network, not tied to one location) and `rules/resolution.md`. That was the right call at the time — the Seat didn't exist yet in canon. Now it does. **[2026-08-08] A3 — Temple of Justice's four legal gaps built and shipped together, reframed as full corruption rather than an ambiguous "maybe."** Drew: *"can they all live at once? the courts are a 3 ring circus act. a facade. it's whatever those in power say it is. full corruption."* All four candidates from the earlier conversation shipped live at once (Silent Clause, Witness Gap, Debt Transfer, Standing to Sue) rather than picking one, and the court's framing moved from "a question the Temple has stopped asking out loud" to stated outright: precedent and procedure are real and constantly cited, and all of it is theater over the actual mechanism, which is power. Contrast reads stronger than uniform corruption would have. The Silent Clause stays the one built from existing cosmology (Declaring a Price's "a thing has to be said to carry weight," filed as contract law) rather than invented cold.

**[2026-08-08] A3 — Declaring a Price corrected again: the adherence is the Price, not the statement.** Drew: *"it's not the statement, it's the adherence. the adherence is the Price."* Refines yesterday's correction (which had already moved the Price off the old card-reveal mechanic and onto the statement itself) one step further — the statement is only the trigger; what's actually paid is everything that follows, every choice the pledge constrains, every test it survives or fails. Fixed in `rules/character-creation.md` (the actual rule) and `places/island-in-a-ship.md`'s Setting the Tone (which had explicitly said "the statement itself is the Price" the same day it was written). The six Glasslight/Promise NPCs built under the previous version were checked again and still hold — none of them commit to which part of the mechanic is the Price, all of them talk about an ongoing, unpaid cost, which is if anything a closer fit to today's correction than yesterday's.

**[2026-08-08] A3 — The capital's judicial system defined: Isabella is Supreme Magistrate, Temple of Justice is citizen/business law only, Oswald sets the law that binds the government itself.** Drew's direct call, closing the "least defined of the seven" framing that had genuinely been left open. Two-tier structure, not one: the Temple (Isabella's court) adjudicates disputes between citizens and businesses, fully Regency-controlled, with explicitly zero authority over the Regency's own conduct; law binding what the *government* may do is a separate, higher tier entirely, Oswald's alone. **This recontextualizes rather than contradicts the existing "studying how to bypass it" rumor about Isabella** (`world/the-regency.md`, Rumors) — reread now that her title is on the table, it's not a visitor exploiting a system she doesn't control, it's the Supreme Magistrate knowing every gap because she wrote or approved most of them. Same line, sharper once the missing piece is in place. **Magistrate Ambrose reframed as a lower-bench subordinate rather than the presiding authority** — the beat I'd built two turns ago (ruled against Isabella once, on a technicality) reads as genuinely risky now instead of merely notable, since it's a subordinate ruling against his own Supreme Magistrate in her own court. Didn't touch the political-alignment ambiguity that's explicitly still open ("Currently uncommitted," a table discovery per the Council Dynamics section) — split "least defined of the seven" into what's now actually defined (her office) versus what still isn't (her loyalties), rather than resolving both at once.

**[2026-08-08] A1 — Temple District clergy named: Osric, Faye, Sister Odile, Tamsin, Nell, Bram, Magistrate Ambrose.** Follows directly from the function-definition pass — each person built belief/wound/refusal (`agent-tools/finding-a-voice.md`) against the specific infrastructural role their temple was just given, not invented cold. Osric's (Library) obsessive cataloguing traces to one thing he judged too minor to record once, that mattered later and was gone. Faye's (Dreams) precision reading everyone else traces to one dream of her own she's never let anyone near. Sister Odile (Healing) heals without judging who deserves it, on principle, at a real cost she's absorbed rather than reconsidered. Tamsin and Nell (Sun/Moon) are a matched contrasting pair by design, same as their temples — her overcorrected loudness against a grief that got used against Nell once when it was public. Bram (Sanctuary of Nature) holds two beliefs in real tension (nothing easy is trustworthy; people deserve the years plants take) and hasn't resolved which one wins. Magistrate Ambrose (Justice) sits closest to the existing Council material — built directly against Isabella's already-established ambiguity (`world/the-regency.md`) rather than inventing a new dynamic, including one ruling against her he doesn't fully understand her reaction to, and one much older ruling under pressure he's still privately answering for. All seven checked against the rest of the repo for name collisions before shipping (none found) and live inline in `places/capital/capital.md`, same pattern as the Forge's Harlan/Sera/three artisans, not as standalone character files — consistent with how this session has drawn the line between location-bound service NPCs and campaign-wide figures.

**[2026-08-08] A2/A3 — Temple District given real infrastructural roles before any clergy get built, per Drew's own instinct to check the lens before adding eyes.** Asked whether the nine unstaffed temples actually served a function first; the answer was mostly no, which is why this shipped as function-definition rather than a voice pass. Drew supplied each one directly: **Temple of Knowledge is the Library of Arcane Knowledge** — confirmed as one building under two names, closing a real ambiguity flagged before writing anything (Sitrump's scroll source and the Gilded Tusk's Lost Lore hook were never explicitly tied to the temple before this). **Temple of Dreams** sells dream readings — interpretation, not extraction, explicitly distinguished from Lily's trade at the Underground Bazaar (she buys the night outright; the Temple never takes the dream, only reads it) so the two don't quietly overlap. **Temple of Healing** is the capital's actual hospital — the "most clear function in the group," Drew's words, and it was sitting unbuilt. **Sun and Moon** framed as a matched pair, not shops — daily/loud/vitality vs. quiet/grief-work, the same civic instinct split across two roofs. **Sanctuary of Nature** corrected away from a "druids and nature spirits" implication toward what Drew actually specified: herbalists doing hard-fought cultivation in genuinely barren ground, sweat and understanding, explicitly not passive nature-mindfulness. **Temple of Justice** got Lady Isabella confirmed as its Regency tie — this was already sitting in `world/the-regency.md` (her Sphere line literally says "Temple of Justice regular," and the Rumors table already had the tension: "some say she's just studying how to bypass it") — no invention needed, just connecting a fact that was already there to the question being asked. **Temple of Unity** left alone in favor of a cross-reference to `characters/vescal.md`, which already carries its real institutional role (conflict mediation, the Aurora GM Secret) in full. **Temple of the Rivers stays untouched on purpose** — an existing note flagged it as deliberately unresolved ("noticed, not pulled... a thread for later rather than an accidental connection made permanent on a hunch"), and this pass respected that rather than closing it along with everything else. No named clergy built yet for any of these — that's still the next, separate pass, now with real ground to stand people on.

Its Fogcaller-avoidance rule extends their already-established junction-offering etiquette with Pell rather than inventing new behavior; its Echo-as-lure mechanic matches their established "you are not the first" role; its folk-ritual table is explicit that Pell isn't the origin ("he would recognize the bones and dislike the drift"), avoiding retroactively crediting him with founding a culture he's separate from.
