**[2026-08-08] A3 — The Masons built the Briarwatch line on purpose, knowing what it would do to the Root Heart. Real redefinition, Drew's explicit call.** Follow-on from the parasite-framing fix: Drew, *"it's the reason for the masons fence after all."* Genuinely ambiguous which way that cut — asked directly rather than guessing, since one reading (thematic: unchecked growth in general is what the Masons' whole order exists to fence against) left everything already written alone, and the other (causal: they knew about *this* fence's effect on the Root Heart specifically) deletes a piece of established dramatic irony outright. Drew confirmed the causal reading. **`factions/the-masons.md`'s Known Works entry rewritten**: "The Masons don't know this either" (the Root Heart half of that sentence) reversed to explicit intent — they understood a sentient forest's unchecked growth behaves parasitically, and built the line to redirect that growth inward on purpose, judging the Root Heart's violent cycle a containable cost against letting the whole Weald spread unchecked. **The pillar failure and Western Property sinkhole stay unknown to them** — that thread wasn't part of this correction, and splitting the old sentence in two (one fact now known, one still genuinely not) took a second pass to get clean; the first draft accidentally restated the pillar/sinkhole fact twice in adjacent paragraphs, caught by re-reading the file rather than trusting the edit had landed right. Propagated to the two other files that describe the same mechanism: `bestiary/root-heart.md`'s GM Notes (the redirection is what the fence was built to do, not an accident it happened to cause) and `characters/aege.md`'s GM Secret, which now states plainly what this means for her personally — her family is paying an installment on a decision made by people they've never met and never will. Real weight added to an already-established character rather than new plot invented for her.

**[2026-08-08] A3 — Root Heart's "What It Is" section corrected: the parasite framing was wrong, not just underspecified.** Drew, direct: *"the RootHeart was never a parasite. it was always Turnroot as a whole. Unchecked growth as a parasite analogy."* My first pass kept the Read-Aloud Description's existing "feeds the forest or feeds on it" ambiguity alive by closing on "in Overgrowth, it's doing both at once" — which still treats parasitism as a live possibility for the Heart itself. Corrected: the Heart was never a parasite, full stop, because there was never a separate Weald for it to feed on — it isn't a part living off a whole, it *is* the whole. The parasite analogy belongs to unchecked growth itself (Overgrowth), not to the thing growing. The Read-Aloud Description's own ambiguous line stays untouched on purpose — that's deliberate in-fiction uncertainty for players, a different register from the GM-facing section that's supposed to give the actual answer.

**[2026-08-08] A2 — Turnroot Overgrowth ruleset shipped in full: the content the earlier scan report was actually pointing at.** Drew delivered the real draft directly after I'd (wrongly) called the "Turnroot dual-canon" scan item stale — turned out the underlying claim was right, I'd just conflated "Root Heart's Overgrowth backstory is already shipped" with "the mechanical consequences of it are already shipped," and they weren't the same thing. Corrected by actually reading the delivered content against current files before shipping anything, same discipline as always. **`bestiary/root-heart.md`** gained a "What It Is" section: not an invader, not a guardian — the Weald concentrated, periodically overgrown, the rings from Post-Defeat now explained as interruptions from previous cuttings rather than left as an unexplained detail. Checked against the existing Masons-fence causal explanation in GM Notes first — compatible, not contradictory: that section explains why *this* peak is happening now, the new section explains what overgrowth *is* in general. **`quests/turnroot-weald-adventure.md`** gained: a Rootstalker Trail Quality table (hunting from Pressure 0 during Overgrowth, trail quality scaling with Pressure rather than gated behind it — checked against `bestiary/rootstalker.md`'s existing "no longer needs a violation" line first, confirmed as a consistent extension of an already-established fact, not a new invention); the Forest Customs section rewritten in full as "Sharpened" — three customs became six, consequences now scale with Pressure instead of landing flat, the Moss-Warden's riddle left untouched since it only ever named the original three; a new POI, The Fallen Tree, the campaign's first hard "obey the custom or pay for it" set piece. **`bestiary/rootstalker.md`** and its own file's spawn-condition note updated to state both rulesets explicitly (Overgrowth now, gate-at-Pressure-2+ once it ends) rather than only the one currently active. **New creature: Flower Snake** (`bestiary/flower-snake.md`, `cards/flower-snake.md`) — CTR 6, the ambush inside the Fallen Tree. One real correction made shipping it: the delivered draft stated HP 11 for Mind 3/Body 1/Soul 2, but the formula gives 8, and nothing about a small ambush predator justifies the bespoke-boss exception the way Root Heart's HP does — corrected to 8 rather than shipped as an unmarked deviation. Six new signature cards, all built from already-established keywords (Evade, Weak, Vulnerable, Resist, Deadly, Rooted, Ward) — no new mechanic invented for a creature whose whole identity is camouflage-then-strike-then-disengage. Glossary block count and the seven touched keyword counts updated to match (335 blocks now, up from 329).

Root Heart's Basic Attack had `Range: Any / Any`, outside the closed Melee/Ranged/Both set (`rules/combat.md`) — changed to `Both`, which already means "either position valid" and matches the fiction (`bestiary/root-heart.md`'s own Position note: "always counts as being in both Frontline and Backline"). **[2026-08-07] A2 — Ledger Weight's mechanic ruled and locked down.** Drew asked directly whether the defender could use it on their own reveal — a real ambiguity in the shipped one-line text ("force the defender to re-reveal their chosen card"), and the wrong answer would have broken something load-bearing: if a defender could pop this after seeing the attacker's card, it stops being a weapon and becomes a look-then-pick button, straight through the blind-reveal principle the whole resolution system runs on (`combatsimulations/README.md`'s own words: "the thing most worth trusting the sim on"). Ruled attacker-on-defender only, one direction, post-reveal, face-up redo since the attacker's card is already public by that point — the wielder can deny an opponent's first answer but can never fix their own blind guess, as either attacker or defender. Written into `items/capital-items.md` as the actual ruling rather than left ambiguous, and added to `rules/invariants.md`'s Mechanic-override reference table (card selection, one reveal, attacker-on-defender only) alongside Axiom and Paradox — the same override family, same discipline the file already asks for on any new card that reaches into engine-adjacent resolution.

No verify.py check currently catches item-price duplication the way `check_hp_formula` catches formula duplication; logged as a real gap, not fixed this pass (out of scope for a one-line correction).

**[2026-08-07] A1 — New tool: `agent-tools/finding-a-voice.md`, a generative method for named-person interiority.** Grew out of Drew's own diagnosis ("most of the people are barely more than functions to me") plus a GPT brainstorm he relayed: belief → wound → refusal to conclude the obvious thing, applied to a person the way Design Principles applies to a creature's deck. Landed as its own file rather than folded into `alignment-checker.md` because it's a generative engine, not a checklist — the existing "Stuck on an NPC" bullet there now points to it instead of duplicating it. Also carries the Seats-as-voice-engine technique (relationship to a Seat, not representation of one — four people, one domain, four irreconcilable readings) and a location-coverage rule (loves it / hates it / refuses to interpret it — the third case is the one that gets skipped). Explicitly scoped against overreach: a full repo scan run just before this (`ToolSearch`/`Agent`, not logged separately since it produced no file changes) found several characters — Weck, Moth, Steve, Cole — whose thinness is already load-bearing by design, so the new file states plainly that it doesn't override an intentional gap, only closes an accidental one. Found the real gap while scanning: Vulture's Nest's named commercial layer (Dess Harwick, Salla, Harlow, Bean) reads almost entirely as function, the predictable result of a location built dense enough to need names for its stalls before anyone had a reason to give those names an interior life. That cluster is the queued next target, not yet started.

**[2026-08-07] A3 — Declaring a Price corrected: the statement is the Price, not a card reveal.** Drew's direct clarification, pasted as finished rule text: *"The statement is the Price. Not the reveal. There isn't one at this time... Breaking the pledge incurs a higher cost."* Previously the ritual revealed-and-buried an Oracle card at the moment of declaration, same shape as the session-end Price step; that's gone. The declaration alone is the Price now — a pledge the fiction treats as binding from that moment, with a real, larger cost (curse, forced card, permanent mark, GM's call) only if it's ever broken. **This cascaded into three other files that had assumed the old mechanic**, all fixed same pass: `rules/character-creation.md`'s Advancement section and `places/island-in-a-ship.md`'s Ritual Flow both had an "Oracle may remember" note (added earlier today) that explicitly compared a session-Price card against "a creation Price" card — no longer coherent with nothing revealed at creation, simplified to just compare against earlier session Prices. `places/island-in-a-ship.md`'s Setting the Tone section (also written earlier today) had built a whole two-reveal structure around this — a "rehearsal beat" card explicitly justified as practice for "the real Price card" that would follow the declaration. Both reveals removed; the declaration's weight now comes from the pledge and the held silence alone, which is arguably a tighter fit for what Drew actually described building toward in the first place. **Checked the six new Glasslight/Promise NPCs (Rue, Corren, Marren, Sael, Wren) against this before touching anything else** — all five that reference Declaring a Price talk about an unpaid "cost" or "declaration," never a specific card, so all five read as fully compatible with the corrected mechanic with zero edits needed. Worth noting for how close that was: written a few turns earlier, before this correction existed, and they happened to be abstract enough to survive it.

**[2026-08-07] A1 — Six new Glasslight/Promise NPCs shipped: Rue, Corren, Marren, Thess, Sael, Wren.** Drew asked for voices the repo was missing — rage, love, cruelty, self-punishment, xenophobia, control — then closed with a standing principle rather than a request: *"we don't not build this game for the players. the world does not exist because of them. it continues on like it always had and the players sometimes wind up in the middle of it. that's canon."* Checked `world/tonal-bible.md` before writing anything — that principle is already there almost verbatim ("Not heroic fantasy... the world is not organized around the protagonist's journey"), so this wasn't new doctrine to add, it was the existing standard to hold every file to: none of the six reference the party, all six have a private life that runs whether or not anyone ever meets them. Placed per Drew's own draft, all in Glasslight Reach / the Promise congregation there — the town's established "loud certainty could crack something" culture does real work as a pressure vessel for Rue's controlled anger and Marren's precision cruelty. **Thess is a second failed Echo in the same town as Kaine (`characters/kaine.md`)** — not a collision, an intentional contrast (same failure, opposite processing: Kaine turned his outward into doctrine, Thess turned his inward into penance) — and followed Kaine's own established restraint on which Seat was reached for: unnamed in both files, on purpose. **"The Oracle's price wasn't paid" is carried as folk cosmology, not a literal replay of the Session 0 ritual** — none of these six sat at the Oracle's table; each just made a real declaration in-world that has the same shape a binding statement does (`rules/character-creation.md`, Declaring a Price) and never had a cost collected on it. That's an interpretive call, not a redefinition: the Oracle ritual itself stays exactly what it's always been, player-facing and mechanical; this only borrows its language to describe something true about how these six carry an old promise. Cross-referenced from `places/glasslight-reach.md` and a new Related Documents section on `factions/people-of-promise.md`, which didn't have one yet.

**[2026-08-07] A2 — The Silent Choir built: origin site for the Silent Blade, guardian Fermata, and a new companion Artifact, the Silent Ring.** Drew: *"build out the surrounding location for the silent blade... a guardian entity that uses song to protect the blade... another companion item that the guardian uses. the silent ring."* Asked for target CTR and placement before building, per `CLAUDE.md`'s Stat Blocks instruction — Drew chose above-curve legendary (~CTR 40+) and a new, deliberately unmapped location rather than folding into existing geography. **Fermata** (`bestiary/fermata.md`): Mind 10/Body 12/Soul 20, HP 66 (formula, not bespoke), **CTR 42** — explicitly calibrated to be survivable, not fair, against the 3-player baseline of 27. Bespoke 12-card deck against a 42-card formula baseline, same exception `bestiary/hullback.md` already set precedent for. **No new Seat invented** — silence/song had no existing cosmological home (checked `mythology/seats.md` in full first), and rather than claim one, the Silent Ring's alignment is left explicitly open, same technique already established for the Fog Goggles (`items/fog-basin-items.md`: "which Seat, if any, is open design, not a gap to close"). **The mechanical core reuses existing vocabulary rather than inventing new keywords**: Locked and Sealed (both already in `rules/card-glossary.md`) carry the "silencing" identity: THE HELD NOTE denies a random card from a hand entirely (not just a turn — the first bestiary use of Locked), SEALED CHORD strips equipped item passives mid-fight. STILLNESS (`cards/blue-mind.md` — already flavored *"Certainty is loud. Silence holds its shape"*) and READ (`cards/green-soul.md` — forces a hand reveal) both got pulled into the deck specifically because they were already thematically perfect, unprompted, the same kind of resonance SHARPEN turned out to have with Deadly earlier this session. **The location's real content is a table rule, not just flavor** (`places/the-silent-choir.md`, The Ringing Silence): players communicate only by gesture or written note for the whole scene, matching the same "the rule works by being real" principle the Session 0 tone section already established. Reveal Hand gets a fictional reskin (shown silently instead of stated aloud) with zero change to the actual keyword. **A genuine non-combat resolution exists** — waiting quietly, without attacking, can end the encounter with no cards played — matching Drew's "not necessarily expected to win outright" framing and Root Heart's own precedent for a fight that isn't the only way through. **The Silent Blade got its real home**, closing the "pending a real home" note from its own ship earlier today: moved out of `items/consumables.md` into a new `items/the-silent-choir-items.md` alongside the Ring, with an in-fiction reason for why a Tier 3 weapon already loose in the world doesn't imply a second one's sitting here for the taking (Fermata guards the *site*, not a lootable duplicate). **A real, unplanned interaction, named rather than left silent**: a party that already owns a Silent Blade and brings it into this fight would have SEALED CHORD shut off their own weapon's passive mid-combat — the guardian silencing the very blade it once lost, which reads as intentional even though it wasn't planned that way going in.

**[2026-08-07] A2 — The Adventurers' Hall's contract board written out, pulling every straggler location into a real hook.** Drew: *"make a list of official adventurers guild job postings. try and pull on all the straggler locations, make up a potential hook if there isn't one already."* No new institution invented — the Hall already existed in `places/capital/capital.md` ("a long contract board... the public desk for the freelancer branch of Lord Cedric's military"), just never had its board's actual contents written. New file `places/capital/adventurers-hall.md`, cross-referenced from capital.md same as the Waterworks/Wallows/Bazaar/Tavern split-outs. Checked every location file for an existing quest reference first (`quests/*.md` cross-refs) to find the real stragglers: Apnea, Canille, Clayhollow, Pneum, Veldmire, Weatherheart Vale, and the Abyssal Trench all got postings, five of them (Apnea, Pneum, Clayhollow, Veldmire, Weatherheart Vale) wired to hooks the location file already had — Canille was the one genuine invention, and it's deliberately just a paperwork lapse (correspondence overdue) with zero hint of the cubs, masks, or Founding Hall, matching the file's own Tone section on purpose. **Havenrise, Roaat, and RavenHold got a second tier — "Word of Mouth, Not on the Board"** — since an official posting can't point at a destination that's either explicitly blank (`places/havenrise.md`, `places/roaat.md` both say "treat as blank region") or explicitly unmappable by design (RavenHold, since archived — `archives/ravenhold.md`). Framed as missing-persons rumors instead, which doesn't require inventing anything about the places themselves. Same section also carries the Underground Bazaar's already-written Mother of Many fight (its own real reward, a FourthEye pass) — Bazaar business, not Regency business, so it stays off the official board even though it's the most concrete job of the bunch. Nothing here touches the FourthEye pipeline itself, which stays exactly as gated as `memory.md`'s own B-thread note already says ("don't develop until party pulls on it").

**[2026-08-07] A1 — Session 0's ceremonial tone written into `places/island-in-a-ship.md`.** Drew wrote out the full design in chat — the ritual shouldn't feel like "now we do character creation," it should feel like a conversation that slowly becomes something else. Landed as a new "Setting the Tone" subsection under Session 0 — First Visit, before Deck Building: four tone principles, a rehearsal card-reveal beat kept explicitly distinct from the real, formal Price (avoids reading as a second Price when only one exists per `rules/character-creation.md`, Declaring a Price), and the actual opening script as ordered beats. Only expresses mechanics that already exist (Name/Price/Distance, Declaring a Price) in a performance register — no new rule. Trimmed Drew's chat framing down to table-usable GM voice; the meta-commentary about why the piece was being designed didn't travel into canon. One-line cross-reference added from `rules/gm-guide.md`'s Using the Oracle section, rather than duplicating the script in both places.

**[2026-08-07] A1 — The Silent Blade built, closing the item held back from the last review.** Drew: *"silent blade is a tier 3 weapon. every attack gains deadly... whatever is best for the way the repo works though."* First pass routed the recurring grant through Anchored (`rules/card-glossary.md`), matching `items/briarwatch-items.md`'s SPLIT WEDGE — then Drew asked for it without Anchored, naming the real restriction himself: Melee only (Both range counts too, but only when actually played at melee range). Reworked to a plain Range-gated passive instead — every attack this weapon makes gains Deadly while the attack is Melee, or Both thrown from melee range (`rules/combat.md`, Range Matrix) — no Anchored, no position-holding fragility, no new keyword: Melee/Ranged/Both is already the repo's vocabulary for exactly this condition. Whole Tier 3 budget on the one effect, no flat damage stacked on; comparable in raw expectation to the plain +3 flat-damage default Tier 3 spend (Deadly's d6 averages 3.5), just variance instead of a flat number, gated to closing to melee range. No narrative source was given in the original three-line draft, so it wasn't invented — filed under No Fixed Source (`items/consumables.md`, retitled "Items — No Fixed Source" now that it holds equipment too, same subheading pattern `items/turnroot-weald-items.md` already uses) pending a real home. **Sim-checked before treating the budget math as settled, per Drew's own ask.** A monkeypatched `Duel.attack` (scratch script, not a permanent engine feature — equipment isn't part of the sim's content model) granted a just-in-time Deadly stack on qualifying attacks, clawed back if unconsumed so it couldn't leak onto a later non-melee swing. Result: every matchup swung 15–21 points (Steele vs Frost 43.8%→61.6%; Steele vs Mire 53.7%→74.5%), because instrumentation showed 87.5% of Steele's attacks were already melee-range under tactician play — a Body-heavy attacker essentially never leaves Frontline, so the "melee only" restriction barely restricts the archetype who'd actually buy this weapon. Reported to Drew as a CRITICAL-severity finding by the repo's own red-team scale, with three options (accept as a genuinely best-in-class capstone; cap it to the first melee attack each turn; something else) rather than quietly downgrading a shipped item. **Drew's call: accepted as-is.** No further changes made; the +3-flat-damage budget comparison in the paragraph above undersold its actual measured value for a melee-postured wielder, and that's now a known, confirmed-intentional property of the item rather than an unexamined assumption.

**[2026-08-05] A3 — Apnea shipped: the last file held on hold in `experimental/` finally has its three gaps filled.** Drew: *"ship the apnea.md too."* All three explicitly-pending pieces (the nightmare's nature/origin/resolvability, the Old Man's name and characterization, the story hook) built and confirmed with Drew before writing final prose. **The nightmare reuses existing cosmology rather than inventing new:** the village is a contained, tended pocket of the same Three-Cuts thinness the In-Between wears diffusely (`world/the-in-between.md`) — once held by a rotation of dream-born wardens walked and witnessed at night (Havens' own principle: "a safe place is a place someone is paying to hold," here paid in being seen), that rotation thinned and stopped, and what's Out There past the edge is unheld thinness with no fixed shape, deliberately left uncatalogued. **Tobin** (the Old Man, named) was one of the last wardens; something on his last walk under, never told to anyone, is why he stopped and retreated to the smaller, safer job of tending IVs — the specific loss stays a GM Secret, unresolved on purpose, same register as Aege's fate. **The hook**: a capital family paid Lily (`characters/lily.md`) to learn whether a relative sent to Apnea is still alive — dream-years don't track waking-years — and she told them only that something at the island is close to slipping. **The Lily question, confirmed with Drew before writing:** one soft breadcrumb line added to her own file (*"Apnea sits differently with her than her other clients' nights do... she has never explained it, even to herself"*) — not the richer "she IS the nightmare" version, which was explicitly rejected as too much unrequested weight on a shipped character from an unshipped draft. Placed on its own lake, south-southwest of Vulture's Nest, in `world/geography-overview.md`'s coordinate table and location summary, deliberately apart from the Canille/Pneum/Coil cluster. `experimental/apnea.md` fully absorbed and removed — `experimental/` now holds only its own `README.md` and `archives/`. Full reasoning in Standing Reasoning.

## Active Reasoning, archived 2026-08-11 (restructure sweep)

Moved wholesale from `memory.md`'s "Active Reasoning," per Drew's 2026-08-11 restructure: *"fold the contents of active reasoning into key design decisions. active reasoning is for the most current work, for reasoning that is currently being used/improved on, not for reasoning that's resolved and not being used currently."* These are the ~40 entries that read as closed — fully absorbed into the canon text they explain, no longer needed to interpret current design. Preserved exactly as originally written, same as the rest of this file. Five threads that were still genuinely open, embedded as trailing sentences inside otherwise-closed entries here, were pulled out to a live home (Active Pending Threads or `unresolved-concerns.md`) before this move — see the 2026-08-11 Recently-shipped entry for which went where.

**[2026-08-06] Harness review overhead — two audits pointed at different bloat, and only cross-checking both against this session's actual ships separated the adoptable half from the risky half.** `experimental/harness` proposed a Phase 1 Intake / Phase 2 Integration / Phase 3 Arbitration split of the Canon Gate — cheap conflict-detection, cheap mechanical propagation, and only real conflicts get full reasoning-token spend — plus a "default to integrate, not evaluate" mindset shift. The older Harness Audit Feedback thread separately named `CLAUDE.md`'s jurisdiction sprawl and `memory.md`'s drift into a second repository. These aren't the same complaint: one is about the review *process* applied per task, the other is about the *files* the process writes into. Treating them as one bundle would have meant adopting or rejecting both wholesale.
  - **My own audit, run before touching either document, found the real cost was neither of the two named targets.** `CLAUDE.md` never once caused friction this session — the jurisdiction-sprawl complaint is true in the abstract but wasn't costing anything in practice. `memory.md`'s growth was the real, measured cost (591 lines, ~150 added this session alone), and a third cost neither audit named: I was independently writing the same reasoning twice per ship, once compressed in Recently Shipped and once expanded in Active Reasoning, regardless of whether the ship actually needed judgment or was purely mechanical.
  - **The harness's central claim — skip full review when a cheap pass finds no conflict — was tested against the same week's actual ships, not evaluated in the abstract, and failed.** Two of the five files processed in this exact batch (`weaver rooms`, `guilds`) each carried a real contradiction with established canon that only a full semantic read caught: Eveline's proposed direct access to Aurora would have broken "the Weavers never go near where she is"; the guilds draft asserted Percival had already been warned when the Table #9 letter is established as hidden and undelivered. Neither is a syntactic conflict a cheap classifier (duplicate names, broken references) would flag — both require holding the current shape of the world in mind while reading, which for this repo's failure mode *is* the expensive step the proposal wanted to reserve for Arbitration only. A formal two-gate structure here either repeats that same full read twice or genuinely skips it; there's no cheap middle step for narrative contradiction the way there is for a codebase's static-analysis-catchable conflicts. This also directly tests against Drew's own standing, dated instruction in `CLAUDE.md` ("it's more important than ever that you give anything found in experimental a thorough review") rather than just deferring to it — the instruction held up under the test, so it stays.
  - **What the harness got right, and what `verify.py` already proves works for this repo, is separating semantic judgment from mechanical execution — just not at the conflict-detection stage.** Once a conflict is resolved, propagating a rename or fixing a cross-reference really is deterministic and doesn't need essay treatment. The HP-formula-consistency check (Harness Audit point 3, shipped 2026-08-05) is the correct shape of this idea already proven in the repo: it converted one specific, genuinely mechanical sub-check into a script rather than skipping semantic review anywhere. The adopted change (write-up length scaled to decision difficulty, Active Reasoning reserved for genuine judgment calls) extends that same proven pattern to the review's own output, rather than to what gets reviewed.
  - **Rejected, not silently — recorded in `CLAUDE.md` itself so it isn't re-proposed without the evidence that killed it once.** The "default to integrate" mindset and the formal phase-gate structure are not adopted. This is written directly into the "Reviewing `experimental/` before it ships" paragraph, not just left in this log, because the failure mode being guarded against is exactly a future session (or a future external brainstorm) re-suggesting the same shortcut without knowing it already met two counterexamples in the same week it was proposed.

**[2026-08-06] Eveline/Weavers — the fix was finding a seam already built into an existing character, not inventing a new one.** The draft's central claim (Eveline has direct, physical access near Aurora) couldn't ship as written without contradicting a load-bearing sentence already in canon. The instinct to reject the draft outright, or to accept it and quietly patch the contradiction, were both wrong moves — the actual answer was already sitting in her Behavioral Contract: "no backstory, no motivation, that's a table discovery," "she has been on the council longer than any record accounts for." A character explicitly built to be an unexplained exception is exactly the right shape to *become* a structural exception to a rule everyone else follows, without weakening the rule for anyone else. The Weavers' "never go near where she is" stays true for every Weaver who isn't Eveline; only her already-established specialness absorbs the new access. This is the same move as reusing Eveline's silence for the Custodians paragraph and reusing Harlan/Harran's near-identical names for the Forge combine, earlier this same batch — new draft material gets checked for a seam in what's already shipped before anything gets rewritten to fit it.
  - **Why "alongside, not through" mattered as its own decision, not just phrasing.** The existing Weavers structure has exactly one secret channel: the Quiet Chair answers to Oswald's circle for the Grid's political direction. Routing Eveline's new access through that same channel would have made her Oswald's subordinate by implication, which nothing in her existing "attends everything, votes nothing," longer-than-any-record character supports. Keeping her channel parallel — she supplies the material, the Quiet Chair still answers for what it's turned into — preserves both existing hierarchies without forcing one to absorb the other, and leaves open (on purpose, matching her contract) whether she even knows what her craft is ultimately feeding.

**[2026-08-05] Apnea — the design risk was never the island, it was an already-shipped character three files away.** Everything genuinely new in this pass (Tobin, the boundary mechanism, the hook) was low-risk by construction — new content in a brand-new file, nothing else depends on it yet. The one place a wrong call would have actually cost something was Lily: her file has stood with a deliberately blank origin, and the design's own strongest instinct (make her the nightmare, or kin to it) would have been the most narratively satisfying answer and the worst one to make from Apnea's side of the relationship. Presenting the range (zero-touch / soft breadcrumb / full identity claim) rather than picking one and defending it after the fact is what let Drew choose the middle option deliberately, instead of either me erring maximally safe (and leaving real texture on the table he might have wanted) or erring toward the richer version because it read better on the page.
  - **Checked the mythology files for a dream-cosmology claim before inventing one, and found none — but that's not the same as license to invent freely.** No Seat, Archon, or Echo covers dream-logic anywhere in this repo, which meant the nightmare's mechanism was genuinely unclaimed ground, not a case of forcing new material into a slot something else already occupied. It also meant nothing forced the *In-Between* connection specifically — that was a design choice (reusing Name/Price/Distance rather than inventing a fourth axis), not a discovered fact, which is exactly why it went to Drew as a real question instead of being asserted as obviously correct. Unclaimed ground is still ground; "nobody's using this yet" isn't the same test as "this is clearly what belongs here."
  - **The GM Secret (what happened to Tobin on his last walk) was left unresolved on purpose, not because the design ran out of room.** This repo's own pattern — Aege's fate, the Root Heart's cause before this session gave it one — is that a mystery earns being left open by being load-bearing to something, not by default. Tobin's unnamed loss is the thing that makes his retreat into IV-tending read as real rather than as a job description; naming it would answer the adventure's actual climax before the adventure exists. Recorded as a deliberate choice in the file itself, not left ambiguous by omission.

**[2026-08-05] Typed modifiers with lifetimes — the scoping-down was the actual work, not the code.** The original brainstorm's example list (Predictable, Axiom's color-ban, Paradox's RPS inversion, Interrupt's cannot-defend, stagger) reads as one uniform category of "rule-benders." It isn't. Checking each example against the live code before writing anything found: "Predictable" doesn't exist in canon at all; `special_reveal` (PARADOX, AFTERIMAGE) and `wins_ties` (EQUAL FOOTING/ADAPT/CERTAINTY) are static fields declared on the `Card` class itself, not runtime state with a lifetime — there's nothing to type because they don't expire, they're just permanent facts about what a card does. Presenting the narrowed scope to Drew before designing anything, rather than either quietly forcing all five examples into one system or quietly dropping the two that didn't fit, is what let "go ahead" actually mean something — he approved the real shape of the work, not the brainstorm's shape.
  - **A second research pass (a Plan agent, cross-checked against the code afterward rather than trusted blind) found detail the first pass and the original note both missed.** `staggered` has two independent consumption points (the defense-eligibility gate, and a separate check at the top of the staggered combatant's own turn) — described in the source note as a single flag with a single lifetime. `keyword_lab.py` sets `staggered` via a bare `setattr()` that bypasses `apply_staggered()`/`debuff()` entirely — a real dependency invisible from `engine.py`/`team_engine.py` alone, and a naive refactor that removed the raw attribute would have broken that tool with no exception raised, no test failure, just silently-wrong keyword tests from then on.
  - **`Combatant.ongoing` looked like existing infrastructure for exactly this problem, and checking it in detail is what confirmed it wasn't.** Same superficial shape (per-combatant, list-like, kind-tagged) as what the North Star wanted, and reusing existing infrastructure over inventing new structure is this repo's own standing preference. But `ongoing` is architecturally for *recurring* effects re-applied every turn while a position condition holds (healing ticks, stat buffs) — axiom_ban/cannot_defend/staggered are *one-shot* blocks that fire once and expire, never re-tick. Forcing them into `ongoing`'s shape would have meant new special-case handling in `_ongoing_support_tick` for effects that don't tick at all — more complexity than the plain-dict alternative, not less. Worth recording as a real "checked, not just assumed" negative result, not a shortcut skipped.
  - **The AXIOM bug (never clears, in either engine) was found as a side effect of reading the exact lines this refactor needed to touch, not gone looking for.** Confirmed with Drew before fixing anything: close the "never clears" half (matches the card's own documented text, one line, provably safe via a scripted before/after test), leave the "never checked on the banned combatant's own attack" half alone as a separate, smaller, explicitly logged thread. A code-quality pass finding a real bug is not, on its own, permission to fix everything adjacent to it — the redefinition test applies to engine behavior the same way it applies to lore.
  - **Verification matched the size of the actual behavior change, not the size of the diff.** Most of this change (the property-backed dict) is provably behavior-identical by construction — every old call site still runs the same code path, just through a getter/setter instead of a bare attribute, confirmed by direct callback checks. Exactly one line changes real behavior (the AXIOM clear), and that's the one line that got a dedicated scripted before/after test proving the old behavior (ban never clears) and the new behavior (ban clears on next reveal) directly, plus duel/battle series through both engines afterward as a broader sanity net.
  - **Closed 2026-08-06:** the attacker-side gap logged here as its own small deferred decision got its own decision and shipped — see the entry below. Step 3 (the full policy stack) remains explicitly deferred, not abandoned; nothing about closing Step 2's loose end changes that sequencing call.

**[2026-08-06] AXIOM's attacker-side gap — the scoping conversation feared a policy-brain-by-policy-brain rewrite; the actual fix was two lines in one shared function.** Presented to Drew as a real fork: a post-hoc voided reveal (matches the existing defense-side pattern, architecturally cheap, but diverges from the card's literal "cannot play that color" text) versus a true pre-selection filter (accurate to the text, but feared to require touching every policy brain — random/reader/greedy/tactician/punisher — across both `engine.py` and `team_engine.py`, "closer to a Step 3 change than a Step 2 fix"). Drew picked the accurate option: *"True pre-selection filter."*
  - **The fear was wrong, and finding out why is the actual lesson.** Every one of those policy brains' `choose_action` already routes attack-card selection through exactly one shared function per engine — `legal_attacks()` (`policies.py`) and `legal_attacks_team()` (`team_policies.py`) — which in turn call `can_attack()`/`can_attack_t()` for range legality. A third site, DOUBLE DOWN's bonus attack in `content.py`, independently reimplements the same selection shape but still calls the same `can_attack()` directly (`content.py` deliberately doesn't import the policy layer, per its own header comment, but does import `engine.py`). All three sites already funneled through one true chokepoint per engine — the ban only needed to land there, not be threaded through five separate brains. Assuming an architecture change requires touching every call site, without first checking whether those call sites already share a function, was the actual scoping error in the original question to Drew.
  - **`can_attack()` is nominally a range-legality function; extending its job to also cover the axiom ban is a real, if small, scope widen — worth naming rather than sliding in quietly.** The alternative (checking the ban separately inside each of the three call sites) would have meant writing the same two-line check three times instead of once, purely to keep `can_attack()`'s name matching its historical scope exactly. Reused the existing chokepoint on purpose, same reasoning as `Combatant.ongoing` getting checked-and-rejected for the Step 2 pass: matching an existing shape saves more than a narrower, more purpose-built name would earn.
  - **Verified the fix does what "cannot play" means, not just what "can_attack returns False" means.** Confirmed `legal_attacks()` genuinely excludes the banned-color card from the returned list (not just that the specific card fails a check in isolation), confirmed a combatant with only banned-color cards in hand gets an empty legal-attacks list (falls through to move/idle, same as any other total legality lockout), and confirmed the mirrored team-side filter (`can_attack_t`) behaves identically. Also confirmed, via a direct forced-through `attack()` call bypassing selection entirely, that the ban still clears on reveal even if some future caller skipped the selection-time filter — the consumption logic doesn't silently depend on the filter always running first.

**[2026-08-05] `places/capital/` and `experimental/The Unheld` — a structural move and a doctrine-formalization pass, kept distinct rather than folded into one another.** Drew: *"the capital is getting big let's give it its own sub folder in locations. then move to the file named the unheld. it's my design notes."*
  - **The subfolder scope was read from the file's own Related Documents, not guessed.** `capital.md` already names, in its own footer, exactly the six files that are structurally *of* the capital (topside, the Tusk, and the four-file underground stack) versus the ones merely *near* it (the ruins, the basin, the Coil legend). The reorg followed that existing boundary rather than drawing a new one — nothing in `capital.md`'s own text treats abyssal-ruins or fog-basin as part of the capital's structure, so neither moved.
  - **This is the first time this repo has split a top-level content directory into subfolders.** No existing precedent to match (`experimental/archives/` is a different pattern — cut content, not active structure), so `places/capital/capital.md` keeps its own basename inside the new folder rather than becoming an `index.md` or `overview.md` — minimizes the diff to a directory-prefix change and keeps every cross-reference's target file recognizable at a glance.
  - **`experimental/The Unheld` bundled a proven-but-unwritten practice with a genuinely new doctrine addition, and they got different treatment.** The Canon Gate role (*"give anything found in experimental a thorough review"*) had already been running for two full ships before this — writing it into `CLAUDE.md` now is documentation catching up to practice, not a new decision. The tonal note (*"self inflicted suffering is not only common, it's mandatory"*) is different: nothing currently in `world/tonal-bible.md` states it, and it's sharper than the nearest existing line (*"Sacrifice costs something real"*) — a real doctrine addition, not a restatement. Asked Drew where each piece should land rather than assuming both were the same kind of change.
  - **The tonal-bible addition was grounded in existing canon on purpose, not written as abstract doctrine.** Citing the Weavers' extraction, the Tavern's barter economy, and the People of Promise's ritual bathing as the concrete shape of "self-inflicted suffering" means a future reader checking whether new content fits this principle has three real examples to compare against, not just an adjective to match.
  - **The epigraph quote in the source file** (*"Nothing can be spoken of it. Anything that can be spoken cannot be understood."*) **was deliberately not promoted into `world/the-unheld.md` as in-world dialogue or doctrine.** It reads like it could be one, but nothing in this pass asked for new Unheld cosmology, and inventing a speaker or source for it would be exactly the kind of unrequested extrapolation the Translation Principle's redefinition test exists to catch. Left out rather than guessed at; the source file is gone, but the line itself isn't lost — it's sitting here if Drew wants to place it deliberately later.
  - **Both experimental source files removed outright, not archived, matching the Weavers/Waterworks precedent from earlier the same day** — everything in `experimental/The Unheld` landed somewhere real, so keeping the draft around would only create a second, staler copy of decisions already made.

**[2026-08-05] The Weavers and the Waterworks — a design reversal handed off as a directive, and a geography restructure that needed a conflict scan, not a re-design.** Drew: *"everything in experimental is already reviewed. the last step is a handoff to you to scan for any conflicts. if there are no conflicts then push it to canon."*
  - **The Weavers/Demon Court reversal is Drew's call, not something surfaced and negotiated.** The prior draft (`experimental/the-weavers.md`, 2026-08-04) stated flatly: *"No relationship, and the absence is the point."* Drew's newest instruction directly contradicts that: the Weavers' infrastructure extends into the labor level, where prisoners physically harvest the threads. The right move here was not to flag this as a collision and stop — it's Drew reopening his own prior call, in the same session, with full context. The job was to execute the reversal cleanly across every place the old framing was asserted (the relationship section, the Quiet Chair paragraph, the faction cross-reference), not just the one sentence that stated it most directly.
  - **Reused existing cover language instead of inventing new "nobody knows" scaffolding.** `factions/demon-court.md` already had *"Overseers already know and have decided not to think about it"* doing exactly this job for the threshold/Deep boundary. The new collection detail reuses the same posture rather than requiring a second, parallel explanation for why the Court doesn't ask questions about things below its own jurisdiction.
  - **The geography restructure was checked for collision, not redesigned from scratch.** `world/geography-overview.md`'s existing river web names no capital-specific rivers, so the Waterworks' four new ones (Vein, Mourn, Ledger, Quiet Arm) extend it rather than duplicate or contradict anything already on the page. The Diamond Shelf stays untouched — the new prisoner assignment is a separate gallery, not a repurposing of established, named territory.
  - **Scope was checked rather than assumed.** "Everything in experimental is already reviewed" read as a plausible blanket statement, but the active conversation thread was specifically Weavers/Waterworks — asked Drew directly rather than guessing whether it also meant shipping unrelated, undiscussed drafts (The Unheld, items, new content, weald's deferred overgrowth-phase line) in the same pass. Confirmed: Weavers/Waterworks only.
  - **Both experimental source files removed outright, not archived.** Unlike a cut card or a rejected extrapolation, nothing here was left out — the entire content of both drafts is now live in canon, so keeping the experimental copies around would only create two sources of truth for the same material.

**[2026-08-04] Aege's family and the Holdfast — first real test of the elevated Canon Gate role, and it found one thing worth naming before shipping.** Drew, in `experimental/The Unheld`: *"Claude your role is starting to shift more towards being the Canon Gate for the repo... give anything found in experimental a thorough review."* This was the first piece landed under that standard, and the standard justified itself once: reading the draft against current canon rather than just against itself surfaced something a same-file read wouldn't have.
  - **The draft closes a thread `characters/aege.md` protects on purpose, and that needed to be said out loud, not just executed.** The pre-existing Backstory: *"Whether this is the Root Heart stirring is a live question, not a decided one."* The draft states it as settled fact. This is Drew's own call — dated the same day, explicit — so the right move was never to refuse it; it was to name that a deliberately-open thread was the thing being closed, so the closure is a decision Drew is making with full information rather than a side effect of accepting the prose around it. Surfaced in the plan; the plan was approved with the line included, which is the record of that being a conscious yes.
  - **Checked for reuse before checking for conflict, and found real craft, not just risk.** The cord-wrist detail (*"the same grammar as the Knot-Keeper's bracelets, practiced without the sacrifice"*) checks out exactly against `quests/turnroot-weald-adventure.md`'s actual Knot-Keeper section — a bracelet Mark that costs a personality trait. Whoever wrote the draft read the source material and used it correctly, including getting right that the Holdfast's people pay a lighter version of a real, specific cost. Worth recording because it's evidence the other AI conversation this came out of wasn't just improvising color — it earns being taken seriously on the parts that weren't flagged.
  - **The Rootstalker shift was named rather than smoothed, on purpose, per the red-team evolution check.** *"Rootstalkers hunting forest-people"* collides with their whole established identity — *"it doesn't kill to eat, it kills to prune,"* a custom-enforcer, not an open predator — and the Holdfast family explicitly keeps every custom there is. Two ways to handle that: let a GM discover the tension unprompted, or state it. Chose the second — `bestiary/rootstalker.md` now says outright, *"It no longer needs a violation."* Read generously this is a good horror beat (the forest's own rules fraying, which is exactly what Drew's tonal note in `The Unheld` was asking for); read carelessly it's an unexamined contradiction. Naming it converts it from the second reading to the first, regardless of which one a table lands on.
  - **Two things arrived in the same source file and got left out on purpose, not by accident.** An unmarked paragraph extrapolating the forest-people's diet, clothing, and crafts (*"we can extrapolate how their survival mechanics... logically take shape"*) — unhedged invention in a different voice than the rest of the draft, exactly what the Translation Principle's redefinition test exists to catch, and the spare register the real draft uses (*"no permanent structures that would insult the canopy"*) is the better version specifically because it doesn't itemize. And a single unmarked line claiming the Root Heart has a periodic "overgrowth phase," which nothing in `bestiary/root-heart.md` supports and which sat at the tail of an unrelated section. Both are exactly the shape of thing that's easy to wave through if a reviewer is checking "does this fit the tone" rather than "does every claim in here have a source" — the second question is the one that actually matters, and it's the one the elevated review standard was for.

**[2026-08-04] The nine-card audit triage — researching the sim before designing each fix caught real complications, and the collision sweep for one card (CHARGE) turned up staleness in six unrelated files.** Drew triaged the three-agent audit into nine per-card instructions, ranging from exact ("fix CHARGE," "drop 'melee only'") to open ("STUDY/PROFILE take a swing," "FLOW needs reworked").
  - **BREAK's "melee only" turned out to be provably decorative, not just probably.** `_break_defense` only checks `foe._redirect_dmg` (the clean-win signal) — no positional logic anywhere. Confirms Drew's read directly rather than just deferring to it.
  - **SLIPSTREAM's Effect was already unmodeled before the reword**, and stays that way — the ongoing-marker registration is real and tested (clears correctly on Collapse), but nothing in the turn-processing loop ever reads it. "The next time" fixes the *contradiction with Anchored's glossary definition*; it doesn't newly implement a mechanic that was never implemented. Worth being precise about, since "fixed the wording" and "fixed the mechanic" are different claims and only one is true here.
  - **PROFILE's new Defensive Bonus ("look at the attacker's hand") isn't a new category of unmodeled effect** — `_read_effect`'s own comment already establishes the precedent: *"'defender must reveal their hand' — pure info, no state change (BREAK's own precedent)."* The reader-policy AI has full information regardless; hand-reveal and hand-peek effects are consistently no-ops in this sim, and PROFILE's rework just joins that existing, already-justified category rather than inventing a new kind of gap.
  - **STUDY and FLOW's fixes were genuine design calls, made and stated as such rather than hedged.** STUDY's Deadly (pure combat keyword) became Scry 1 (pure card-manipulation) to match its Effect's identity — a smaller, more defensible move than PROFILE's, since Scry 1 was already a proven mechanic elsewhere (ALIGN) rather than a new no-op category. FLOW's rework added Gain Evade rather than just lowering the die, on the reasoning that a die cut would have made FLOW *weaker than SWAY at the same tier* instead of *differently good* — SWAY moves the defender, FLOW moves yourself; giving FLOW real payload preserves that as two distinct niches instead of one card quietly outclassing the other.
  - **CHARGE's own Range fix collided with the Oracle's pinned composition again — the third time this exact shape has come up tonight (after GORE and STILLNESS) — and this time the right call was to do nothing about it.** CHARGE sits in `ORACLE_DECK`'s Red Melee-12, but that whole list is already frozen and explicitly marked pending-propagation from the Oracle-clearing decision earlier tonight (*"do not 'fix' this by emptying the code to match; wait for the hand-built list"*). Swapping in a replacement card, the way GORE→INTERCEPT worked, would have meant touching a file Drew already said not to touch until his hand-build is ready. Flagged in the plan and here, not silently skipped.
  - **The real yield of the collision sweep wasn't CHARGE at all — it was finding that FOCUS, PROFILE, and FLOW's restated text had already gone stale in five character files** (`kino.md` from checking BREAK; `aege.md`, `masaharu.md`, `greed.md`, `ryder.md` from checking PROFILE and FLOW), independent of tonight's batch — FOCUS's staleness traces back to the *Initiative Shift rework earlier this session*, not to anything in this pass. This is the same failure shape as `rules/combat-example.md`'s STILLNESS collision two passes ago: a card's canonical text moves, and a restatement elsewhere doesn't move with it, silently, until something forces a reader to check that exact card in that exact file. Six separate instances of it, found only because the collision-check habit is now routine rather than optional.
  - **A separate, larger bug surfaced and was deliberately left alone.** rules/combat-example.md *(archived 2026-08-06)* labels PROFILE `Range: Both` and builds an entire legality argument on that label — but PROFILE is actually `Ranged`-only, meaning the scene as written would be illegal under the real rule (Frost and the Scratcher are both Frontline in that beat; Ranged is illegal there, Both would be legal). This isn't adjacent to tonight's PROFILE edit (which only touched the Defensive Bonus, not Range) and isn't a one-word fix — correcting the label without rewriting the scene's positions would just replace a wrong claim with a broken one. Left unfixed and flagged rather than pulled into this batch's scope.

**[2026-08-04] "Delete the Oracle folder and its contents entirely" got a pause and a scoping question, not immediate execution — and the answer narrowed the blast radius a lot.** The instruction, taken literally, would have removed a directory `CLAUDE.md`'s own canonical structure table describes as *"the shared starter pool players draft from at character creation and draw from at end of session,"* referenced from `README.md`, `memory.md`, `combatsimulations/content.py`, and `printing/generate-cards.py` — and would have discarded, with zero stated reason, a composition that had just received a fixed 12/6/3 target, seven newly-written cards, and a bug-driven card swap, all in this same session. That combination — documented shared infrastructure, wide reference fan-out, hours of very recent deliberate work, no reason given — is exactly the shape of request this repo's own safety posture says to confirm rather than execute silently.
  - **Asked with three concrete options rather than a bare "are you sure," so the answer could be specific rather than a yes/no.** Cut the mechanic entirely with full cleanup / delete just the file, leave the code dangling / something narrower. Drew's actual answer matched none of the three exactly: *"the mechanics are staying but the folder needs to be at least emptied. I'm going to build it by hand from the coreset right before session 1."* The mechanic (the rule, the eligibility criteria, the 12/6/3 target) stays; only the specific 63-card selection goes, because Drew wants to make that selection himself rather than use mine.
  - **Archived rather than deleted, unprompted — this is house convention, not a new call.** `CLAUDE.md`'s Release-mode guidance says superseded `memory.md` entries move to `archives/`, *"untouched, not deleted"* — cut draft cards already do this via `experimental/archives/`. The 63-card build represented real, reusable design reasoning (which keywords were at zero and got covered, why HEAVE AND HAUL was too strong for a starter pool, the eligibility bar that eliminated three "Wins ties" cards as a set) that Drew may want to reference even though he's not using the selection as-is. Deleting it outright would have destroyed working reasoning to satisfy an instruction that only asked for the pool to be *empty*, not for the reasoning to be *gone*. `archives/oracle-pool-2026-08-03.md` holds it verbatim, with a header explaining why it's there and that it's a past state, not current truth — matching `archives/key-design-decisions.md`'s own preservation format exactly.
  - **A gap left deliberately, and named as a judgment call rather than buried.** `combatsimulations/content.py`'s `ORACLE_DECK` and `printing/generate-cards.py`'s oracle card list still contain the full old 63-card composition — they are hand-maintained duplicates of what used to be in the markdown file, never generated *from* it, so emptying the markdown broke nothing mechanically; the simulator and print pipeline still run exactly as before. But that also means canon (the now-empty `Oracle/baseoracledeck.md`) and code now disagree about what the Oracle pool contains, and will keep disagreeing until Drew's hand-built version replaces both. Left alone on the reasoning that nobody asked for the code changed and touching working simulator/print logic uninstructed is a real risk for zero requested benefit — but stated plainly, in both this entry and the new file's own text, rather than left for a future reader to discover as an unexplained inconsistency.

**[2026-08-03] The core sets moved toward the Oracle's 12/6/3 shape — this is what "that was my call, if Drew meant core" from the Oracle entry turned out to mean.** Drew: *"for the core set it a rough metric to aim for. we need to bring it closer by going through and changing cards that can support it thematically."* Confirms the Oracle pass's 12/6/3 was never meant to stop at the Oracle — the core sets get it too, at whatever scale their size supports, moved by re-ranging existing cards rather than adding new ones.
  - **"Rough metric," not an exact count, and that distinction did real work.** The Oracle pass fixed nine exact cells (21 = 12+6+3 per colour). Here the instruction was explicitly softer — *aim for*, *bring it closer* — so the target was the Oracle's own **57/29/14 percentage shape**, scaled to each colour's actual size (53/42/45), not a literal 12/6/3. Treating a rough instruction as if it were the exact one would have meant forcing quotas the core sets can't cleanly support (Green's Both pool doesn't have 26 clean candidates any more than the Oracle's did) — same failure mode as reading "even if it means new cards" too literally, inverted.
  - **Existing cards only, per Drew's own phrasing — "changing cards that can support it thematically."** Every move is a re-range of a card already in `cards/`, chosen because its own fiction and mechanics survive the new range, not a card built to fit a slot. This is the opposite operation from the Oracle pass (which added cards because swapping ran out of eligible members) and was chosen deliberately: re-ranging touches a card everywhere it's used — bestiary decks, character decks, the sim, teaching documents — where Oracle-swapping was contained to one file. That wider blast radius is exactly what caught the STILLNESS collision below.
  - **Final shape, all three colours within a couple of points of 57/29/14:**

    | | Primary | Secondary | Tertiary | n |
    |---|---|---|---|---|
    | **Red** (melee-lean) | Melee 30 (57%) | Both 15 (28%) | Ranged 8 (15%) | 53 |
    | **Blue** (ranged-lean) | Ranged 24 (57%) | Melee 12 (29%) | Both 6 (14%) | 42 |
    | **Green** (both-lean) | Both 26 (58%) | Ranged 13 (29%) | Melee 6 (13%) | 45 |

  - **Twenty-one cards moved: Red 5 (Melee → 2 Ranged, 3 Both), Blue 6 (Ranged → Melee), Green 10 (Melee → 7 Both, 3 Ranged).** Selection logic, by colour:
    - **Red.** CERTAIN CONTACT *(renamed **CERTAIN STRIKE** 2026-08-04 — this entry keeps the name it had on the day)* ("Some things you don't have to aim") and DRAIN (stealing a buff, a classic ranged-drain trope) → Ranged. BRACE, DIG IN, PRESS THE WOUND — none of the three has any positional word in its fiction, all three are pure stat/status grants — → Both.
    - **Blue.** RETORT and DEAD END were the two obvious melee-coded picks (Thorns is contact-coded everywhere else in the game; "some paths were never actually open" reads as physically blocking someone's way) — **and both are Oracle members, pinned by the pool composition just fixed.** Passed over for UNNAME (a whispered true name is intimate, not shouted from range), DISTRACT (getting in someone's face), FORGET (a psychic touch), TAINT (a corrupting touch, the same structural card as RED's melee REND), and two picked after the STILLNESS collision below: INTERRUPT and REBUTTAL.
    - **Green.** DUST, BIND, CONSUME, BRISTLE stayed Melee on hard mechanical or fictional locks — DUST's self-Blind only makes sense because *"you kicked it up, you're standing in it,"* the exact reasoning fixed in this same file's rename entry; BRISTLE's flavour literally says *"touch carefully"*; BIND and CONSUME are grappling/devouring, contact by definition. OPENING and TWIN STRIKE kept Melee on judgement (OPENING's "a gap in someone's guard" is a melee image; TWIN STRIKE's name is a strike verb with no thematic pull toward range). The other ten had no positional lock in their text and moved: ATTUNE, PATIENCE, URGENCY, STEADFAST, SHADE AWAY, PATIENCE OF STONE, SEED → Both (waiting, standing firm, evading, and planting a seed at your *position* — none of that requires touching an enemy); WITNESS, VOID, FLOW → Ranged (witnessing is an observing verb; a repositioning card gains nothing from being melee-locked).
  - **A hard mechanical exclusion, caught before it became a candidate: SEISMIC REDIRECT-style Rushdown-gated cards were never in the pool.** No card considered here grants Rushdown or another Frontline-only mechanic; final sweep confirmed zero hazard words (`Frontline`, `Backline`, `melee only`, `Rushdown`) across all 21 final effect/defensive-bonus texts.
  - **The collision: `rules/combat-example.md` is not just prose, it is a load-bearing teaching artifact, and a blind re-range would have broken its own argument.** The worked fight uses STILLNESS by name, twice, as one of two Ranged cards illustrating *"a hand that holds cards can still have no legal defense"* — the exact rule Range exists to teach. Moving STILLNESS to Melee would have made that hand legal, silently reversing the lesson. Caught by grepping all 21 card names against every prose file before committing, not by re-reading the doc cover to cover — the same discipline that caught the Well Property/Western Property collision during the contradiction hunt. **Reverted STILLNESS to Ranged, substituted REBUTTAL** (not in the Oracle, not named anywhere in the teaching doc, "you just have to still be standing when it appears" reads as a close-quarters comeback) — net effect on Blue's count is zero, a straight swap. **PATIENCE's own annotation in the same doc** — *"(Green, Range: Melee — legal, both Frontline)"* — needed a one-word fix to Both; the mechanical outcome in that exact scene doesn't change (Both is legal wherever Melee was), so this was a label correction, not a rewrite. FLOW, WITNESS, BRACE, and DISTRACT all appear in the same document's illustrative decks but are never played with an explicit range check in the narrative, so their range changes are safe.
  - **Regression-tested against the standing reference matchup, not just `check_sim`.** `ROSTER['steele']` contains one touched card (FORGET); `ROSTER['frost']` contains none. Frost/Steele mirrors held near 50/50 (47.6–50.1%, the usual turn-order residual), and the head-to-head landed at Frost 60.0% / Steele 37.7% — statistically indistinguishable from the 59.3%/38.9% measured right after the HP-formula revert, before this pass touched anything. Twenty-one range reclassifications did not move the game's own reference matchup outside noise.

**[2026-08-03] The Oracle pool is now a specification, not a lean.** Drew: *"red melee 12, both 6, ranged 3 / blue ranged 12, melee 6, both 3 / green both 12, ranged 6, melee 3 — those counts exactly even if it means new cards."* This turns the range convention he stated earlier (blue ranged / red melee / green both) into a fixed 12/6/3 composition, 21 per colour, 63 total.
  - **I asked which pool and got "no preference," so the choice was mine and is worth recording.** The counts land on the Oracle (20/20/20 → 21 each, a one-card growth, exactly the shape of "even if it means new cards"). Read as the core sets they would have meant retiring **70 canon cards** and leaving the core set smaller than the Oracle drawn from it — structurally incoherent. Took the non-destructive reading. If Drew meant core, that is a separate and much larger job and nothing here blocks it.
  - **Composition was changed by swapping pool membership, never by re-ranging cards.** Re-ranging alters a card everywhere it appears — bestiary decks, character decks, the sim. Swapping which core card sits in the pool touches only the Oracle. No card's printed Range changed in this pass.
  - **Seven new cards, because two slices genuinely could not be filled.** Blue melee needed 6 and the *entire colour* has three melee cards (PREDICT, DEFLECT, ANTICIPATE) — so HESITATE, TELL, SECOND GUESS. Green Both needed 12 and ran out of core candidates clearing the pool's own bar — so SETTLE, GIVE WAY, BRAMBLE, QUICKEN.
  - **The four Green additions are not filler, and the reasoning is the reusable part.** Cutting Green melee from 8 to 3 displaced STEADFAST's Resist, SHADE AWAY's Evade, BRISTLE's Thorns and URGENCY's Initiative. The new cards re-home exactly that coverage from Melee into **Both** — which is where Green's identity now says it belongs. The 12/6/3 rule doesn't just redistribute cards; it says what range a colour's keywords should live at.
  - **Blue's cuts removed the colour's only Initiative Shift cards** (INTERRUPT, REBUTTAL), so HESITATE was built to carry it back. A composition rule can silently strip a pillar from a colour; check coverage after the cuts, not before.
  - **The bar did the cutting, not the counts.** All three *"Wins ties"* cards — EQUAL FOOTING (Red), CERTAINTY (Blue), ADAPT (Green) — are an RPS auto-win, which the pool's own criteria exclude, and all three were sitting in it. They were a deliberate one-per-colour trifecta, so they left **as a set** rather than two-of-three; all three are untouched in core. Same consistency on RETALIATE and REBUTTAL, which share the "if an enemy attacked successfully last turn" precondition.
  - **Red-teamed by measurement, and the first measurement was worthless.** Testing the three 21-card colour slices head to head gave Red 1.4% vs Blue, Red 100% vs Green, Blue 0% vs Green — a perfect RPS cycle. That measures the colour wheel, not the cards; mono-colour decks are degenerate by construction here. **The real test isolates the one variable:** a balanced 12-card deck plus the three Melee originals scored 46.1%, plus the three new Both versions 48.2% — **+2.1 points from the range alone**, against a 50.1% control. Both versions sit *below* control, so the cards dilute a small deck rather than strengthen it. A real but modest buff, stated rather than discovered later.
  - **Two Blue melee cards are functional twins of Red cards** — TELL is ATTRITION's shape, SECOND GUESS is REELING's. Deliberate: the pool's Vulnerable trifecta (OPEN GUARD / MARKED / OPENING) is the same pattern, and a *starter* card should be a plain keyword grant with the colour carried by flavour. Flagged rather than hidden, since it is the kind of thing that reads as laziness if nobody says it was a choice.
  - **The glossary recount was refused, deliberately.** Adding seven cards broke `check_glossary_count`. A recount script I wrote disagreed with the header on 16 keywords *in both directions* — including decreases, which adding cards cannot cause — meaning my counting rule differs from whatever produced those numbers. Updated only the six keywords the seven cards demonstrably touch, and filed the rest as real debt in `unresolved-concerns.md`. Overwriting 16 live numbers from an unvalidated regex is exactly the failure this repo has already had twice.

**[2026-08-03] Three starter cards renamed — and the rename quietly rewrote history until it was caught.** Drew: *"EDDY, PHASE LOGIC, and SMOKESCREEN need replaced with cards that have names that are more basic/starter thematically."* Read as a rename, not a redesign: the property he specified is the name, and all three are already well-shaped d4 starters. Mechanics, dice, ranges, and flavor lines untouched.
  - **The register was measurable, not a matter of taste.** The Oracle pool reads ATTRITION, CHARGE, GUARD, PUSH, PULL, FOCUS, STUDY, DEFLECT, ADAPT, BALANCE, BIND, OPENING, SUPPORT — plain verbs and plain nouns, almost all one word. Against that, **PHASE LOGIC** is two abstract words in a sci-fi register, **EDDY** is an obscure noun, and **SMOKE SCREEN** is a military compound. Drew's read is straightforwardly right; the three were the only names in the pool that needed a second beat to parse.
  - **Chosen for what the card actually does, in the pool's own voice.** **SIDESTEP** (was PHASE LOGIC) — Evade plus an optional reposition is literally stepping aside, and the existing flavor *"You struck the possibility, not the outcome"* keeps the Mind framing that the plainer name drops. **SWAY** (was EDDY) — moving the defender without force is influence rather than current, which is closer to what Soul is; it also keeps the one-word shape EDDY had. **DUST** (was SMOKE SCREEN) — the card blinds the enemy Frontline *and yourself*, and "dust" explains the self-blind in a way "smoke screen" never did: you kicked it up, you are standing in it.
  - **The real hazard was not the rename, it was what the rename overwrote.** A blind replace across 15 files silently falsified **four dated historical statements** that named the old cards as facts about a past day: `Oracle/baseoracledeck.md`'s *"HEAVE AND HAUL replaced by EDDY (2026-08-01)"* became "replaced by SWAY," which is not what happened on 2026-08-01; the same in `content.py`'s comment; and both Vescal files said SMOKE SCREEN was promoted to core *"same name, rebalanced text"* — a sentence that becomes self-contradicting the moment the core card is renamed. All four restored to the old name with the rename noted alongside. **The general rule this is the second instance of: a find-and-replace over a repo that keeps a threshold log will edit the log.** Records of what was true then are not references to be updated; only live references are.
  - **Verified by resolution, not by grep count.** `build_cards()` confirms all three resolve at the new names with unchanged color/stat/reach/die, that none of the old names still exist, and that the Oracle pool membership survived — then a duel series was run to confirm the engine still executes. A rename that leaves a dangling name in one of the sim's frozenset keyword tables would pass a text search and fail at runtime.

**[2026-08-03] Three red cards re-ranged — Drew set the counts, I picked the cards.** Drew: *"2 red cards needs changed to ranged. and 1 to both."* Red was the most range-locked color at 72% Melee against Blue's 74% Ranged; it now reads Melee 66% / Both 23% / **Ranged 11%**.
  - **The selection rule was fiction that survives the mechanics, and that second half did real work.** **SEISMIC REDIRECT** was the obvious pick and is wrong: its Effect is *"Rushdown,"* and the glossary says Rushdown requires **the user to be in the Frontline**. A Ranged card can only be played while *not* in melee range, so the card would have gated itself out of its own effect. Caught by reading the keyword rather than the card name — the same species as the "Immune to Blind" collision earlier today, where a word that sounds right names something else.
  - **REPEL was rejected for the inverse reason.** *"All enemies must move to backline"* is about opening a gap; as Ranged it could only be played once the gap already existed. The fiction points at range and the mechanic points away from it.
  - **What was chosen, and why each survives both tests.** **STARING CONTEST** — pure initiative manipulation off eye contact, *"Move first and you've already lost."* It needs line of sight, not contact, and Melee actively contradicted it; this is a correction more than a nudge. **RHYTHM BREAK** — its own gate is *"if the defender moved since their last turn,"* which is a read taken across a field; a Backline attacker watching someone reposition is the card's fiction stated exactly. **ROLLOUT** to Both — the whole card is about positional discipline (*"if you did not reposition last turn"*) and it returns to hand; a card whose identity is movement being locked to one position was the odd one out. None of the three carries a keyword with a positional requirement, which is what makes them safe to move.
  - **Left alone deliberately:** BLINDSIDE was a strong second candidate for Ranged (Blind applied before contact reads well) and lost to RHYTHM BREAK only on tightness of fit. Worth knowing if a future pass wants a third.

**[2026-08-03] The Immunity trifecta split by Range too — and the harness that measured it was wrong the first time.** Drew: *"blue ranged, red melee, green both"* → *"make the split."* LAST RESORT to Ranged, UNBROKEN to Melee, UNTOUCHED stays Both.
  - **He asked whether the ranges were already split; they weren't.** All three were Range: Both. The convention he described is real but only for two colors — measured across the core sets, Blue is **74% Ranged** and Red **70% Melee**, while Green is the one color with no range identity at all (39% Melee / 37% Both / 24% Ranged). Nothing in `rules/cards.md` or `rules/combat.md` states a color-range convention; it is emergent in the card sets. So "green both" is new rather than existing practice, which is worth knowing if it ever gets generalised to other trifectas.
  - **Range: Both is not a neutral middle, and I flagged that before he decided.** The Range Matrix makes Both legal in all four position cells, Ranged in three, Melee in one. And `rules/combat.md` gates **defense** on Range as well as attack — *"a Melee card cannot defend unless both combatants are Frontline."* All three cards carry the Immunity grant on their Defensive Bonus, so this is a restriction on the half of the card that matters most. UNBROKEN, which kept the biggest die, is now the one that can go dead when it is most needed.
  - **The harness was confounded and the control did not catch it.** Every test card was swapped in for TWIN STRIKE — the frost deck's *only* Green card. Fine for UNTOUCHED (Green for Green); catastrophic for the other two, which left the deck with **zero Green in an RPS system**. The tell was UNBROKEN reading 34.6% against UNTOUCHED's 42.9% at the same Range with a bigger die, a 15-point gap that made no sense as a card property. The stock-vs-stock control could never have caught this, because the control never swapped anything. **Fixed by swapping each test card for a card of its own color**, with an assertion that the deck's color histogram is unchanged in every configuration.
  - **What that means for cross-card claims: this harness cannot make them, and my earlier chat wording did.** I said the three "span about six points of win rate purely by color." The 6.4 was UNTOUCHED's own before/after delta, not a spread between cards. Each test card displaces a different neighbour, so only within-card deltas are valid — same card, same deck, one attribute moved.
  - **Corrected numbers, color-balance preserved, 20,000 paired seeds per configuration.** LAST RESORT: 56.6% as printed → 54.4% on the new die (−2.2) → **50.6% with Ranged (−3.8)**. UNBROKEN: 46.7% as printed → **44.3% with Melee (−2.4)**. UNTOUCHED: 49.3% → **42.9% on d4 (−6.4)**, Range unchanged.
  - **The totals are the interesting part.** Blue −6.0, Green −6.4, Red −2.4. The two changes together land almost evenly on Blue and Green while Red gets off light: it keeps the biggest die *and* the smallest total cost, paying only with the harshest range restriction on paper. Whether −2.4 understates Melee's real cost is a live question — the sim's reader policy positions in its own way, and a Melee restriction only bites when a table's positioning makes it bite. **Not acted on.** Drew set both attributes deliberately, one after the other, and measuring is not licence to adjust.

**[2026-08-03] The die philosophy beats trifecta parity — the reusable ruling.** Drew: *"give them the traditional 4/6/8 spread."*
  - **The three Immunity cards all printed d8**, and I had flagged that as *probably intentional parity* the turn before: LAST RESORT / UNBROKEN / UNTOUCHED are one card in three colors, built in the 2026-08-01 Oracle keyword-coverage pass to put an Immunity grant in every color, and identical text invites an identical die. Drew's answer says the stat table wins. **That is the general rule, not a one-off:** when a trifecta's uniformity collides with d6-Mind / d8-Body / d4-Soul, the spread is the thing that holds. The next trifecta doesn't need asking.
  - **Only two cards moved.** UNBROKEN was already correct at Body + d8, which is part of why the d8s read as deliberate rather than as an unswept remnant of the 2026-07-22 global bump. LAST RESORT went d8 → d6, UNTOUCHED d8 → d4.
  - **Measured before reporting, and the cost is not small.** Stock frost deck with one card swapped for the test card, run against unmodified frost, 20,000 paired seeds per configuration. UNTOUCHED: **49.3% at d8 → 42.9% at d4, a 6.4-point drop.** LAST RESORT: **56.6% → 54.4%, 2.2 points.** Both track expected damage lost almost linearly (−2 and −1 average), so this is what a smaller die costs rather than an anomaly worth chasing.
