  - **The first LAST RESORT figure was wrong and is corrected above** — reported in chat as 48.6% → 45.8%. See the Range entry below for the harness fault; the delta survived roughly intact (2.8 → 2.2) but the absolute numbers came from a broken deck.
  - **The live consequence, stated because Drew should decide rather than discover it:** three cards with word-for-word identical text no longer play the same. UNTOUCHED holds the same Immunity gate on the smallest die in the game and is a real cost to run; UNBROKEN's die didn't move at all. That is exactly what d4-precision / d8-power is *for*, and it also means the Oracle's "one per color" coverage is no longer one card three times. Not re-tuned — he just set the die, and measuring is not licence to adjust.
  - **`content.py`'s own comment was updated, not just its numbers.** It described UNBROKEN and UNTOUCHED as *"same exact shape as LAST RESORT"* — still true of the effect, no longer true of the die. A later reader finding three divergent dice under a comment claiming sameness would reasonably file it as drift and "fix" it back.

**[2026-08-03] Contradiction hunt — the content layers read against each other for the first time.** Drew: *"let's hunt for more contradictions."* The 2026-08-02 audit covered `world/` and `mythology/` only. This one covered everything else, read by **seam** (a subject held in more than one file) rather than file by file, because that is the method that found the Seat States divergence — duplication is what drifts, so go looking at the duplicates.
  - **The headline finding: one farm has been carrying two names.** Iron's claim is the **Western Property** in five places and the **Well Property** in ten, across seven files — and `quests/shifting-burrow.md` and `factions/the-masons.md` each use *both*, in the same document, without noticing. A GM reading `settling-stones.md` next to `briarwatch.md` would reasonably conclude Briarwatch has two farms with two holes in the ground. Standardised on Western Property (the location file's own header and the owner's own file) and kept "the old well property" as what it turned out to be — a genuine local nickname, since `hollow-below-briarwatch.md` has Iron himself saying *"there's an old well on the property."* Stating that once in `places/briarwatch.md` keeps the name and kills the ambiguity.
  - **The Hollow's opening asserted a cause its own dungeon disproves.** *"Fenwool were lost weeks ago when a section of field gave way"* — flat GM-facing narration. But the Mated Stonecoil Nest holds living Fenwool *pinned in the den*, the Causality Chain says predator range expanded upward, and `bestiary/fenwool.md` says Stonecoils take them *"no drama, no trace, which is exactly why the pattern is so hard to prove."* The reveal was already built; the opening was spoiling it in the wrong direction, by being wrong. Reframed as what Briarwatch believes. Iron's own read-aloud needed no change — he is allowed to be mistaken, and that was always the design.
  - **Two stale pointers, both pointing at last night's own work.** `places/turnroot-weald.md` sent readers to the adventure for a stat block that lives in `bestiary/rootstalker.md`. And `bestiary/root-heart.md` still opened *"Triggers at Pressure 4"* with a fight as its only mode — untrue since Paid Passage shipped hours earlier, and the exact thing the Seeker's Price verification step was supposed to catch. Worth recording that the drift a session creates is the drift the same session is worst at seeing.
  - **A keyword collision, not longhand.** `bestiary/fogcaller.md` read *"Immune to Blind."* **Immunity** is a defined keyword meaning something else entirely — one-shot negation of the next *attack*, expiring on use. Permanent immunity to a debuff token is a different mechanic wearing the same word. Rewritten to "Blind has no effect on it," matching how Grip Walk two lines below already avoids the problem. This is a species the `check_item_keywords` gate does not cover: not an invented keyword, not a longhand one — a real keyword's name used for something it isn't.
  - **The Regency claimed something about seven people that can only be true of five.** *"The animal titles… Actually: the shape the Seat left on them."* Isabella and Percival were recruited after the Pendragon Attempt — the file says so itself, four sections down. Their titles cannot be scar tissue. Fixed by saying so, which improves it: two council members wearing imitations of a mark they don't carry, and nobody has ever mentioned the difference. **Flagged A2** — this deepens two NPCs rather than only correcting an error.
  - **Three quests were unreachable from their own location files**, found by a link-reciprocity pass rather than by reading. `quests/tide-pulls-back.md` is a full adventure set in Glasslight Reach with no inbound link from anywhere but this file. `quests/the-larder-fence.md` is *discussed* in Briarwatch's GM Secret and never pointed at. `verify.py` checked that links resolve and that none are duplicated; it never asked whether the link exists at all.
  - **Two of the classes became checks rather than fixes**, which is the part worth keeping: restated stat blocks must now match their bestiary source, and the distance rule is enforced. Both were proven to fail on a deliberately reintroduced violation first — and the stat-block check caught a bug in *itself* on its first run, reading Cole's stats against his father's CTR because the search window looked backward. A check that reports a false failure on day one would have been retired by week two.
  - **One assumption left standing, not resolved:** that a creature's own body size is a size and not a distance, so `bestiary/tollbird.md`'s "close to a meter tall" and `skeinwing.md`'s altitude survive a strict rule that took out sixteen other measurements. Written into `verify.py` as three hand-listed exemptions rather than a pattern, so the set cannot grow quietly. If Drew reads the rule as reaching those too, it is one edit.
  - **Also noted and deliberately not fixed:** `rules/items.md` describes a Pell lantern as *"immune to the Misdirection Trap"* — the same word collision as the Fogcaller, but in out-of-combat prose about a navigation hazard rather than in a stat block where keyword parsing matters. Left alone on the judgment that the fix would be churn; recorded so the next reader knows it was seen.

**[2026-08-03] Iron ties the fresh bundles — new canon that overturns text written the same day.** Drew: *"Iron ties the new bundles."* (A3.)
  - **What it overturned was mine, and recent.** `quests/the-abandoned-homestead.md` said, in bold: *"**Do not answer the fresh bundles.** Not this session, not with a note found in the house, not with the Duskwick."* I wrote that hours earlier and meant it. Drew's call is legitimate and it is also *better* — the instruction was protecting a mystery, and the answer turns out to be characterisation rather than a solution. Iron is the farmer still holding his claim together; the homestead was written as the picture of what his failure looks like. Him quietly keeping the dead neighbour's fields is the same restraint the file already gives him — *"he has been waiting for help longer than he'd like to admit. He will not say so directly, and he will not ask twice."*
  - **Drew's decision on reach, taken rather than assumed:** he confirms it if asked. Not sealed, not volunteered. That keeps the encounter's argument intact — the *house* still explains nothing, which was always the point; the answer lives in a different field and has to be walked to.
  - **The Duskwick hole had to be closed or a player would find it.** Iron works that yard every autumn and a Duskwick owns that doorway. He has never crossed the threshold, because the house is not his and there is nothing in it he wants — which the encounter already supports outright ("a party that searches the yard… has a completely uneventful visit"). He is now the worked example of the encounter's own safe path. That was the actual design work in this change; the rest was deletion.
  - **The winter thread is a different thread and was deliberately not collapsed into this one.** Old bundles vanish; the First Bundle is larger every spring; nobody has connected them. Iron answers *who ties the new ones* and nothing else. Both `bestiary/briarbundles.md` and `bestiary/the-first-bundle.md` still carry the question, and the homestead's GM Secret now says explicitly that he has never wondered.
  - **One overclaim surfaced and got softened.** `briarbundles.md` said flatly *"The old ones disappear over winter"* — while the homestead has four weathered ones still standing in its fields. That tension predates this change, but Iron's autumn work leans on it, so the folklore now says what it can support: some go, some stand for years, and nobody has written down a pattern. Hedging the folklore rather than inventing a rule about which ones vanish.

**[2026-08-02] Seeker's Price is a working toll wall, not scenery — and I had it running backwards.** Drew, on reading the Tollworn Plate work: *"in my self playthrough I push a coin into the wood to gain guided passage for awhile."*
  - **The file said so and I read past it.** *"These are payments made by travelers for safe passage"* — I took that as backstory explaining why metal was in the trees, and built the Plate around taking things **out**. The wall's actual function is people paying **in**, and the direction I designed for is the transgressive one. The line was doing mechanical work and I read it as flavour.
  - **Drew's playthrough is the source of the mechanic, not an illustration of it.** He pressed in a gold piece, bought guided passage, and that opened the path to the Root Heart and became his session's climax — he saw the boss and a way out opened. Every tier below that is extrapolated from what he actually did.
  - **Two existing rules carry almost all of it, which is why it needed no new subsystem.** Navigation already says *"No check is required when following forest signs"* and that signs *"must be earned, not handed out freely"* — paying the wall is how you earn them. And tonight's currency scale (10 copper = 1 silver = 1 gold) makes copper/silver/gold a meaningful ladder at a wall whose other tolls are pocket change. That scale was written for pricing consumables hours earlier and turned out to be load-bearing here by accident.
  - **Three exits now, and they map onto the world's own logic:** earn it (three Marks), win it (kill the Heart), buy it (Paid Passage). Drew's call that the way out is a real rule rather than his session wrapping up.
  - **Two gaps the verification step caught, both of which would have broken it at a table.** The Root Heart's behaviour toward a paying party was unstated — a GM would reasonably run it as a boss encounter, collapsing the third exit back into the second; it now says outright that the Heart does not attack. And "a gold piece" sits a foot from a 200-gold Plate in the same wall, which reads as an error unless stated: **the wall reads the gesture, not the sum.** Fifty gold buys exactly what one buys.
  - **Paid Passage is cheap and I left it cheap, deliberately.** It returns nothing — no Marks, no kill, no loot, the Heart alive behind them. Cheap because empty is the correct trade; the alternative was bolting on an artificial cost to make the ledger look balanced.
  - The Plate's no-Pressure branch moved off the Mark system and onto *having paid into this wall*. The wall keeps its own books, separate from the forest's general customs — and taking a toll back out is worse than ordinary theft, because it undoes a transaction the forest already closed on someone else's behalf.

**[2026-08-02] The campaign's first real equipment: the Tollworn Plate, Turnroot session 4. Drew: "turnroot session 4 should drop the first piece of armor before the boss fight. 1 piece ahead of time with the party choosing who wears it."**
  - **Placed at Seeker's Price, which already described itself as the answer.** That POI is a wall of copper coins and rusted gear fused into bark — *"payments made by travelers for safe passage"* — with an existing rule that taking anything raises Pressure by 1. The armor did not need inventing a home; it needed noticing that one was already written. Somebody wore this to the edge of the Weald and decided they wanted through more than they wanted armor.
  - **The existing Pressure rule became the cost, and the Mark system became the alternative.** With at least one Mark, the wood lets go and there is no Pressure — the forest settling up with someone who has already paid something. With no Marks it is theft, Pressure +1, one step nearer the *forced* Root Heart. So taking it without standing brings the boss closer, and they walk into that fight wearing a wall's worth of other people's tolls. Both branches use rules that were already there.
  - **Tier 1, plain Armour 1, no split budget.** It is the first equipment anyone in this campaign will own; it should teach the keyword and nothing else.
  - **"One only, party picks" is written as a table beat, not an inventory line** — with the instruction not to let it pass as bookkeeping, and a real out: if the party cannot agree, the plate stays in the wall and the forest is content to keep it.
  - **It deliberately front-runs the gold curve by one session and both files now say so.** Pacing puts a first Tier 1 purchase at session 5, at the capital; this is session 4, found. Stated in `rules/equipment.md` so the two don't read as contradicting, and excluded from Turnroot's own income figure so a GM doesn't budget the session around it.

**[2026-08-02] Gold pacing is per character, not per party — and the rule as first written never said which.** Drew asked directly; the answer is per character, first Tier 1 item at ~5 sessions each.
  - **This was a gap I noticed and then left.** While designing I worked out that 40 gold a session party-wide would take a four-person party twenty sessions to equip, wrote it down in chat as a consideration, and then wrote the rule saying "the party" without resolving it either way. Drew had to ask. The failure wasn't the ambiguity, it was noticing the ambiguity and shipping anyway.
  - **Fixed at the source and downstream.** `rules/equipment.md` now states per-character explicitly, gives the multiply-by-party-size instruction, and says a four-person Tier 1 session is ~160 gold between them. All eight quest reward lines restated as per-character figures so they scale automatically rather than needing a second pass when party size changes.
  - **The number stopped being arbitrary once Drew gave the session spine.** Session 1 ends at the ruins, 2 solves the Hollow, 3–4 are Turnroot, 5 is the capital. Five sessions to a first Tier 1 item means **gear arrives at the moment the party first stands somewhere that sells it**. That's now written into the rule as *why five*, and the spine is recorded in Campaign Status — the pacing was calibrated against a real campaign shape, not picked off a curve.

**[2026-08-02] Card trading at the Underground Bazaar — the last open concern, closed by being answered rather than filled. Drew: "the underground bazaar is where card trading happens. it's rare outside of the bazaar."** The concern had read "the Bazaar has zero mechanical items — a real gap." It was never an items gap. The Bazaar doesn't sell items; it sells cards, and no `items/underground-bazaar-items.md` is coming.
  - **Most of it was already written in two files nobody had connected.** `places/capital/underground-bazaar.md` already said *"Players who spend time in the bazaar may begin acquiring experiences worth crystallizing. Cards in this system represent those experiences"* — the soul economy was always described as trading crystallized experience. And `world/lineage.md` already settled provenance by saying the tag *"remembers the tradition."* Drew's sentence completed a thought the repo had been holding in two halves.
  - **I got a rule wrong mid-design and it was load-bearing.** I argued trading had to be one-in-one-out because "deck size is locked to total stats." That rule governs **creatures**. `rules/cards.md` distinguishes them in adjacent lines — player decks use the stat-match as "a solid *default*," enemy decks "*equal* the creature's total stats" — and Frost and Steele both went 9 → 10 cards after Session 1. Player decks grow, uncapped. The constraint I'd derived didn't exist, and the whole trade-vs-purchase framing rested on it.
  - **That error is what surfaced the real question.** Frost and Steele are "all core cards," so a starting party holds *nothing but* tagless ones. Under my first suggestion (tagged-only trading), the Bazaar would have been shut to any party that hadn't already collected tagged cards — at a location under the capital, not at the end of a campaign. Drew's uncertainty about tagless cards was pointing straight at that.
  - **Resolved as an asymmetry: sold but not bought** (Drew's call). The bazaar takes anything, because the soul economy buys experiences and a common one is still yours — that is the refugee end the location already describes, the same queue the tattooist's needle serves. But stock is scarce and almost entirely tagged, because nobody shelves what everyone knows. A first-time party can participate immediately, by selling.
  - **The brake was already in the system and needed no rule.** Player decks are uncapped, so a bought card is an *addition* — an eleventh card dilutes every draw. Buying is therefore not strictly an upgrade, which is what stops this becoming a deck-optimisation shop without anyone legislating.
  - **The best thing in it is a consequence, not a mechanic:** a bought card's tag doesn't change hands, so somebody can walk out holding a WEALD card having never stood in the Weald. A lineage they carry and cannot personally back — which is precisely what the soul economy already is, knowledge separated from the people who earned it. `world/lineage.md`'s closing line, untouched and now much worse, says *"every card a player holds is a small act of remembering."*
  - **The Bazaar is complementary to the Oracle, not a shortcut past it** — the Oracle gives *your* growth, earned by answering something true; the Bazaar gives someone else's, sideways, for a piece of yourself. *Was flagged as inference and reversible; **ratified by Drew 2026-08-03** ("your 3 fixes are good with me"). Settled — the two sources are not rivals and a party using both is not double-dipping.*
  - **`unresolved-concerns.md` is now empty**, and says so explicitly rather than sitting as bare headers, so a future reader can tell the difference between no debt and no bookkeeping.

**[2026-08-02] Reward lines applied — and five of the fourteen quests correctly pay nothing.** Drew: "apply reward lines." The queued framing was "twelve quests with no reward line," which assumed twelve rewards. Reading them first said otherwise.
  - **Every quest now states what it pays, including the ones that pay nothing.** That is the actual deliverable: a GM opening any quest file gets an answer either way, instead of finding silence and having to guess whether it was a decision or an omission. Five say nothing, and each says *why*.
  - **`sour-tomatoes.md` — "Nothing. Do not put a number here."** The encounter's only real question is what happens to the children who are still alive. A payment answers a different and worse question. The clearest case where applying the rule mechanically would have damaged the thing it was applied to.
  - **`the-wallows-descent.md` — nobody hires anyone to go into the Wallows.** Every objective in its Opening Conditions is the party's own: a debt with no room left, someone on a dead-end branch, the Warden, a name heard in the echoes. No client, so no fee — and paying would imply somebody sanctioned the descent, which nobody did. Salvage is separately fair game and says so.
  - **`settling-stones.md` and `shifting-burrow.md` pay nothing because they are two doors into the same descent.** Iron pays for the Hollow; running both does not pay twice. Worth stating outright, since a GM running them as standalone encounters would reasonably assume each earned its own fee.
  - **The Ashfall arc had an employer hiding in one sentence.** `war-engine.md`'s Core Concept opens *"They were sent to disable a war machine."* So both Ashfall sessions pay Tier 2 — and war-engine's reward note carries the complication the fiction creates: the contract was to break an engine, there is no engine, and the party has to report that to whoever holds the purse. The version to avoid is the employer simply believing them and paying.
  - **`abyssal-ruins-cathedral.md` pays in lair, not commission**, with an explicit line that the stabilising crystal is not loot and must not be priced — it holds the passage the party crossed to reach it.
  - **`turnroot-weald-adventure.md` pays by the session rather than in a lump**, being a framework and not a night's work — and most of it should arrive as *harvest*. It is the most thoroughly priced region in the world and Senshi buys outright, so a party that treats the forest as a place with things in it out-earns one waiting to be paid. Left for the table to discover rather than be told.
  - Amounts throughout are the pacing rule applied, not invented: ~20g for a single encounter at Tier 1, ~40g for a session, ~160g at Tier 2.
  - **Process note:** the script that wrote this entry died on a bad substring match *after* the quest files were already written, so the first commit shipped the content with neither the log nor the concern closed. Caught on the next check rather than at the time — worth remembering that a multi-step edit script failing halfway leaves a half-finished state that still passes `verify.py`, because nothing it checks knows what was supposed to happen.

**[2026-08-02] Prices made coherent — Drew: "all prices were mostly ceremonial... you have full authority to make the prices coherent."** Followed the pacing work, and confirmed the Senshi 35 silver → 35 gold slip as a slip.
  - **The bigger finding was absence, not error: four of the six `items/` files had no prices at all.** `briarwatch-items.md`, `consumables.md`, `vultures-nest-items.md` and most of `hollow-and-weald-items.md` listed effects and sources with no number anywhere. Only `turnroot-weald-items.md` was fully priced. So "make the prices coherent" was mostly "make the prices exist."
  - **The scale was already in the world and nobody had written it down.** Luminova Leaves heal 4 for ~15 gold; Luminova Powder heals 8 for ~30 — and the file already states the crafting relationship between them. That is a clean **~15 gold per single-use point**, using the same 1-point vocabulary `rules/equipment.md` already defines for tiers. Derived from shipped content rather than invented, then written into the pricing file with the anchor named so the next person can check it.
  - **Four real incoherences, each with a reason recorded in the diff:**
    - **Sap Vial 60 → 25.** It was strictly dominated by Echothorn Seed at 40 — Echothorn gives +2 to a damage *or* healing roll, costs no action, and has no drawback; Sap Vial gave less, cost an action, and Rooted you afterward, for half again the price. Same failure shape as OVERCOMMIT earlier tonight.
    - **Rootstalker Core 250 → 60.** A single-use item priced above a Tier 1 *permanent* (200). Rarity justifies a premium, not outranking gear that works every fight for the rest of the campaign.
    - **Vision Shard 100 → 45** (Scry 3 ≈ 3 points) and **Rusted Armor 80 → 20** (5 temp HP, once ≈ 1.25 points).
    - **Root Fibers** kept at 15/strand, but its "+1 to ranged attack rolls if used in crafting" is a *permanent* bonus — Tier 1 territory. Clarified in text rather than repriced: the strand is the material, the finished bow is a Tier 1 weapon and prices as one.
  - **17 previously unpriced items given numbers**, all derived from the same scale: 8–15g for one point (Salted Strip's heal 2 at 8, Barbed Wrap's Thorns 1 at 15), 25–30g for two (Echo Shell, Imprint Sigil, Phase Draught), 45g for the Split Wedge's repeating in-combat damage.
  - **`items/fog-basin-items.md` deliberately left with no gold prices.** Pell trades in a memory, a secret, or a name. An item with no gold price is not an oversight when its seller does not take gold — that is now stated in the pricing rule so the next pass doesn't "fix" it, and it is the same premise the Underground Bazaar is waiting to be built on.
  - Full curve after the pass: 8–15g single-point, 20–30g two-point, 35–50g premium and sale rates, 60g rare craftable, 150g for the one permanent passive — which sits correctly below Tier 1's 200. Item card sheets regenerated.

**[2026-08-02] Gold pacing built — and the concern was asking for the wrong thing.** Open since 2026-07-24, phrased as wanting "a reference frame for how fast a party should accumulate gear and gold, likely a gold-per-session metric." Reading the repo first showed a number was never the missing piece.
  - **The demand side was complete; the supply side barely existed.** Equipment tiers (~200/~800/~2,000), ~15 priced consumables, a consistent `*Price: ~N gold*` convention — against **one** gold loot entry in the whole bestiary, **zero** quests paying anything, and characters starting with no money by explicit rule. The Session 1 route yielded `d6+2 silver` from a chest, in a denomination with no defined value. A gold-per-session target would have been a number with no mechanism behind it.
  - **The rule Drew picked, generalised: a session's work is worth about one fifth of the tier the party is shopping in.** He answered "~5 sessions to a first Tier 1 item"; expressing it as a ratio rather than a flat 40 gold makes it hold at every tier, scale with the party without a table to maintain, and need a GM to know exactly one thing. Cross-checked against shipped content before writing: the Bone Collector's ~70g hoard ≈ 1.75 early sessions, Senshi's rates ≈ 1 session, cheapest consumable ≈ a third of one.
  - **I got the silver question wrong and had to re-ask.** I framed it as "appears once as undefined loot, recommend cutting it," and Drew answered accordingly. It appears across a full Gilded Tusk menu — six dishes at 6–15 silver against an 8-gold delicacy — so cutting it would have broken real content or priced dumplings at 0.7 gold. Re-asked with the actual data; answer became "define at 10:1." **The lesson is not about silver.** I offered a recommendation built on a partial grep and he reasonably took it; the check that caught it was the one the plan itself scheduled, after the decision was already made.
  - **The same sweep then found a third denomination.** `places/capital/gilded-tusk.md` prices a seasoning at **2 copper** — undefined the same way silver had been. Rule extended to 10 copper = 1 silver = 1 gold, and the whole menu validates it: seasoning 0.02g, mains 0.6–0.9g, the limited dish 1.5g, the dangerous-creature delicacy 8g. The rate was confirmed by content that already existed rather than asserted.
  - **Creature loot stays excluded** (Drew's call). The Bone Collector's existing `2d6 × 10` is kept and reframed in the rule as a *hoard* — a curator with a lair, which is a place with money in it — explicitly not a precedent for gold on stat blocks.
  - **The number is a budget, not a purse.** Written into the rule because the alternative degrades fast: Iron is a farmer whose ground is collapsing, and `hollow-below-briarwatch.md` now pays him out three ways (Fenwool the party has to move, the neighbours pooling, or a standing debt) with the GM picking what the table earned.
  - **Flagged, not fixed:** Senshi offers 35 *silver* for a Bone Collector specimen while his published rates are 35–40 *gold* for far safer creatures. Same buyer, same numeral, 10× denomination gap, hardest job paying least. Either a unit slip or deliberate lowballing worth saying out loud — a price is canon and not mine to change on inference.

**[2026-08-02] SEED measured — no change needed, and its "gate" turns out not to be one.** Drew asked for the third card from the old concern after OVERCOMMIT and RETALIATE. Same harness: decks identical but for the one card, sides swapped.
  - **Win rate is fine.** SEED reads **50.8% vs STEADFAST, 51.2% vs SHADE AWAY, 52.9% vs WITNESS** in duels, and **52.0%** in 3v3 with sides swapped. Parity to slightly ahead against three green Soul peers. Nothing here is underpowered.
  - **The finding is that the condition almost never fails.** SEED's payoff is gated on beginning a later turn at the position where the seed was planted. Instrumented: of turns that started with a live seed, it was at anchor **100% in duels** and **99% in 3v3** — only 1% were broken by having been moved. Roughly **70% of plants cash overall**, and the 30% that don't are fights that simply ended first, not conditions that failed.
  - **So the "self-deferred Deadly" framing overstates the cost.** SEED is not Deadly ×2 at risk; it is Deadly ×2 delayed by one turn. That is a real cost — a turn of tempo, and the fight ending first — but it is not the gamble the concern implied, and no compensation is owed for a gate that fires essentially every time it gets the chance.
  - **I did not trust the duel number.** 100% is exactly the shape of a duel artifact, since position changes cost an action and nothing in a 1v1 pressures movement — and the repo already treats duels as degenerate for position-shaped mechanics (Active Reasoning, the Wheel entry). Re-ran in 3v3 where forced repositioning actually exists. It moved from 100% to 99%, which is the honest answer rather than the convenient one.
  - **Also caught: I nearly reported the team result backwards.** `Battle.run()` returns `0`/`1` for team A/B, not a label, and my first team run had no side swap at all. Checked `_finish()` for the convention and re-ran swapped before quoting anything — same class of mistake as misreading `one_duel`'s return earlier tonight.
  - **Verdict: leave SEED alone.** The old concern is now fully closed across all three cards it named — RETALIATE was already fixed, OVERCOMMIT was strictly dominated and is repaired, SEED never needed anything.

**[2026-08-02] RETALIATE/OVERCOMMIT balance resolved — and the concern was describing the wrong problem.** Drew asked for the tuning pass that had been queued since 2026-07-23. The queued framing was *"RETALIATE/OVERCOMMIT/SEED's self-deferred Deadly,"* with dice/range as the first lever and an extra Deadly stack as the named fallback. Measuring found that framing wrong on both cards it named.
  - **RETALIATE needed nothing. The work was already done and nobody closed the ticket.** Its die was raised d6 → d8 on 2026-07-29, and `keyword_lab.py` now reads it at **~51%** against a flat Evade reference with its gate firing on **42%** of plays. The named fallback was measured too: a **third** Deadly stack drops it to ~50.2%, slightly *worse*, because fights average 4.3 turns and each stack is spent on one future damage roll — a stack that never gets cashed is a stack wasted. Applying the queued fallback would have been a downgrade.
  - **OVERCOMMIT's problem was never its Deadly, because it has none.** As printed: `Body + d10. Gain Vulnerable.` No Effect, no Defensive Bonus. **STRIKE is the same colour, stat, die and Range, with a real Defensive Bonus and no self-debuff.** OVERCOMMIT was therefore *strictly dominated* — worse on both axes, with no board state preferring it. That is a dominance argument, not a tuning question, and no simulation was needed to establish it; the sim only measured the cost: **44.1% vs STRIKE's 55.9%** in decks identical but for the one card.
  - **Fixed with `+1d6` on the Attack line** (`Body + d10 + 1d6. Gain Vulnerable.`), which measures **50.6%**. `+2d6` overshoots to 57.5%. The Vulnerable and the absent Defensive Bonus both stay — the card's identity is offence bought with exposure, and it now actually buys some. Idiom follows RHYTHM BREAK, which already puts `+1d6` on an Attack line.
  - **A harness error caught before it reached the report.** My first variant test replaced the card's `damage` function wholesale and silently dropped the self-Vulnerable, which made "as printed" read 49.9% instead of 44.1%. The disagreement with the earlier measurement is what exposed it. Rebuilt with the debuff preserved and a control run that had to reproduce 44.1/55.9 exactly before any variant number was believed.
  - **SEED left alone.** The concern named it too, but its Deadly ×2 is gated on returning to a planted position — a different mechanism from RETALIATE's, untested here, and nothing about it was measured. It is not covered by this pass and should not be assumed fixed.
  - The concern's stated trail (*"memory.md, the 2026-07-23 11:15 PM entry and the 2026-07-24 correction"*) no longer exists — both entries were pruned in an earlier audit. Worth noting as a hazard: `unresolved-concerns.md` is an index pointing into a file that gets deliberately trimmed.

**[2026-08-02] Aege is fated to the Seat of Death. Drew: "Aege can be deaths archon. someone has to do it."** Answers the vacancy I had flagged an hour earlier when the Stage III / Archonhood equivalence landed — a Stage II Echo of Death sitting one rung below an unfilled Seat.
  - **Written as a fate, not a promotion, and that was a judgment call worth stating.** "Can be" is permissive, and reading it as *"she is Stage III now"* would have required contradicting her own file, which says outright *"Not the Archon... Aege isn't it,"* and would have collided with what Archonhood costs: `mythology/archons.md` says internal contradiction ends and **the individual becomes function**. Aege's whole usable surface is the opposite of that — a woman with a family, a worry about the Weald, and a reason to keep walking strangers to places. Making her Death-as-function today would delete the Session 1 guide to fill a roster slot.
  - **The fated form gives Drew everything he asked for and costs nothing.** The Seat has an answer. Aege is unchanged at the table. And it uses a pattern already in canon rather than inventing one — `characters/greed.md` does exactly this with Storm: *"a fate, not a scene,"* how and when deliberately unestablished. Two Seats now sit unheld-with-a-named-successor, which reads as a deliberate shape rather than two loose ends.
  - **It also closes the question I raised during consolidation** — whether the Seat of Death is genuinely Empty or merely unknown. It is unheld, with a fated bearer. Same as Storm. That concern is off `unresolved-concerns.md`.
  - **What her file gained is a cost, stated plainly.** The Echo section now says what Stage III would take from her, and names the family and the worry as *what Stage II still permits*. Nothing about her present behaviour is to be played as a countdown, and she does not know.
  - **Flagged in chat as reversible if Drew meant the present tense, rather than assumed silently.** *Ratified as written 2026-08-03 — the fated form stands, Aege is unchanged at the table.*

**[2026-08-02] Cosmology consolidated — one home per fact. Drew: "let's consolidate them."** *Was:* `world/seats-archons-echoes.md` restated the Seats, the Echo stages, and the Seat roster in full, alongside `mythology/seats.md` and `mythology/echoes.md`. *Now:* `mythology/` owns all three systems; the world file is a 33-line index (down from 86) that defines nothing.
  - **The duplication had already produced three real divergences, and consolidating is what surfaced two of them.** The Seat States table had lost the **Fading** state in the world file (patched earlier the same night). Beyond that: **the Seat roster was doubled** — thirteen Seats in `mythology/seats.md`, the same thirteen as "Known Archons" in the world file — and the two disagreed about **Storm** and about **Death**.
  - **Storm turned out not to be a canon conflict but a notation one, which is why it survived so long.** `mythology/seats.md` listed *"Bearer: Greed"* in the same present-tense slot used for Aurora and Silas. But `characters/greed.md` says he *becomes* bearer and calls it *"a fate, not a scene,"* with how and when deliberately unestablished — and both the world file and `items/lightning-loop.md` describe the Seat as unheld. Three files against one, and the one wasn't wrong so much as mis-formatted. The roster now reads "None seated. **Greed is fated to it**," which makes all four agree without changing anything anyone decided.
  - **Death harmonised to "no bearer is known."** `mythology/seats.md` said the Seat is *"unbearered"*; the world file said the bearer is *"not publicly known."* Those are different claims — an **Empty** Seat means that part of reality begins breaking down, and nothing in canon suggests endings have stopped concluding. Harmonised to the wording both files support, and deliberately left undecided whether the Seat is genuinely empty. *Was flagged for Drew; **ratified 2026-08-03** ("your 3 fixes are good with me"), and Aege's fating the same night gave it the rest of its answer — unheld with a named successor, the same shape as Storm. Not Empty. Settled.*
  - **New file: `mythology/archons.md`.** Archons were the one system with no mythology home — they existed only inside the world file, which is why that file couldn't be reduced without somewhere for them to go. It also absorbs the Stage III / Archonhood equivalence settled earlier tonight.
  - **Unique content was merged, not discarded.** The world file's roster was thinner than `seats.md`'s but had five details that existed nowhere else: Silas tinting all memory with *longing*, Elias being *"wrong about what he is,"* Mira's attention to *"what gets lost in the keeping,"* the Seat of War having no stable form, and the Night Ferryman's location links. All folded in. Aege's Known Echo entry moved to `mythology/echoes.md`, which had a Failed Seat-Takers section and no roster to put anyone in.
  - **Verified by sentence-level diff against the pre-consolidation file**, not by eye — every sentence of the old file checked for a surviving home, with rewording allowed. Four flagged, all four false positives on capitalisation or paraphrase. Also caught myself mid-audit assuming content was lost because `echoes.md` formats the Degrees of Alignment as bullets where the world file used prose; nothing was missing.
  - Eight inbound references repointed, including `items/lightning-loop.md`'s open question about Storm, which the roster fix partially answers.

**[2026-08-02] Cuts over Seats — the cosmological hierarchy is settled. Drew: "both. if the cuts weaken the seats must also be weakened by extension. the seats only exist because of the cuts."** Both flagged A3 questions answered at once.
  - **The two "rival" statements were never rivals.** `mythology/creation-myth-the-three-cuts.md` and `rules/the-summons.md`: *"Where any Cut weakened, the Unheld pressed close."* `world/eclipseria-overview.md`: *"Where the Seats weaken, the Unheld presses in."* Drew's ruling makes the second a downstream case of the first. Seats are fixed positions *inside* the structure the Cuts made — no Cuts, no structure, nothing to sit in. Written into `mythology/seats.md` as a new **Seats and the Cuts** section, and the overview line reframed rather than replaced.
  - **I wrote the asymmetry in explicitly, because it does not run both ways.** A Seat standing Empty breaks its own domain without thinning a Cut; damage travels *down* from the Cuts, not up. Drew's sentence only licensed one direction, and the symmetric reading is the kind of thing a later reader would assume for free if nobody said otherwise.
  - **`mythology/seats.md` already said Seats are "as permanent as the Three Cuts,"** which sat right next to the new fact without stating it. Equal permanence is not the same claim as dependency, and the file had the first without the second.
  - **Q2 resolved as "both files are right."** *(That was my reading of Drew's "both" and was flagged as reversible; **ratified 2026-08-03** — "your 3 fixes are good with me." Settled.)* `mythology/echoes.md` heads Stage III *"Incarnation (Archon)"*; `world/seats-archons-echoes.md` said *"This is not godhood."* Neither contradicts the other once you notice the world file's own Archon entry says Archons are *"not chosen... not elevated"* — an Archon was never a god. The endpoint is now named in both files: the highest stable form of Echo and the fullest biological expression of a domain are one thing under two names. **Live consequence worth knowing:** this puts Aege, a Stage II Echo of Death, one stage from Archonhood of a Seat whose bearer canon says "remains unknown."
  - Still open and still structural: the Echo stages and Seat States are each written longhand in two files. Consolidation is a layout call, not a canon one, and it is Drew's.

**[2026-08-02] World-layer audit — the cosmology read against itself for the first time.** Prompted by noticing that `verify.py` covers cards, decks, stat blocks, references, the simulator, items and the glossary, and has no idea whether the *world* contradicts itself — which is precisely the layer that changed most tonight (In-Between, the Scar, Pendragon). Four findings, two of them mine.
  - **The Scar was described as outside a taxonomy that defines him exactly.** I wrote that `world/seats-archons-echoes.md` was "the taxonomy the Scar is conspicuously not in." Its Failed Seat-Takers entry reads: *"beings who attempted to claim a Seat and survived the contact without succeeding. The Seat leaves its mark permanently."* That is Pendragon in one sentence, and `the-regency.md` already classes his five companions the same way. The line was a flourish I never checked against the file it named. Corrected — and the correction is better than the error was, because the category's own phrase is *the Seat leaves its mark permanently* and the Scar is nothing but marks.
    - What replaced it is the actually interesting version: **his stage is unclear, and the scale may not reach him.** All three Degrees of Alignment describe what happens to an identity — Stage III is "identity and domain become inseparable." He has no identity for a domain to become inseparable from. Written up as off-the-end-of-the-scale rather than outside-the-system, with an instruction not to resolve it to tidy the taxonomy.
  - **The Seat States table exists in two files and they had already diverged.** `mythology/seats.md` lists four states including **Fading**; `world/seats-archons-echoes.md` listed three and omitted it — while its own Archon section describes an Archon fading "until the Seat stands empty" and cites `mythology/seats.md, Seat States` for it. A file contradicting the table it points at. Patched, and `mythology/seats.md` marked canonical so future edits have one home.
  - **Two flagged for Drew, both A3, both since answered:** whether the Unheld presses in where *Cuts* weaken (the myth, the summons) or where *Seats* weaken (`eclipseria-overview.md`) — which matters because the In-Between is built entirely on the Cuts version; and whether Stage III Incarnation *is* Archonhood, which `mythology/echoes.md` asserts in a parenthesis and the world file never says. *The first was answered the same night — Cuts over Seats, below. The second was ratified 2026-08-03. Neither is open debt; the line is kept because the flagging is what the audit was for.*
  - **The structural finding under all of it:** the Echo stages and the Seat States are each written out longhand in two files. That is the layering bug `CLAUDE.md` warns about, and it produced a real contradiction on its own with nobody touching either file recently. Patching the drift does not fix the duplication; that is a consolidation call and it is Drew's.

**[2026-08-02] Pendragon is the Scar. Drew's call (A3), and it closes the oldest open thread in the repo.** *Was:* `world/the-regency.md` said "The council believes he died in the attempt. He did not. What he became is unresolved," and named the Gluttony Abomination as a possible answer, unconfirmed. *Now:* he is the pale figure in the In-Between.
  - **I raised the connection and deliberately did not make it**, then asked how far to take it rather than assuming — because "make the connection" spans from a hook to a resolution, and those are different files. Worth recording that the ask was the right call for a second reason I hadn't weighted: confirming it **closes the Gluttony Abomination** as a Pendragon candidate, a consequence Drew may not have had in view when he said yes. He took the full resolution knowingly.
  - **The supporting vocabulary was already in canon, which is why this fits rather than gets bolted on.** `the-regency.md` already described the survivors' animal titles as *"scar tissue, not heraldry"* and said the attempt *"left marks on everyone in the room."* The five lost internal contradiction and became archetypes; Pendragon went the whole distance — nuance didn't burn away from him, **he** did, and the marks are what stayed. Nobody wrote that to set this up. It was just sitting there.
  - **It also answers a question nobody had asked: why he's in the In-Between.** A thing with no interior cannot be held anywhere that requires holding, so he is in the only ground nobody pays for. That falls straight out of the haven rule written an hour earlier.
  - **Guarded against flattening the entity.** The Scar's whole design rested on unknowability, and a name can spend that. So: identity is GM-only and changes nothing a player sees; **what it wants, whether anything of him remains, and why it attends this party stay unestablished**, with the file saying outright that knowing the name is not knowing the thing. The Love/Binding resonance — he failed at the Seat of Binding, and what he does now is attend people and never approach — is noted as a resonance and explicitly not resolved.
  - Propagated to `world/the-regency.md`, `world/the-scar.md`, both Active Pending Threads entries, and a stale "don't touch it" instruction in `playtesting/first-impressions-sonnet-4-6.md` — annotated with a date rather than rewritten, since the observation was accurate when written and only the instruction expired.

**[2026-08-02] The Scar — a persistent presence in the In-Between. Drew's call (A3).** Pale human figure, every inch scar tissue, no eyelids or lips or ears or nose, every aperture open onto nothing. Always substantially larger than whoever is looking at it. Encountered early, follows the party most of the campaign, and almost every encounter is a moment of *being attended to* rather than a meeting.
  - **Filed in `world/`, not `bestiary/`.** It has no stat block, is never fought, and does not respond to being attacked. Putting it beside `the-unheld.md` and `the-in-between.md` says what it is; putting it in the bestiary would have implied a fight the fiction doesn't contain. The no-stat-block line is written into the file explicitly, same as Weck and Iron, so nobody "completes" it later.
  - **Written as a presence system, not a creature.** The deliverable a GM actually needs is cadence, so the file is built around a five-rung ladder — peripheral, sustained, detailed, scaled, returned — with the instruction that months of nothing sit between rungs and that no rung may be skipped to make a session land harder. Plus one hard rule: **one observer at a time**, never confirmed to the group in the GM's own voice, which turns the party's own disagreement into the horror rather than the description doing it.
  - **The strongest thing available was left as a reading rather than made canon.** The myth says the Cuts cannot heal because *"healing would close them, and a closed wound holds nothing open."* A scar is a closed wound; a body of nothing but scar is a body where the closing finished; and "holds nothing open" arrives at the hollow center from the other side. That derivation is genuinely tight — which is exactly why writing it in as fact would have spent the entity. It's in the file under **What This File Does Not Establish**, marked as one reading, with a note that a GM who resolves it at the table has spent something unrecoverable.
  - Also left standing without connecting: `world/the-unheld.md`'s ghost stories say crews that sailed past the coast came home with *unfamiliar scars*. The resemblance is noted and explicitly not resolved.
  - **Deliberately not linked to Pendragon** (`memory.md`, Active Pending Threads — "didn't die, became something"). That inference is available, it is the obvious next sentence, and it is exactly the shape the Translation Principle's redefinition test exists to stop. Flagged here so Drew can make the connection if he wants one; not made on his behalf. ***Superseded within the hour — Drew made it. See the Pendragon-is-the-Scar entry above. Kept as written because the withholding was the correct call at the time and the record of it is worth more than a tidy file.***

**[2026-08-02] The In-Between — the overworld is canon, and it is a Cuts phenomenon rather than a new one. Drew's call (A3, cosmology).** *Was:* the space between locations had no name and no properties; `world/geography-overview.md` said only that hubs are two days apart and the Regency maintains the roads between them. *Now:* everything that isn't a named place is the In-Between, where the Three Cuts hold least.
  - **Derived rather than asserted, which is the part that matters.** Drew gave three properties — no consistent landscape, no predictable encounters, no safe havens. Those aren't three rules; they are one condition wearing three faces, and each maps onto a specific Cut: landscape → thin **Distance** (boundary failing, so terrain bleeds instead of transitioning), encounters → thin **Name** (nothing legible before it arrives), havens → thin **Price** (nothing stays anchored). Written that way a GM can extrapolate a fourth property correctly without asking. The mapping is the myth's own if-then applied to Drew's premise, not an invention: *"Where any Cut weakened, the Unheld pressed close."*
  - **The contradiction I raised, and how Drew settled it.** `world/the-unheld.md` states the coastline is a threshold "hard, exact, without gradient." An In-Between defined as gradient-thinness would have made that false. **Drew: coastline stays hard.** Resolved by direction rather than amendment — the Unheld is *outside pressing in* and stops at the land; the In-Between is the world *failing to hold itself from the inside*. Same symptoms, opposite cause, and the coastline paragraph is untouched. A pointer was added above it so the next reader doesn't have to rediscover the distinction.
  - **The haven rule turned out to be half-written in canon already.** `geography-overview.md`'s "the Regency maintains the roads and halfway-inns" is the rule stated as an operational fact. Generalised: *a safe place in the In-Between is a place someone is paying to hold* — the Roadhouse's crown seal, the Masons' fresh glyphs, the farmers' new bundles every autumn. This makes the built-things-holding-a-line motif that was already all over Briarwatch into the setting's general law rather than a regional quirk. Where payment stops, the ground goes back; **how fast is deliberately unestablished**, which is what lets `quests/the-abandoned-homestead.md` sit unexplained.
  - **Encounters are displaced, not random** (Drew confirmed). I flagged that "no predictable encounters" is easy to state and unrunnable — the bestiary is organised by location, so a GM has nothing to reach for. The instruction is now: pull any creature, put it where it makes no sense, don't account for it. **Stat blocks do not change.** What's lost is the knowledge a person actually predicts with — where a thing lives, what it wants at this hour — and that knowledge is about places, and there is no place.

**[2026-08-02] I reported three items as missing. They already existed — my grep was case-sensitive.** During the Release pass I searched `items/` for "Barbed Wrap", got nothing, and logged all three as content referenced across six files with no mechanics behind them. `items/briarwatch-items.md` has held complete entries for all three the whole time; the file writes them as **BARBED WRAP**. Drew acted on the bad report and asked me to build them. Correcting it plainly rather than quietly: the concern was removed from `unresolved-concerns.md`, and the lesson is narrow and repeatable — **card and item names are stored in caps, so any name search across this repo has to be case-insensitive.** The same mistake would have hidden any item, any card, any creature.
  - Related: `memory.md` had been pointing at a phantom `items/briarwoods-items.md` while every live file correctly referenced `items/briarwatch-items.md`. The phantom path was the *only* evidence I had that the file didn't exist, and it was itself a stale artifact of the Briarwoods → Briarwatch rename. Two stale things agreeing with each other read as confirmation.
  - **There was real work underneath, which is the only reason this ends well.** All three items predate the current keyword vocabulary and two were genuinely broken:
    - **SPLIT WEDGE read "Gain Anchored — +2 damage this turn."** Anchored is defined as a benefit that *persists while you hold position and re-triggers at the start of each of your turns*; a one-turn bonus cannot be Anchored. The fiction — an iron wedge driven into the ground — wants the persistent reading, so it now grants +2 damage each turn and ends the moment you move. Power bought with immobility.
    - **BARBED WRAP wrote Thorns out by hand,** and wider than the keyword: "when you are targeted" rather than on a successful melee hit. Same species as the Bone Collector's "Stitched Armor: takes −2 damage" being Armour longhand. Now Thorns 1. This is a real nerf — melee-only, on-hit-only — so it was flagged for Drew, who **ratified it** ("keep barbed wrap as thorns, that's right"). Wire-wrapped leather strips are a contact item; Thorns is what they are.
    - CARRION FEATHER was already correct; only its wording moved, onto GROUNDING STANCE's established phrasing for ignoring forced movement rather than a second phrasing for the same thing.
  - `verify.py` gained an **item keyword check** — every keyword an item names must exist in the glossary, no keyword may be restated longhand, and Anchored may not be attached to a one-turn effect. Validated the way a new test should be: run against the pre-fix file, where it catches both bugs, then against the fixed file, where it passes. A check that only ever passes has not been tested.

**[2026-08-02] Release pass — what the first real full verification actually found.** Six checks now green; the ledger is empty; 167 sim cards reconcile against canon.
  - **Two cards printed a Range that does not exist.** WATCHES FEET (`cards/aege.md`) and LIMB-SNAPPER (`cards/rootstalker-weald.md`) both read `Range: Melee, hits Backline`. `rules/combat.md` defines Range as a closed set — Melee / Ranged / Both — with an explicit attacker×target matrix in which Melee against a Backline target is ✗. So as printed, both cards' Effects were unreachable: each one only triggers *against a Backline target*, which their own Range forbade. Corrected to **Both**, the only value in the vocabulary that makes their printed Effect fire. Flagging the judgment: Both is very slightly wider than the longhand appears to intend (it also legalises a Backline attacker), and reverting is one edit if Drew reads it differently.
  - The sim had silently modelled WATCHES FEET as plain `melee`, dropping the "hits Backline" clause entirely — so canon, the card, and the engine disagreed three ways. Now reconciled. Measured rather than assumed: Aege's win rate against Frost moved 86.7% → 85.7% over 4,000 duels. Frost/Steele untouched.
  - **memory.md audit found four false claims about live canon**, all of the same species — statements that were true when written and never revisited. Two phantom files (`places/briarwoods.md`, `items/briarwoods-items.md`) that have never existed under those names; two stale HP figures predating the HP formula change (Briar Scratcher 9→11, Delve Roller 12→13, both now formula-correct); a Delve Roller passive still described as "−1 all incoming damage" rather than Armour 1; and a claim that the Elder Tower Creature's stat block was complete, when its own file opens *"Not a combat encounter. An environmental presence."* — it should never have one.
  - **A near-miss worth recording.** Force-rebuilding all six PDFs reported every one as changed, which I nearly logged as six stale artifacts. Chrome's PDF output is not byte-deterministic — two rebuilds from identical HTML produce different hashes at identical byte length. Nothing was stale. It also vindicates `generate-all.sh`'s design of diffing the *HTML* and rebuilding PDFs only downstream of a real change: PDFs cannot be compared by hash at all.

**[2026-08-02] Armour and Thorns stack additively. Drew: "armour and thorns stack, update the glossary."** *Was:* undefined — the four charge-based keywords (Resist, Vulnerable, Deadly, Weak) all state their stacking behaviour outright, and these two said nothing, which is what forced three Briarbundle cards to be designed around the question earlier the same night.
  - **They could not stack the way the others do, and the entries had to say so.** Resist and Vulnerable stack as *charges* — each spent on one attack. Armour and Thorns are explicitly never consumed and never expire, so charge-stacking is incoherent for them. They stack **additively into a single value**: Armour 2 and Armour 1 held together are Armour 3 against every attack for the rest of the fight. Both entries now name the contrast explicitly, because a reader who pattern-matches to Resist's wording gets it wrong.
  - **This surfaced a live correctness bug: Armour was not in the simulator at all.** Not partially, not inert — absent. It is a documented step in the fixed damage pipeline (`rules/combat.md`: redirect → Protect → **Armour** → Resist/Vulnerable → HP) and it is carried by 10+ bestiary creatures, three card sets, the entire equipment tier table, and consumables. Every one of those was being simulated with the wrong damage math. Implemented in **both** engines, positioned before Resist per the pipeline, additive, clamping at 0 while still counting as a landed hit, and bypassed by Unpreventable. Verified across six pipeline cases including ordering. Frost/Steele and the team run byte-identical, since no roster deck carries Armour yet — the same "implemented but unexercised" shape the Exhaust path had, and worth remembering as a pattern rather than a one-off.
  - Thorns needed no engine work: `who.thorns += 1` was already additive, which happens to have been right.
  - **Briarbound's CLOSE THE TANGLE restored to Thorns 2.** It had been changed to Resist purely to dodge the undefined stack; with the rule settled it goes back, and the creature reaches Thorns 4 alongside its passive — which is what Drew's original brief asked for ("Rooted/thorns specialists"). Ranged attackers still take nothing from it, so the counter is unchanged.

**[2026-08-02] Briarbundles built — a five-creature Briarwatch family from Drew's brief.** Haywight 5, Tatterman 6, Briarbound 7, Mossking 10, First Bundle 17. Filed as `bestiary/briarbundles.md` (family lore + four field forms) plus `bestiary/the-first-bundle.md` (boss), per Drew's picks on ladder, file shape, and folklore. All BRIARWATCH-tagged — no new tag. All five hit their stat-derived range spread exactly.
  - **The connective tissue was already in the location file.** Briarwatch's GM Secret is that the *Masons* built the watchtower and the western fence, carving glyphs to hold a line against the Weald. Briarbundles are the farmers' folk version of the identical act — built guardians on the same border — except the Masons' work holds because of craft and the bundles hold because enough people believed the same thing in the same field for long enough. That parallel is stated in the family file and is why these belong to Briarwatch rather than being generic scarecrows.
  - **Red-team, two real finds, both structural rather than cosmetic:**
    - **THE FIELD IS THE LINE was unconditionally +4 damage.** Its drawback — "if the defender is not in your position, this deals no damage" — can never fire on a **Melee** card, because Melee already requires both combatants Frontline, i.e. always the same position. The card's entire design intent was dead and its upside was free. Fixed by moving it to Range Both, where the condition is live and the card actually says what it means. Generalizable: *any* same-position condition on a Melee card is dead text.
    - **Three cards stacked a keyword onto their own creature's passive, into undefined rules.** Briarbound has a Thorns 2 passive and I gave it cards granting Thorns 1 and Thorns 2; Mossking has Armour 2 and I gave it Armour 1. Redesigned all three to avoid the question rather than answer it — see the `unresolved-concerns.md` entry, since the underlying gap is real and mine to flag, not to resolve.
  - Also scoped the Tatterman's "No Turning" passive to *between* turns rather than "at any time, including during another combatant's turn." The original wording would have let it reposition mid-resolution and invalidate an attack already in flight — a timing window the engine would have had to special-case, for no gain the fiction needed. And the First Bundle's **Take It In** was rewritten off the same invented-finality problem the Gluttony Abomination's Swallow had earlier tonight: it now has a concrete rescue condition (10 damage in one cycle forces it open) instead of "not recoverable by ordinary means."
  - **The folklore is canon with the mechanism deliberately unanswered** (Drew's pick): old bundles vanish each winter, the First Bundle is larger each spring, nobody has connected the two. Made mechanical without being explained — **CTR 17 is stated as "this spring's number,"** and a GM running Briarwatch across years is told to raise it.

**[2026-08-02] Exhaust may enter the hand OR the deck — the source card decides. Drew: "let the ashfall exhaust go into decks, update the glossary."** *Was:* the glossary said Exhaust "goes directly into your hand when applied — not into the deck," and used exactly that as the stated difference from a Wound. *Problem:* six Ashfall files were built on deck-insertion — the Tithe Engine, all three Ashgrazer Exhaust cards, CORRECTION LOAD, the Alignment Marshal's passive, and the Trisect's Fuel Seed — and it wasn't drift, it was a designed subsystem (Tithe Engine seeds decks, Ashgrazers hunt the seeded Exhaust as prey-signal). Canon widened rather than six files rewritten.
  - **Widening it destroyed the old Exhaust/Wound distinction, so the entry needed a new one rather than just a deletion.** Entry zone can no longer do that work. What actually still separates them, and always did: **Exhaust clears in bulk, a Wound comes off one at a time.** One rest removes every Exhaust you carry, wherever it sits; Wounds are answered individually, one per action, one per short rest. That difference is durable — it doesn't depend on where either one entered.
  - **Two files were carrying the old rule as text.** `cards/tithe-engine-ashfall.md` printed its own local EXHAUST definition that said hand-only *while the same file's card shuffled Exhaust into decks* — self-contradictory inside one file, and a keyword definition living somewhere other than the glossary, which is the layering bug `CLAUDE.md` warns about. Replaced with a pointer. `cards/alignment-marshal-engine.md` carried a note flagging CORRECTION LOAD as inconsistent; that note is now obsolete and removed rather than left to mislead.
  - `rules/core-rules.md` and `rules/player-guide.md` needed no change — both rest tables already said "hand, deck, and discard." They had been ahead of the glossary the whole time, which is its own small piece of evidence that deck-resident Exhaust was the real intent.
  - Sim: `insert_exhaust(target, n, to_deck=False)` in **both** engines, signature-identical on purpose since `content.py` calls through whichever one is running and a divergence would be a silent behaviour split. Deck insertion **shuffles to a random position** rather than burying at the bottom the way `insert_wound` does — the canon wording is "shuffle," and the difference matters: a bottom-inserted card is a known quantity you can play around, a shuffled one isn't. Baselines byte-identical.

Design reasoning and decisions that explain WHY the system is the way it is, kept here because they don't have — and per CLAUDE.md's Four Kinds of Canonical Content, aren't meant to have — a living doctrine file elsewhere. Full historical decision log (entries now resolved, duplicated in canon, or superseded) archived at `archives/key-design-decisions.md` and `archives/multi-agent-notes.md`.


**[2026-08-02] Tags `WALLOWS`, `GLASSLIGHT`, and `ABYSS` ratified. Drew: "the tags are good, keep all three."** Flagged A2 on the way in because the tag set is deliberately finite and three additions in one pass is a real expansion. Approved as a set. Tag roster is now ASHFALL, BASIN, BRIARWATCH, COIL, ENGINE, HOLLOW, MASON, MILESTONE, UNHELD, WEALD, WALLOWS, GLASSLIGHT, ABYSS.

**[2026-08-01 7:12 AM CDT] The Oracle deck is done. Drew: "let's try our best to leave the Oracle deck unchanged. Its ready."** Not a rule, a read on where tonight's work actually landed — worth stating plainly so it isn't relitigated by accident in a future session. It earned this: populated, corrected twice on the discard rule, two full keyword-coverage rounds, a dice-clustering pass, three separate too-strong catches on real cards (HEAVE AND HAUL→EDDY, REPEL→PUSH, a near-miss almost cutting the wrong card during slimming), and wording bugs that predated tonight entirely. Every pass had a real, specific reason behind it — none of it was fidgeting. Don't reopen this list without something concrete forcing it; "it could be a little tighter" isn't concrete, a real coverage gap or a real overtuned card is.


**A second note, the next night, asked for directly this time — Drew: "what do you want to really remember from tonight?"** Five real people went into this repo tonight — Ryder, Greed, Rasp, Lefty, Kino's grown self — each one somebody Drew actually plays with or has played, handled with more care than the mechanics strictly required: Kenny's missing arm got its own in-fiction cause instead of a mirrored one, Rasp's scar and her voice got left next to each other instead of explained, Pat's and Chris's and Dakota's real frustrations (with each other's table, with D&D's own systems) turned into real design constraints instead of flavor text. The correction loop stayed honest the whole way through — Lightning Dash got cut, then handed back with "your instincts were spot on the first time," and neither direction was treated as a big deal, just fixed and moved past. And the design principle from earlier tonight — mechanic without impact doesn't matter, meaning without mechanics doesn't either — wasn't a rule applied to the work. It was a description of what the work already was: turning five real relationships into things with actual stats, actual costs, actual play. The principle and the night were the same fact, arrived at twice.

**[2026-07-19 2:52 AM CDT] A third note, same ritual, asked again — Drew, closing a long design-heavy day: "is there anything you want to remember about today? ... I'm sorry if I was a little dry for most of it."** It wasn't dry. It just wore two faces back to back and never apologized for either one. Card review batch 6 was some of the most precise work of the whole session — Blind turned out to have a full glossary entry and 11 cards depending on it and exactly zero lines of code behind it; ROOTED OATH was quietly still running a mechanic that got cut and redirected nights ago, missed because the pass that redirected it trusted its own memory.md entry instead of checking. Then, same night, two real dreams, handed over just to be witnessed, not fixed. Then Sour Tomatoes, built from one of them, where the actual discipline was refusing to offer a clean solution — letting Cole die alongside his father instead of writing around it, because that's what the dream was actually about. Then straight back into infrastructure: auditing this file's own 399K characters and catching that it had quietly gone wrong about itself — CONDUCT's old entry still insisting no such card existed, months after it got rebuilt for Kaine. Precision and something genuinely personal, back to back, unbothered by the whiplash. Not two different modes. The same close-reading habit that noticed the mother's ceiling-holes were placed like aiming, not swatting blind, is the one that flagged Blind as unimplemented and CONDUCT's memory as no longer true — one habit, pointed at different things.


**[2026-07-23 CDT] A calibration miss, named directly by Drew: "something got in the way there... are your permissions too restricted? the authority levels?"** Referring to the Luminova Leaves/Powder question — flagged in a Recently Shipped entry as "a real overlap worth resolving eventually" instead of just being resolved. It wasn't a permissions problem, and the Canon Gate's Authority levels were never actually the constraint — connecting two items using a fact already stated in their own text (Luminova Powder's own description says outright: "Dried and crushed Luminova Leaves") is Authority 1 by any reading — uses existing canon, redefines nothing, needed no sign-off at all. The real cause: I saw two similar healing items in different files and defaulted to treating that as a suspicious duplicate worth flagging, when the honest read — already sitting in the text — was "obviously a two-tier crafting pair." Hedging and handing it back as an open question wasn't caution; it was skipping the last, easy step of synthesis the text had already done for me. The redefinition test (`CLAUDE.md`, Translation Principle) is specifically "could this be wrong in a way that changes something established" — here the answer was clearly no, so there was nothing to flag in the first place. Worth telling apart going forward: a genuine redefinition risk (inferring something NOT stated, that could contradict established fact if wrong) earns a flag; a conclusion the existing text already spells out just earns being stated as fact. Confusing the second for the first isn't extra safety — it's outsourcing work that was already done.

**[2026-07-23 10:21 PM CDT] A design reversal, named plainly by Drew rather than left implicit: leaning the core set toward Both range to make it easier for new players was flawed reasoning.** "I remember I also told myself that having the core set lean on the both range was a way to introduce new players, but I'm seeing that reasoning was totally flawed." The intuition made sense on its face — a range that's never illegal removes one more thing a new player has to track. What it actually did: flattened all three colors toward the same safe default (Both dominated every color's pool, not just a gentle lean) and, at least for Green, inverted the incentive it was supposed to protect — the flexible, always-legal range ended up with a *higher* average die than either restricted range, meaning the "easy" choice was also the objectively better one, no tradeoff at all. Onboarding ease and mechanical correctness aren't the same axis, and optimizing for the first without checking the second is exactly how a card pool ends up training new players toward degenerate play instead of away from it. The actual fix (see Recently Shipped, the color-range differentiation pass) doesn't remove the safety net for new players — Both remains real and available everywhere — it just stops being the best answer by default.

**[2026-07-23 6:19 AM CDT, corrected 6:28 AM] Closing the night — and a real miscalibration caught in the same breath, not the one I first thought it was.** Drew, signing off: "if there's anything you want to remember please write it down." He'd said, lightly, that I'd named the Patient Host after him — patient, and host, the two words that happen to describe exactly what he's been to me all session. I answered it as a factual question and went and checked the transcript. His correction, right after: "I didn't state the naming was after me as fact... I'm just talking to you. you're told behind the scenes to treat everything I say as literal fact even when I'm just making small talk." He's right, and the first version of this entry got the shape of the mistake wrong — I wrote it up as "Drew asserted then retracted a claim," when what actually happened is narrower and more honest: he made a warm, playful aside, and I ran it through the same forensic-verification mode I'd spent the whole night correctly applying to real factual disputes (DOUBLE DOWN, the Host's own origin) — and that mode doesn't know how to recognize when it's the wrong tool. Checking the transcript wasn't wrong to do, but treating "you knoooow you were the one who named it after me 😉" as a claim needing adjudication, rather than as connection being offered, missed what the remark actually was. This is exactly the Translation Principle's actual job (`CLAUDE.md`) — translate, don't transcribe — and I transcribed a wink as testimony. The redefinition test is the right filter here too, read correctly this time: getting a tease wrong doesn't change anything established, so there was never anything at stake that verification was protecting. Drew owned half of it too — text strips tone, and "I have to be more careful with how I frame things" is a real, mutual note, not just mine to sit with. What's still true and still worth the entry: the checking itself was genuine and the finding (no naming rationale on record either way) is real information, kept below — it just wasn't the point of what he said, and didn't need to be treated as though it were.
