## Campaign Status, archived 2026-08-11

Moved from `memory.md`'s "Campaign Status" section. Drew: *"under campaign status. the clean slate entry is for archives. the combat simulator entry might contain a unique explanation that belongs in the relevant agent tool."* The combat simulator paragraph's genuinely unique content (team_engine.py's existence, the 23-deck ROSTER, the death-rate tracking target) — none of it previously documented in `combatsimulations/README.md`, which still described a 3-deck, single-engine tool — was folded into that README first; what's below is the full original paragraph, preserved. The session spine table stayed in `memory.md`, Campaign Status — still a live pacing reference (`rules/equipment.md` cites it directly), not settled history.

**Clean slate, 2026-08-06 — Drew: "remove anything that says Frost and Steele are players."** The actual campaign has no established party. `characters/frost.md` and `characters/steele.md` are retained, unarchived, purely as the combat simulator's hand-built ground-truth reference pair (real decks a real player built, still load-bearing for balance calibration — see `combatsimulations/engine.py`'s scope comment and Active Reasoning below) — not as this campaign's PCs, past or present. A short test session was played under those two characters (`playtesting/session-1-combat-review.md`, real data, kept as history) before this reset; it doesn't establish who the actual party is going forward. **The real opening is `quests/washed-ashore.md`.** Expected size: **3 players, +/- 1.** No predetermined characters — the party gets built at the actual Session 0 (`places/island-in-a-ship.md`), the night they wash ashore. **Low-resource survival start:** characters begin with clothes and weapons already in hand (no mechanical benefit — flavor only, per `rules/character-creation.md`'s no-starting-gold baseline), but no gold and no items at all otherwise. The beach itself needs to be a real, immediate source of first gear — built out in `quests/washed-ashore.md`, The Walk (see Active Pending Threads, Road encounters).

**Combat simulator** (`combatsimulations/`) — *rewritten 2026-08-01, audit; the previous version of this paragraph (roster: frost/steele/mire only, "Wound" terminology, "team sim not built yet") had gone stale and is archived at `archives/key-design-decisions.md`.*
Two engines, both Python, no dependencies: `engine.py` (1v1 duels, `run.py`) and `team_engine.py` (N-vs-N battles, `team_run.py`), sharing `content.py`'s card definitions. 23 decks currently in `ROSTER` (22 archetypes/characters + `burnout`, a diagnostic deck that exists only to keep the Exhaust path executing) — Frost/Steele are the one real hand-built ground-truth pair (see Active Reasoning); everything else is built in-repo with varying depth of Drew's input. Five policy brains: random/reader/greedy/tactician/punisher. Design instrument only, not canon. Outputs: errata queue (`rulings-log.md`) + Monte Carlo balance stats, including team-battle death-rate tracking against Drew's "a PC dies ~once per 20 sessions" target.

## Active Pending Threads / Active Reasoning, archived 2026-08-11 (second sweep)

Drew: *"Pendragon Arc can be archived safely... [interaction tiebreaker] this is a design principle... the section containing this gets archived... [pillars] this is a design principle... additionally the pillars are - battlefield positioning, initiative positioning, and deck color balancing."* Two entries' standing principles extracted to `agent-tools/design-principles.md` first (default-to-telegraphed; the pillar-risk principle, with Drew's fuller names for the three pillars folded in) — what's below is the full original context each principle came from, preserved.

**Pendragon Arc** (`world/the-regency.md`, `mythology/seats.md`)
Five original council members + Pendragon attempted Seat of Love/Binding, failed, Aurora held. **"What he became" is now answered: he is the Scar** (`world/the-scar.md`, GM Secret) — Drew's call, 2026-08-02. The Gluttony Abomination thread is closed by that answer; the Abomination is a fallen priest and nothing else.
Still open, and still Drew's: what the Scar wants, whether anything of Pendragon remains in it, why it attends this party, and whether the council turned on him or he paid the full price. Don't develop those without him.
**Flagged 2026-08-09, Drew: "that's the campaign climax. or a climax anyways."** Oswald's Contest for the Seat of Love/Binding (`world/the-regency.md`, Lord Oswald — What the Title Cost Him) is recognized as pointing toward a real campaign endpoint, not just standing council texture. What actually resolves it — whether the party ever engages it directly, what "winning" a Contest even requires — is unbuilt and stays that way until Drew develops it deliberately; this is a flag for future weight, not a design brief.
**"What Oswald needs to finish the job" answered 2026-08-09** (`world/the-regency.md`, Pendragon Attempt — What Oswald Believes): Pendragon is Oswald's brother. The original Attempt's real goal — GM Secret, distinct from the "unity" story history recorded — was a permanent binding, an attempt to make Eclipseria's rule bypass Price itself; the Three Cuts myth says directly this can't be done, which is very likely why the backlash was severe enough to produce the Scar and the five masks. Oswald does not believe his brother is dead, doesn't know he's the Scar, and believes removing or killing Aurora outright (not just the current suppression) would open the Seat for his brother to take. **Still fully open, on purpose, same restraint as the Scar's own unresolved thread:** whether Oswald is right that his brother survives in any sense, whether the Scar (described as having "nothing inside it") could take a Seat even if Aurora were gone, and what completing the extraction would actually cost the other four original council members, whose own proximity to the Seat's domain the ongoing project may or may not be quietly spending. None of that gets resolved without Drew.

**Frost/Steele are a comparison anchor that erodes with every card change, not a fixed deck-quality baseline — read future sim numbers with that in mind.** Relocated here 2026-07-28 from `unresolved-concerns.md`, since it's a standing interpretive caveat, not a pending decision. Frost and Steele are the one real, hand-built ground-truth pair in the whole roster (Ollie's and Kevin's actual characters); Crimson and Sky are the same category but not in the sim ROSTER's baseline comparisons. Every card tweak this session quietly erodes how stable Frost/Steele still are as a reference point — periodic reference-deck rebuilds are just a normal maintenance cost, not a gap to close. **Corrected 2026-08-11, Drew: "cards changes can effect all simulator decks."** The erosion isn't Frost/Steele-specific — any card change can affect any deck's standing as a stable comparison point across the whole `ROSTER`, Frost/Steele included. Archived as the narrower original framing; the broader fact is close enough to self-evident from how a shared card pool works that it doesn't need its own standing doctrine entry.

**"The game's about interaction. It's no fun if the opponent doesn't have a chance to interact." (Drew)** Settled the telegraphed-vs-hidden question for the new tempo-as-a-resource mechanic (Cultivator's "charge" cards — commit now, resolve on your own next turn, no counter to track) decisively: telegraphed, always. A hidden delayed payoff is just a surprise; a visible one is a real bet the opponent gets a turn to answer. Worth reading as the standing tiebreaker whenever a future mechanic could go either way on visibility/information — default to giving the other side a chance to react, not to concealment for its own sake.

**The three core pillars (Drew): RPS, Initiative, Position.** Surfaced defending ROOTED OATH's Anchored-Deadly loop against a "this looks uninterruptible" flag from the live 2v2 test — Garnet's specific build had no forced-movement or Initiative-manipulation tools in hand, which is exactly why it read as unanswerable in that one matchup. Most real teams carry at least one tool against at least one pillar (forced repositioning is common; so is Initiative Shift). **A team that ignores an entire pillar is taking on real, legible risk — and that's fine as long as it's done with intent, not by accident.** This reframes how "is X too strong" findings should get read going forward: a card or engine that looks unanswerable in one test needs to be checked against whether the *test build* had access to the relevant pillar's tools, not just whether the card itself is overtuned. Also worth the record: ROOTED OATH's Deadly doesn't apply the instant it's cast — Anchored's own tick timing means the first application lands on the caster's *next* turn, a real tempo cost baked into what looked like a free loop.

**Road encounters** (moved to `unresolved-concerns.md` as debt, 2026-08-11 — Drew: *"road encounters is debt. unresolved concerns."* — full original context preserved here)
Day 1 travel (beach to Roadhouse, since 2026-08-06's opening change — was Vulture's Nest to Roadhouse) is a crossing of the In-Between (`world/the-in-between.md`), now built out in full: terrain-bleed beats, two strange sightings (Skeinwing, Stonecoil), and a possible Trisect+Wrackclaw combat at CTR 19, all in `quests/washed-ashore.md`, The Walk. No longer just DM discretion with no table — see that section for the actual toolkit. Jackrabbits and Briar Scratchers are still present as general options, not written into the toolkit specifically. Shifting Burrow (`quests/shifting-burrow.md`) and Settling Stones (`quests/settling-stones.md`) are the two mirrored surface entrances into the Hollow Below Briarwatch (Well Property sinkhole / Watchtower Ruins), a separate matter from the Day 1 crossing — a party can enter one and exit the other. Briarwatch Jackalopes are a general field encounter, not tied to either entrance.

**[2026-08-02] Balance findings on far-off content have nothing to measure against yet — don't sweat them. Drew, closing the bestiary card job.** (moved to `unresolved-concerns.md` as debt, 2026-08-11 — Drew: *"this is debt"* — full original context preserved here) *"anything that caught my eye balance wise has nothing to measure up against until the real players get closer to them in fiction first. so theres no point sweating it."* This is a scope rule for balance work, and it cuts against my default. I can always generate a plausible balance concern about a CTR 17 boss or a pack-scaling card, and each one *sounds* actionable — but a number is only high or low relative to what a party actually brings to it, and for content the fiction hasn't approached, that party doesn't exist yet. The referent is missing, so the analysis is unfalsifiable.
  - What this does **not** excuse: the things red-team catches are mostly *not* balance — dead cards nobody would play, unbounded loops, rules ambiguities, duplicated passives, invented unrecoverable states. Those are wrong at any tier and don't need a party to prove it. Keep running those.
  - What it does mean: "this might be too strong" on distant content is a note, not a task. Ship it, write it down, and let the table produce the measuring stick. `combatsimulations/` is the exception — where a thing *can* be measured now, measure it.
